## M0131 — Bidirectional cluster-directory cold-start + real-PG system-view hosting (filed 2026-08-11)

> **⚠ TOP-PRIORITY MILESTONE (user directive 2026-08-11), immediately after
> M-NIGHTLY and AHEAD OF M0130.** This section sits at the end of the file for
> append-safety only — **document order does NOT reflect priority.** The
> `## Current Priority` banner at the top of this file is the sole ordering
> authority and names M0131 first. Work M0131 tasks before any remaining M0130,
> M0119 or M0122 item.

**Milestone doc:** `docs/milestones/0131-bidirectional-cluster-dir-coldstart-and-system-views.md`
**Implementation plan:** `docs/design/0131-bidirectional-cluster-dir-coldstart-and-system-views.md`
**Source:** M0130 Acceptance-bar item 1 (never discharged — every M0130 acceptance item was proven through the `pg_basebackup` lane, not a cold start); `docs/design/0130-0002-pg-class-heap-persistence.md` §"Remaining for full reverse-path parity" items 1-3 (no ledger rows, contrary to the filing rule); deferral-ledger rows #428, #490, #995, #996.

**Filing rule (inherited from M0130):** no task deferred without a ledger-recorded strong reason; subtasks inline in fix_plan; every non-trivial subsystem lands its design doc (draft → accepted) within M0131.

**Citation precedence:** the ten `0131-000N-*.md` sub-design docs re-verified every citation used here against the repo and the oracle. **Where a sub-doc and this section disagree, the sub-doc wins.** Corrections already folded in below: S1's blast radius is six unregistered GUC names, not four; S2 needs a new `ControlFileData` field decode before it can read pg_control; S4's "three deltas" reduce to one; `tupdesc.c:105` is an `elog(ERROR)` not a FATAL; `pg_stat_activity` and `pg_settings` swap S9 buckets. Ledger references written `#NNN` are LINE NUMBERS in `.ralph/deferral_ledger.md` — that file has no ID column.

**Three corrections this milestone carries (diagnosed at filing 2026-08-11):**
1. Ledger rows #428/#995/#996 blame *"a goopg-built `pg_internal.init` leaves `rd_rules` empty"* and prescribe populating it. **That fix is not expressible.** Upstream `load_relcache_init_file` (`postgres/src/backend/utils/cache/relcache.c:6443-6453`) sets `rd_rules = NULL` unconditionally ("Rules and triggers are not saved"); `write_relcache_init_file` never serialises them; views never pass `RelationIdIsInInitFile`; and `StartupXLOG` deletes every init file at `postgres/src/backend/access/transam/xlog.c:5633` before any backend reads one. The real causes are S5 and S6 below.
2. Those rows name index **2620**. 2620 is `pg_trigger`. The index `RelationBuildRuleLock` scans is **2693** (`pg_rewrite_rel_rulename_index`, `postgres/src/include/catalog/pg_rewrite.h:57`).
3. `copyInitFiles` (`internal/testport/e2e_failover_goopg_to_pg_test.go:808-844`, 3 call sites) is inert — its own adding commit `30b0716f` (2026-05-17, subject ends "add copyInitFiles workaround") admits *"PG's load_relcache_init_file still rejects the file silently"*, and it was superseded next day by `c09d519e` ("step 3cq proper"). S10 deletes it.

Theme A — Reverse cold start (goopg on a PG-initdb'd directory):
- [x] **M0131-S1 — GUC registry accepts a PG-18-initdb `postgresql.conf`** (DONE 2026-08-11; **10** GUCs registered, not 8 — S1.4's `password_encryption`/`log_file_mode` confirmed and included. `internal/config/defaults.go` + `postgresql.conf.sample` + new `internal/config/pg_initdb_conf_test.go`. Guard 3 proven end-to-end: `goopg init --lc-messages=C -T english -g` then `goopg start` now starts, `SHOW` returns the conf values. - [x] **M0131-S2 — `LoadOrCreateSystemID` reads pg_control first** (DONE 2026-08-11; `internal/control/pgcontrol.go` gained a DECODE-ONLY `SystemIdentifier` from `buf[0:8]`, `internal/initdb/initdb.go` `LoadOrCreateSystemID` restructured to pg_control → flat file → random with the flat file corrected from the winner and a WARNING on disagreement; S2.1/S2.2/S2.3 all landed. New `internal/initdb/systemid_pgcontrol_test.go` carries the design's 5 guards, incl. an end-to-end check that a `goopg init` directory agrees in all three places the identity is recorded — flat file, pg_control offset 0, and `xlp_sysid` at bytes 24:32 of the bootstrap segment. The `Init` ordering assumption was re-verified, not assumed: `:1250` still precedes `writePgControl` at `:1264`. 2 ledger rows — no repair path for a directory goopg already stamped with an invented ID, and the encoder still cannot write `system_identifier`.) (est ~1 loop). On a dir with no goopg-private `global/system_identifier`, `internal/initdb/initdb.go:54-76` **invents a fresh random ID**, diverging from pg_control — silent state corruption on any PG-authored directory. Its sibling `LoadOrCreateTimelineID` (`internal/initdb/timeline.go:44-68`) already does this correctly (M0130-S8.1); generalise the lesson. **Prerequisite:** `internal/control`'s `ControlFileData` has NO `system_identifier` field — the struct begins at `State` (offset 16, `pgcontrol.go:44-46`) — so S2 must first add a decode of `buf[0:8]`; decode-only is recommended since `UpdateControlFile` (`pgcontrol.go:205`) preserves untouched bytes. Blast radius if left unfixed: `SystemID` feeds `xlp_sysid` in every long WAL page header, which upstream's reader rejects on mismatch (`postgres/src/backend/access/transam/xlogreader.c:1282-1286`), plus `IDENTIFY_SYSTEM` and BASE_BACKUP. Subtasks: S2.1 read pg_control when the flat file is absent, then write the flat file from it; S2.2 keep random generation only for a genuinely fresh `goopg init`; S2.3 define and log the disagree case (pg_control wins is PG-faithful). Theme B — Forward cold start (real PG on a goopg-created directory):

Guard 7 held **positively**: an index-qualified read under `enable_seqscan = off` returns the right row through a goopg-authored btree, so blocker #12 did NOT resurface. The design's three predicted deltas all held inert (`pg_internal.init`, file modes, `postmaster.pid`/`.opts` absent after the clean stop). **S4.5 verdict: the non-atomic non-HOT UPDATE gap did NOT surface** — `sum(qty)` matches goopg exactly across the UPDATE and DELETE and the UPDATEd row id=9 reads correctly; ledger M0118-0129 stays open but this lane is not where it shows. **Four gaps measured, each locked into the test in the FAIL-WHEN-FIXED direction** (the `0131-0003`→S11 discipline) and filed below as S12/S13/S14/S15 with a ledger row each: F1 no sort works for any type (empty `pg_opclass_am_name_nsp_index` 2686); F2 calling any LANGUAGE SQL builtin aborts the backend (all 3397 `pg_proc` rows carry a malformed non-NULL `proconfig`); F3 `ADD COLUMN … DEFAULT` reads NULL on pre-existing rows (no fast-default; `attmissingval` is not even a column in goopg's `pg_attribute` self-description) — though the pg_attrdef half is asserted POSITIVELY and correct; F4 a goopg-`CREATE DATABASE`-minted database PANICs `could not open critical system index 2662`. Harness: `pgcluster.Stop` was unbounded and hung a whole `go test` for its 20-minute timeout when the postmaster sat in crash recovery — now 20 s then SIGKILL; new `pgcluster.PSQLCombined` returns output without `t.Fatalf` so error text can be classified. Gates: the new test PASS; the whole `^TestE2E_` family PASS; UNITS PASS; pgbench smoke PASS via the hook. Design `0131-0004` draft → accepted with a Findings section; README row given its summary.) (est ~2 loops). New `TestE2E_PGColdStartOnGoopgDataDir`. **The harness needs nothing new** — `pgcluster.OpenExisting` (`internal/testutil/pgcluster/cluster.go:117-125`) takes any directory and `Start` execs `postgres -D -p -h` directly (`:236-289`), so goopg's all-commented conf needs no edit. The work is the test plus what it uncovers. **The delta vs. the proven basebackup lane reduces to ONE thing** (corrected while writing `0131-0004`): `pg_internal.init` present rather than tar-excluded (`internal/server/basebackup.go:113`). File modes are NOT a delta — `writeTarFileWithMode` copies `info.Mode().Perm()` (`basebackup.go:723`, `:728`, `:933`) and forces 0600 only for pg_control and WAL segments; what matters is `checkDataDir`'s 0700/0750 + ownership check (`postgres/src/backend/utils/init/miscinit.c:384-409`), which goopg's 0700 init (`internal/initdb/initdb.go:792`) satisfies. `postmaster.opts`/`postmaster.pid` are NOT a delta after a clean stop — goopg has no writer for `postmaster.opts` at all, and `postmaster.pid` is removed by `stopControlPlane` → `control.RemovePIDFile` (`internal/server/server.go:808-815`); assert their absence rather than caveating it. **Also:** `pg_class.relhasindex` is no longer hardcoded false (`pgClassRelhasindex`, `internal/executor/pg18_user_catalog_rows.go:591-635`, with `pgIndexTupleKeys = true` at `internal/executor/pgindex_btree.go:54`), so a hosted PG may genuinely plan an index scan over a goopg index — put an index-qualified predicate in scope and treat `XX002` as a REGRESSION signal, not an expected wall. Subtasks: S4.1 goopg workload per M0130 acceptance item 1 (CREATE TABLE / ADD COLUMN / CREATE SCHEMA / CREATE INDEX / CREATE DATABASE / INSERT / UPDATE / DELETE) then `Stop(cluster.ShutdownFast)`; S4.2 `OpenExisting` + `Start` + psql reads, asserting `SELECT relname FROM pg_class` lists the user tables — this discharges `0130-0002` Guard #1, which has read *"Needs E2E PG-attach test — not yet implemented"* since it was written; S4.3 assert zero FATAL in the PG log; S4.4 **no view assertions until S6 lands** (a hosted PG cannot evaluate any view today), phrase over user tables and SRFs, then add the view assertion after S6; S4.5 non-atomic non-HOT UPDATE is a known open gap (ledger M0118-0129, restated in M0130-S7.2) and the workload includes UPDATE — if it surfaces, diagnose and ledger, do not silently avoid. Design: `docs/design/0131-0004-forward-coldstart-e2e.md`.

Earlier slices S7.1/S7.2/S7.3/S7.5/S7.6 landed the same day: `scripts/capture-ev-action.sh` initdbs a throwaway PG 18.3, runs the three queries per view, and emits the `.dat` blobs plus `internal/initdb/nailed_view_manifest.tsv` (6 rel rows + 87 attr rows). **The acceptance oracle is green: `--verify` re-derives all six committed blobs BYTE-IDENTICALLY** (design guard #1), across three independent `initdb` runs (guard #6 idempotence — and with it, further evidence the `12xxx` assignments are deterministic). Guard #3's `:relid 12261` landmine never fired: S8a's repin had already disarmed it, so no mapping row was needed and no rewriting pass was grown (Option A identity mapping is *asserted*, not applied — an oracle OID that stops matching its pin is a hard error telling you to re-pin deliberately). Guards #4 (unpinned in-band `:relid`) and #5 (unknown `atttypid`) are enforced at capture time; the pinned table and the pg_type bootstrap set are parsed out of the Go tree at run time so the script cannot drift from the tree's own policy. **Guard #2 (the new offline `internal/initdb/nailed_view_manifest_test.go`, manifest ↔ `nailedRel`/`nailedAttr`) found a real bug on its first run:** `pg_stat_replication_slots.slot_name` was `name`(19, len 64) where PG says `text`(25, len −1) — the view selects `s.slot_name`, the OUT param of `pg_stat_get_replication_slot` (`pg_proc.dat:5676-5680`, `proallargtypes` starts `{text,text,…}`), NOT `r.slot_name` from the base view, which really is `name`(19) (`system_views.sql:1045-1059`). Both the table and the pin test that should have caught it (`TestPgStatReplicationSlotsViewAttrs`) carried the same wrong value — transcribed by the same misreading — so only an oracle could break the tie; both fixed. It was latent because the view returns zero rows with no slots, which is all S6's evaluability assertion exercises; with a slot present a hosted PG would deform a `text` datum as `name`/64 (`tupdesc.c:105`). Two design deviations, both forced by the oracle: PG 18's `initdb` has no `-q` (the `pg-oracle-diff.sh:134` citation is wrong), and queries use psql `-F $'\t'` because `text || "char"` is ambiguous for `relkind`. Gates: `internal/initdb` PASS (61 s); whole `^TestE2E_` family PASS (100 s); `--verify` PASS. 2 ledger rows (S7.4 not built; goopg's runtime virtual replication views declare every column `text`). Design `0131-0007` draft → in progress with Implementation-status + Findings sections; README row updated. **Next slice = S7.4 only.**) (est ~3 loops; re-sized after review — shell capture tool + a new `cmd/gen-nailed-view-tables` generator + a manifest format + byte-identical reproduction of six blobs through `psql -A -t` + attribute-table re-derivation). **Runs AFTER S8a** (LANDED 2026-08-11) — it consumes S8a's pinned table (`internal/initdb/system_view_oid_pins.go`, `systemViewOIDPins()`) and must refuse to emit for an in-band relid with no pin. Under Option A the mapping is the IDENTITY function, so S7.6's acceptance test is a plain `cmp` against upstream's own bytes and S7 must NOT grow a rewriting pass; S7.3's manifest supersedes the hand-captured table, and the guard test then checks table-vs-manifest. Subtasks: S7.1 `scripts/capture-ev-action.sh` on the `scripts/pg-oracle-diff.sh` pattern (throwaway `initdb --no-sync -q`, `PG_BIN`/`PG_LIB` from `postgres/local_install/`, `tmp/` scratch, EXIT trap); S7.2 three psql queries per view — `pg_rewrite.ev_action`, `pg_attribute` (6 cols mapping 1:1 onto `nailedAttr`), `pg_class` (feeding `nailedRel`); S7.3 emit `.dat` + a TSV manifest (`internal/initdb/nailed_view_manifest.tsv`); S7.4 `cmd/gen-nailed-view-tables/main.go` (`//go:build ignore`, "Code generated … DO NOT EDIT" header) renders the Go tables; S7.5 hard-error on an `atttypid` absent from the pg_type bootstrap, since `attalign`/`attbyval` are derived from it; S7.6 the acceptance oracle — re-derive the committed six **byte-identically** via `cmp`. A `scripts/` generator that runs a throwaway real PG 18.3 initdb, queries `pg_rewrite` joined to `pg_class`/`pg_attribute` for a named view set, and emits the `.dat` blobs + `nailedAttr` tables + `nailedRel` entries, applying an OID-mapping table to relids inside the blobs. Makes S9 mechanical rather than artisanal. Its own oracle: re-derive the existing six **byte-identically**. Design: `docs/design/0131-0007-ev-action-capture-tooling.md`.
- [x] **M0131-S8a — System-view OID policy DECISION + pinned OID table** (DONE 2026-08-11; **Option A — PIN — chosen and implemented.** goopg's six nailed system views and their `_RETURN` rule OIDs now carry PG 18.3's own initdb assignments: `pg_stat_wal_receiver` 12100→12240, `pg_stat_replication` 12102→12231, `pg_stat_recovery_prefetch` 12103→12244, `pg_stat_subscription` 12104→12248, `pg_replication_slots` 12105→**12261**, `pg_stat_replication_slots` 12106→12266; rules 12101/12107..12111 → 12234/12243/12247/12251/12264/12269. Captured from two independent throwaway `initdb --no-sync` runs of PG 18.3 that produced **byte-identical** assignments, so the determinism the policy rests on is verified, not assumed. New `internal/initdb/system_view_oid_pins.go` (the pinned table + oracle version stamp + the reserved goopg-only sub-band) and `system_view_oid_pins_test.go` (6 guards = the design's Guards 1-6, each with a non-vacuity assertion). **S6.1 is DISCHARGED, not deferred — strike it:** the landmine blob needs no patch at all, because pinning `pg_replication_slots` to 12261 makes the verbatim `pg_stat_replication_slots_ev_action.dat` `:relid 12261` correct as-captured; S6.2's invariant guard landed here instead (proven non-vacuous — temporarily un-pinning to 12105 makes it fail on exactly those two occurrences). **S6.5's probe is ANSWERED:** upstream mints per-view composite `reltype`s (12233/12242/12246/12250/12263/12268) where goopg carries 2249 RECORDOID; the values are captured in the pinned table and a guard asserts RECORDOID is still the only divergence, so what remains under S6.5 is the implementation (needs real `pg_type` rows) — ledgered. Two risks checked and clear: the repin breaks the old coincidence that declaration order equalled ascending-OID order, but **all 20+ `bootstrap*Index` functions sort explicitly** so no on-disk btree leaf can be mis-ordered; and `catalog_heap_reload.go`'s `evClass < FirstUserOID` filter still skips all six (12231..12266 < 16384) — comment-only update. Band ceiling measured: upstream initdb tops out at 13626 across every OID-bearing catalog, fixing `goopgOnlySystemViewOIDBase = 15000`. **Acceptance taken against a LIVE PG**, not just a table: `assertSystemViewOIDsArePinnedToUpstream` in the S4 cold-start E2E has a real PG 18.3 hosted on a goopg catalog resolve all six `pg_catalog.<view>::regclass::oid` to upstream's own values (`::regclass` not `SELECT *` — evaluation still needs S6's `relhasrules` flip). Gates: `internal/initdb` + `internal/estimateaudit` PASS; `TestE2E_PGColdStartOnGoopgDataDir` PASS; the whole `^TestE2E_` family PASS (99 s); UNITS PASS; pgbench smoke PASS via the hook. Design `0131-0008` draft → accepted + a 6-part Findings section; README row. 1 ledger row (the no-in-place-upgrade scope limit, now sharper than S6's: a stale `$PGDATA` disagrees with the committed ev_action corpus, not just with a flag).) (est ~1 loop; **must precede S6 AND S7**). Pin goopg's system-view OIDs to upstream PG 18.3's initdb-assigned `12xxx` values, or commit to an OID-rewriting pass over captured blobs. Forcing function: **any view built on another view carries that view's OID inside its `ev_action`**, and `system_views.sql` is full of view-on-view chains. Little code — a policy, a table, a guard test — but expensive to reverse, so it must land **before** S9 or the captured corpus needs redoing. Design: `docs/design/0131-0008-system-view-oid-policy.md`.
- [ ] **M0131-S9 — Widen the on-disk system-view corpus** (est ~many loops, LARGE). goopg's ~118 system/information_schema relations are **virtual** (`catalog.Table{Virtual:true, VirtualRows:…}`, `internal/catalog/catalog.go:335-342`; skipped from the pg_class heap at `:7025-7034`), so a hosted PG asking for them gets **42P01**, not 42809 — a different failure from the six-view island. **Do NOT generate `ev_action` from `internal/pgnodes`**: it supports 21 node tags and hard-rejects `rtekind != 0` (`readfuncs_query.go:324-325`), while `pg_stat_replication`'s real ev_action carries RTE_FUNCTION ×2 and RTE_JOIN ×2. Capture is the mechanism. Sub-slices: S9.1 SRF-only views (`pg_stat_get_*` family **plus `pg_settings`**, which is `FROM pg_show_all_settings()` at `system_views.sql:604`) — precondition largely already discharged: `internal/initdb/pg_proc_seed_data.go` holds 3397 entries, exactly upstream `pg_proc.dat`'s count, and all sampled S9.1 SRFs are present with `AllArgTypes` populated; S9.2 views over real catalogs (`pg_tables`, `pg_views`, `pg_indexes`, `pg_roles`, **and `pg_stat_activity`** — corrected: `system_views.sql:878` joins `pg_stat_get_activity(NULL)` against `pg_authid` AND `pg_database`, so it is not SRF-only; note `pg_views` does not exist in goopg in any form); S9.3 view-on-view chains (where S8's policy pays off); S9.4 `information_schema` (65 views) — **expected to be deferred with a ledger row** (verified well-founded: only 3 namespaces are bootstrapped on disk, `internal/initdb/initdb.go:2328-2334`, with NO `information_schema`, and zero occurrences of `sql_identifier`/`cardinal_number`/`character_data`/`yes_or_no` in non-test Go); needs the namespace, its domains (`sql_identifier`, `cardinal_number`, …) and helper functions on disk first. Known ceilings, all to be ledgered not assumed away: the ~8160 B max-inline heap tuple (largest existing blob is 27670 B raw → 4235 B as a pglz varlena, comfortably inline; `pg_rewrite` TOAST 2838/2839 becomes required the moment a capture overflows — a separate unscoped slice); `pg_proc` signature drift (a blob pins `funcid`/`funcresulttype`/`funccoltypes`); and the dual-definition hazard — worse than "types": virtual `pg_stat_replication` (`internal/initdb/replication_views.go:34`) has **24** columns all typed `text` (20 upstream + `slot_name` + 3 goopg ring counters) while the on-disk nailed rel (`relcache_init.go:693`) has **20** with PG-faithful types, so COUNT and TYPE both diverge; proven for one view, not stress-tested at scale. **Two unmeasured `ev_action` shapes** (found while writing `0131-0009`): none of the six captured blobs contains `RTE_RESULT` or `LATERAL` — `pg_stat_bgwriter`/`pg_stat_checkpointer` have no `FROM` at all (RTE_RESULT) and `pg_statio_all_tables`/`pg_stats_ext` use `LATERAL`; neither is known to round-trip. **S8 is resolvable in favour of pinning:** `catalog.FirstUserOID = 16384` (`internal/catalog/catalog.go:3604`) floors every dynamically minted OID and nothing allocates into 12000..16383 (100% hand-written constants, 42 refs across 6 files), so the "collides with goopg's allocator" objection is void; measured corpus is 80 views with 14 view-on-view edges. Design: `docs/design/0131-0009-system-view-corpus-widening.md`.
      **S9.0 LANDED 2026-08-11 (the tooling precondition, not a widening):** two
      of the three hand-edits S7.4 ledgered are closed, so adding a view is now
      capture + regenerate. `cmd/gen-nailed-view-tables` renders the `pg_rewrite`
      `_RETURN` seed rows from the manifest's `rule_oid` column
      (`nailedViewRewriteEntries()`; `replicationViewRewriteEntries()` deleted),
      and the six per-view `//go:embed` lines become one `*_ev_action.dat` glob
      into an `embed.FS` resolved by view name
      (`internal/initdb/nailed_view_ev_action.go`). The view-OID `const`s stay
      hand-written on purpose — they are now test-only, i.e. the independent pin
      the generated table is compared against. New guards:
      `TestNailedViewEvActionBlobSetMatchesSeededViews` (the glob, unlike six
      embed lines, does NOT fail the build on a missing `.dat`),
      `TestNailedViewRewriteEntriesCoverEverySeededView`,
      `TestNailedViewEvActionRejectsUnknownView`. Two order-assuming tests fixed
      to locate rows by OID/name (generated rows follow capture order; heap row
      order is not a catalog invariant). Gates: `internal/initdb` PASS (61 s),
      `^TestE2E_` PASS (105 s), UNITS PASS, pgbench smoke via the hook. One
      ledger row.

      **S9.1 LANDED 2026-08-11 — the on-disk corpus is 28 views, up from 6, and

      Guard #5 implemented as the design asks (capture time): the script fails
      any view whose `pg_column_size(ev_action)` exceeds 8000 B
      (`MaxHeapTupleSize` 8160 minus 160 B of header + the other seven columns)
      and names `DECLARE_TOAST(pg_rewrite, 2838, 2839)` as the prerequisite

      `pg_catalog` view's stored `ev_action` size AND its in-band `:relid`
      set, which both defines the catalog-direct population S9.2 owns and
      fixes S9.3's remaining work as a list of twelve dependents over six
      `pg_stat_all_*`/`pg_statio_all_*` bases (12146, 12151, 12174, 12187,
      12200, 12213) — five of those bases are under the inline ceiling, and
      `pg_statio_all_tables` (10475 B) is not, so its two dependents sit
      behind the TOAST work. `pg_stat_database` is the corpus's FIRST blob
      carrying a set operation (`RTE_SUBQUERY` over a `SetOperationStmt`,
      `system_views.sql:1006-1010`); of this doc's two "unmeasured ev_action
      shapes" only `LATERAL` is now unmeasured. Two type rows became
      canonical for columns the tranche actually seeds: `_name` (1003,
      `pg_policies.roles`; typalign `'i'` not name's `'c'` per
      `Catalog.pm:469`) and `regtype` (2206, `pg_sequences.data_type`).

      a goopg cluster at all; the first blob blocked by a missing base
      CATALOG rather than by content inside one. F13/ceiling #5:
      `pg_publication_tables` (12068, 3793 B) fails with `target type is not

      `TestE2E_PGColdStartOnGoopgDataDir` PASS, `^TestE2E_` family PASS

      §"Implementation status — S9.2d" + F12/F13. 2 ledger rows.
      **S9.3a LANDED 2026-08-12 — S9.3 opens and the corpus is 49 views with

      `pg_statio_all_tables` (12174, 10475 B) being over budget withholds

      that triple. No new ceiling KIND; the five-for-five
      "every ceiling is an initdb-bootstrap gap" generalisation holds.
      Gates: `internal/initdb` PASS (96 s),
      `TestE2E_PGColdStartOnGoopgDataDir` PASS, `^TestE2E_` family PASS

      §"Implementation status — S9.3a" + F14. 2 ledger rows.
      **S9.3b LANDED 2026-08-12 — S9.3's reachable population is FINISHED;
      the corpus is 58 views over TEN view-on-view edges and five bases.**
      The per-index and per-sequence statistics families, pinned and seeded

      from four edges to ten with no tooling change. **F15:** F14's
      dependent/base size ratio is STRUCTURAL, not a quirk of the table

      with no counterexample. The tightest margin yet measured is

      which makes the `pg_rewrite` TOAST (2838/2839) slice a NEAR-TERM
      prerequisite for further base captures rather than a distant one.

      **S9.3c LANDED 2026-08-12 — the corpus is 60 views, and the slice
      added NO capture work: it cleared a bootstrap ceiling and the two

      ('public','s4app')`) with `op ANY/ALL (array) requires array on right
      side`. With `typarray = 0` the parser cannot find an array type at all
      and `transformAExprIn` falls back to an OR-chain that always works; the
      moment `typarray` resolves it upgrades to a `ScalarArrayOpExpr` whose
      `IsTrueArrayType` check needs `typsubscript == array_subscript_handler`
      TOO. Populate a PG catalog column group atomically, never per-symptom.
      **F17:** the repair could not be scoped to the 45 `pgTypeCanonical`

      **S9.3d LANDED 2026-08-12 — the corpus is 71 of upstream's 80
      `pg_catalog` views and the residual work is a CLOSED list of nine.**
      This slice was selected by enumerating the COMPLEMENT (ask the oracle
      for every `pg_catalog` view with a `_RETURN` rule — oid, rule oid,
      reltype, relnatts, stored `ev_action` size, in-band `:relid` set — and

      `cmd/gen-nailed-view-tables` run; the other 60 blobs came back

      §"Implementation status — S9.3d" + F18/F19. 2 ledger rows.
      **S20.1 LANDED 2026-08-12 — ceiling #1's relation pair exists on disk;
      the eight views behind it are now blocked only by the chunk writer.**
      `DECLARE_TOAST(pg_rewrite, 2838, 2839)` (pg_rewrite.h:54) was the named
      blocker for eight of the nine remaining views, and goopg bootstrapped
      NEITHER relation — an out-of-line `varatt_external` pointer would have
      named a relation that does not exist. This slice lands the pair itself,
      every field measured on a throwaway PG 18.3 oracle rather than inferred:
      pg_class 2838 (relnamespace **99**, reltype **0**, relam 2, relkind 't',
      relnatts 3, relhasindex t) + 2839 (relam 403, relkind 'i', relnatts 2),
      their pg_attribute rows (`attstorage='p'` — a chunk is never itself
      toasted), the pg_index row (indkey "1 2", oid_ops + int4_ops, UNIQUE
      PRIMARY), `pg_class(2618).reltoastrelid = 2838`, and the two physical
      files in base/{1,5}. New `internal/initdb/pg_rewrite_toast_bootstrap.go`
      holds the declaration as DATA (`nailedToastPairs`), and the pair is
      deliberately NOT a member of `nailedLocalRels`: that list also drives
      `bootstrapPgTypeTuples` (a TOAST relation has no pg_type row — a
      defaulted reltype=OID would trip PG's `tdtypeid` assertion) and
      `writeRelcacheInitFile` (PG never opens a TOAST relation in the critical
      relcache phase). `bootstrapPgClassRelnameNspIndex` learned the namespace
      it had hardcoded to 11. **Acceptance: a hosted real PG cold-started on a
      goopg $PGDATA resolves `pg_toast.pg_toast_2618` BY NAME and scans it**
      (`assertHostedPGSeesPgRewriteToastRelation`); non-vacuity verified by
      emptying `nailedToastPairs()` — all three reads fail, the last with
      `relation "pg_toast.pg_toast_2618" does not exist`. Second measurement
      worth carrying: **79 of the oracle's ~160 pg_rewrite rows are toasted**,
      including views goopg already hosts INLINE (`pg_roles`, `pg_tables`,
      `pg_stat_activity`, …) — inline vs external is not a divergence PG can
      observe, so only the eight oversize captures are forced out of line.
      Gates: `internal/initdb` PASS (113 s), `^TestE2E_` family PASS (96 s),
      `TestE2E_PGColdStartOnGoopgDataDir` PASS + the deliberate fail-when-broken
      run, UNITS PASS, `go build ./...` + `go vet` clean, pgbench smoke via the
      hook. Design `0131-0035-pg-rewrite-toast.md`. 2 ledger rows.
      **S20.2a LANDED 2026-08-12 — the writer exists; only the captures stand
      between the corpus and 77 of 80 views.** New
      `internal/initdb/pg_rewrite_toast_writer.go`: an oversize seeded
      `ev_action` becomes an 18-byte VARTAG_ONDISK `varatt_external` plus
      1996-byte chunks, written multi-page into base/{1,5}/2838 and indexed by
      a (chunk_id oid_ops, chunk_seq int4_ops) bulk-load into 2839
      (`pgBuildIndexTupleOidInt4Key` + `bootstrapToastChunkIndex`);
      `bootstrapToastRelationFiles` takes the chunks and
      `writeMultiPageHeapRowsExternal` stamps HEAP_HASEXTERNAL on the owning
      pg_rewrite tuple. **A second oracle pass carried three facts the headers
      state wrongly or not at all.** F20: the chunked bytes are the compressed
      varlena MINUS its 4-byte header, so chunk 0 opens with `va_tcinfo` —
      confirmed byte-wise (`08 13 01 00 | 00 28 7b 51` = tcinfo 70408, then
      pglz's control byte and `(`, `{`, `Q`); chunking the whole varlena is off
      by four bytes and decompresses to garbage. F21: `va_rawsize` is the
      UNCOMPRESSED length + VARHDRSZ in both branches, which makes PG's own
      "is it compressed?" test (extsize < rawsize−4) fall out for free — goopg
      stores no flag. F22: `chunk_id == rule OID + 1` universally (0 exceptions
      over the oracle's 280 chunk rows), because upstream allocates the value
      id in the same insert that consumed the rule OID — so goopg pins
      chunk_ids as it pins rule OIDs and its pg_toast_2618 is OID-identical to
      upstream's. **The mandated "detoast on reload" sibling turned out to be a
      DECODE arm, not a detoast:** `loadViewsFromHeapForDB` discards every rule
      below `FirstUserOID` but decodes EVERY row first, and
      `decodePhysicalPGVarlena` rejects header 0x01 outright — a startup
      failure on a directory goopg itself wrote. Fixed with a `pg_node_tree`
      decode arm (sibling of the existing KindBytes encode passthrough) that
      consumes the 18 bytes verbatim; resolving them is ledgered, because no
      reader exists that could tell a real detoast from a stub. **Also corrects
      the S20.1 filing: `pg_statio_sys_tables` (1756 B) and
      `pg_statio_user_tables` (1759 B) are NOT oversize** — they are blocked
      only by their base view, so SIX values go out of line, not eight.
      **The writer is INERT until the captures land** (no seeded ev_action is
      oversize yet, so `goopg init` still emits the S20.1 empty pair byte for
      byte) — that is deliberate staging, and it is why the hosted-PG detoast
      acceptance has not been run. 5 new guards, 4 break directions proven
      fail-when-broken by scripted revert (dropping the tcinfo, rawsize from
      the compressed size, unsorted index leaves, and removing the decode arm →
      the literal "external varlena not supported" startup failure). One of
      those guards had to be repaired before it could bite: the first synthetic
      payload repeated a block verbatim, compressed ~100:1 and never crossed
      the inline budget — it passed while testing nothing. Gates:
      `internal/initdb` PASS (154 s), `internal/executor` PASS,
      `TestE2E_PGColdStartOnGoopgDataDir` PASS (2.9 s, `-count=1` — testport
      does not invalidate its cache on an `internal/initdb` change), UNITS
      PASS, `go build ./...` + `go vet` clean, pgbench smoke via the hook.
      Design `0131-0035` §S20.2a + F20/F21/F22. 2 ledger rows.
      **S20.2b LANDED 2026-08-12 — the corpus is 77 of upstream's 80 views.**
      Capture guard #5 is now "inline OR toastable": `MAX_EV_ACTION_STORED`
      still names the same 8000 B boundary as `maxInlineEvActionStored`, but
      crossing it is admitted when the tree still carries the out-of-line path
      (both `{Parent: 2618, ToastRel: 2838, …}` and `externalizeVarlenaPayload`
      grepped out of the Go tree, as the pins and pg_type set already were)
      AND the ORACLE stores that value out of line under `chunk_id =
      rule_oid + 1` with chunk lengths summing to `pg_column_size` — i.e. F20
      and F22 re-measured per captured view instead of trusted from the S20.2a
      run. Six views entered (`pg_indexes` 12043, `pg_stats` 12053,
      `pg_stats_ext` 12058, and the `pg_statio_*_tables` triple whose base
      12174 goes external while both dependents stay INLINE at 1756/1759 B —
      F14 again, and the corpus's first external-base/inline-dependent edge).
      **F23, and the whole reason S20.2a's inertness was a debt: every chunk
      tuple was written WITHOUT `HEAP_HASVARWIDTH`.** `initdb.hasVarWidthCol`
      decides the bit from a hardcoded list of type NAMES and had no `bytea`
      arm, so a hosted PG took `nocachegetattr`'s fixed-width fast path for
      chunk_data (the var-width scan is guarded by `HeapTupleHasVarWidth`,
      heaptuple.c:588), ran off the 8-byte prefix and died on
      `Assert("j > attnum")` (heaptuple.c:642) inside
      `heap_fetch_toast_slice` — an assert-disabled build would have read a
      garbage offset instead. **F24: goopg's pglz is BETTER than upstream's** —
      every externalised value stores 3-4% smaller (9002→8674, 9316→8985,
      12196→11743, 10475→10125 B), which costs `pg_stats_ext` a whole chunk
      (6 vs 7), so goopg's `pg_toast_2618` is byte-divergent from upstream's
      while the detoasted datum is identical (proven end to end). **Two views
      stayed out and NEITHER for a size reason**, both measured against a
      hosted PG rather than assumed: `pg_seclabels` (12099, 18 chunks) fails
      `could not open relation with OID 3596` — pg_seclabel (3596) and
      pg_largeobject_metadata (2995) are not on-disk relations; and
      `pg_stats_ext_exprs` (12063) fails `type with OID 10029 does not exist`
      then trips `Assert("OidIsValid(typentry->typrelid)")` (typcache.c:3082) —
      **ceiling #6**, the first about a catalog's own COMPOSITE rowtype rather
      than a missing relation or an unpopulated column (goopg seeds the array
      type 10028 and points its typelem at 10029, but never 10029 itself).
      `pg_stats_ext` also forced `_bool` (1000) and `_float8` (1022) into the
      canonical pg_type table; 1022's typalign is `'d'`, not the `'i'` every
      other array there carries. Acceptance:
      `assertNonCorpusSystemViewIsStillAbsent` inverted onto `pg_policies`, and
      `assertHostedPGSeesPgRewriteToastRelation` now runs `SELECT count(*) FROM
      pg_indexes` on a hosted PG (pointer → index 2839 → 5 chunks →
      pglz → stringToNode of the 70408-byte Query) plus a
      `chunk_id/chunks/bytes` pin over the whole heap. Two S20.1/S20.2a
      fail-when-fixed locks fired and were inverted
      (`TestPgRewriteToastPairIndexRowAndFiles` now expects 6/2 pages and
      checks EVERY page header; `TestPgRewriteEvActionDatumSwitchesRepresentation`
      now names the four toasted rule OIDs). 3 break directions proven
      fail-when-broken by scripted revert (drop the `bytea` arm → the hosted-PG
      TRAP returns; chunk_id+2 → guard #5's F22 arm; writer symbol renamed →
      the pre-S20.2 hard failure). Gates: `internal/initdb` PASS (181 s),
      `^TestE2E_` family PASS (100 s), `TestE2E_PGColdStartOnGoopgDataDir` PASS
      (-count=1), UNITS PASS, `go build ./...` + `go vet` clean, pgbench smoke
      via the hook. Design `0131-0035` §S20.2b + F23/F24. 4 ledger rows.
      **Remaining for the corpus: three views** — `pg_policies` (ceiling #4,
      pg_policy 3256), `pg_seclabels` (3596/2995) and `pg_stats_ext_exprs`
      (ceiling #6, pg_type 10029). All three are missing-catalog/missing-type
      slices; the TOAST work is DONE.
      **S9.3e LANDED 2026-08-12 — ceiling #4 CLOSED, corpus 77 → 78 of 80.**
      `pg_policy` (3256) is now an EMPTY on-disk nailed catalog: an 8-column
      `pgPolicyAttrs()` transcribed from `postgres/src/include/catalog/pg_policy.h`

      `TestE2E_PGColdStartOnGoopgDataDir` PASS (-count=1), UNITS PASS,

      **S9.3g LANDED 2026-08-12 — the corpus is upstream's 80 of 80 and the

      `nailedLocalRels{2619}.RelType` 83 → **10029**, and the pin

      pg_class side deletes the pg_type row with it (measured — break direction
      2 reproduces break direction 1's `type with OID 10029 does not exist`).
      **The slice's real content was F30, and it was NOT the missing row.**
      With 10029 seeded a hosted PG still died on `TRAP: failed
      Assert("OidIsValid(typentry->typrelid)")` from `add_paths_to_joinrel` →
      `lookup_type_cache`: `_pg_statistic` (10028) was typed `'c'` under an
      explicit comment that the value "carries no special meaning for the
      standby's TupleDescInitEntry path" — true of that path, false of every
      other. An array of a composite is a BASE type upstream (`'b'`), and
      `insert_rel_type_cache_if_needed` (`typcache.c:3082`) asserts a valid
      `typrelid` for anything claiming `'c'`, so a composite row with
      `typrelid = 0` kills the backend rather than erroring. The audit found
      **four more already-shipped instances** — the `BKI_ROWTYPE_OID` rows
      71/75/81/83 (pg_type/pg_attribute/pg_proc/pg_class), generated out of
      `pg_type.dat` and seeded with `typrelid = 0` since M0106, latent only
      because nothing had yet type-cached a catalog rowtype as a VALUE. New
      guard `TestPgTypeCompositeRowsCarryTyprelid` pins both directions
      (`pg_class.reltype` and `pg_type.typrelid` are mutual inverses). Three
      expectation guards moved with the capture: the toasted-rule set
      (five → six, +12066 at 6 chunks), `base/{1,5}/2838` (10 → 12 pages) and
      the hosted-PG chunk list (`12067/6/11089`; upstream 11481 B). 2 ledger
      rows (the nine header-declared `BKI_ROWTYPE_OID` rowtypes goopg still does
      not seed at all — `cmd/gen-pg-type-data` reads only `pg_type.dat`; and the
      view being proven to PLAN but not to produce rows, since a goopg cluster
      has no extended-statistics data to unnest). Gates: `internal/initdb` PASS
      (226 s), `^TestE2E_` family PASS (106 s),
      `TestE2E_PGColdStartOnGoopgDataDir` PASS + 2 scripted break directions,
      UNITS PASS, `go build ./...` + `go vet` clean, pgbench smoke via the hook.
      Design `0131-0009` §S9.3g + F30.
      **S9.4 MEASURED AND DEFERRED 2026-08-12 — S9 has nothing left to run.**
      The "expected to defer" verdict is now a measurement, not an assertion:
      a throwaway PG 18.3 oracle (`initdb` + `pg_ctl -o "-p 5539 -k $D -h ''"`)
      was queried for the whole corpus — 1 namespace row (OID **13273**),
      **69** `pg_class` rows (65 views + **4 real tables**), **148** `pg_type`
      rows (5 domains + 69 rowtypes + 74 array peers), **696** `pg_attribute`
      rows, **65** `pg_rewrite` rules totalling **1.81 MB** of `ev_action`,
      **11** `pg_proc` helpers, 2 domain CHECK constraints and **801 heap rows
      of DATA** (`sql_features` 755, `sql_sizing` 23,
      `sql_implementation_info` 12, `sql_parts` 11). The slice landed exactly
      one thing — **F31**: goopg registered `information_schema` at namespace
      OID **13183** under the comment "stock PG18 initdb-assigned OID", and no
      run of this build produces that; two independent fresh initdbs both
      answer **13273**. The constant is client-visible (it feeds the virtual
      `pg_catalog.pg_namespace` and `SchemaNameForOID`'s restart path), so it
      is fixed and pinned by `TestBootstrapNamespaceOIDsMatchPG18` together
      with pg_catalog/pg_toast/public and the measurement recipe. Four more
      findings reshape the successor: **F32** — `pg_depend` says ZERO
      `information_schema` rule references any `pg_catalog` *view*, so S9.4 is
      independent of S9.1–S9.3 and of the 14 view-on-view edges; **F33** — the
      TOAST ceiling is already discharged by M0131-S20.2 (11 of the 65 rules
      exceed 8160 B after pglz, but the pg_catalog corpus already ships 6
      toasted rules and `pg_seclabels` alone is larger than anything in
      `information_schema`); **F34** — 10 of the 11 helpers are new-style SQL
      functions whose body is a **`prosqlbody`** `pg_node_tree`, a SECOND
      capture surface `capture-ev-action.sh` cannot yet emit; **F35** — the 4
      tables carry real rows loaded from `sql_features.txt`, a mechanism no S9
      slice has ever produced. Deferred wholesale rather than dribbled: the
      `information_schema` name already resolves in goopg's front end (8
      virtual relations), so an on-disk namespace holding 0 of 69 relations
      would report a schema that exists and is empty — worse than clean
      absence, and the half-filled-catalog anti-pattern this milestone was
      already bitten by. Successor decomposition (S9.4a namespace+domains
      atomically, S9.4b helpers, S9.4c data tables, S9.4d.. the 65 views;
      OID band 13273..13621) is in design `0131-0009` §S9.4; **2 ledger rows**.
      **S9 CLOSURE 2026-08-12 — the successor is now FILED as milestone M0133
      (`M0133-S1..S4`, foot of this file, NOT promoted), not left as a forward
      reference into a design doc.** Before filing it, all thirteen §S9.4
      measurements were RE-VERIFIED against a second fresh PG 18.3 oracle
      (namespace 13273; 65 v + 4 r; 148 types / 5 domains / band 13286..13621;
      696 attributes; 65 rules summing 1 814 056 B, max 210 908 B; 11 helpers
      13274..13285 with 10 `prosqlbody`; 2 domain CHECKs; 11 rules over the
      8160 B ceiling; 0 rule→`pg_catalog`-view `pg_depend` edges) — this time
      the doc's findings promoted unchanged, unlike S24's. `catalog.go` does
      register 13273 and `capture-ev-action.sh` still has no `prosqlbody` mode,
      so F31 is landed and F34's blocker is live. S9 itself stays UNCHECKED:
      ledger rows + an unchecked item is the only permitted deferral form.
      Its fail-when-fixed tripwire (`information_schema.tables`) stays as F29
      installed it. Gates: `internal/catalog` PASS, UNITS PASS,
      `go build ./...` + `go vet` clean, pgbench smoke via the commit hook.
      **Superseded — S9.3f LANDED 2026-08-12 — the last instance of ceiling #4 CLOSED,
      corpus 78 → 79 of 80.** `pg_seclabels` (12099) is on disk and evaluable
      on a hosted PG. `pg_seclabel` (3596) and `pg_largeobject_metadata` (2995)
      are now EMPTY on-disk nailed catalogs (`pgSecLabelAttrs()` 5 cols —
      NO `oid` system column, attnum 1 is objoid — and
      `pgLargeObjectMetadataAttrs()` 3 cols, both re-verified against a fresh
      PG 18.3's own pg_attribute; 2995 added to
      `mappedLocalCatalogPlaceholderOIDs`, 3596 was already there laying down a
      file no pg_class row named). Each proven load-bearing by scripted revert
      (`could not open relation with OID 3596` / `… 2995` verbatim).
      `pg_largeobject` (2613) deliberately NOT added — the view's reference is
      a `::regclass` Const folded at upstream's CREATE VIEW time.
      **The slice's real content was two catalog-DESCRIPTION repairs the view
      forced (F27/F28).** A join RTE makes PG resolve EVERY column of its
      inputs via `expandRTE` → `get_rte_attribute_is_dropped` →
      `SearchSysCache2(ATTNUM)` (`parse_relation.c:3414`), not just the
      selected ones — so `pg_seclabels` audited all thirteen catalogs it joins.
      **pg_type was 14 columns and MIS-NUMBERED past attnum 12** (typelem at
      13, typarray at 14, where PG18 has typsubscript/typelem/typarray at
      13/14/15) while `pgTypeRow` had been writing all 32 values in upstream's
      order since M0106 — descriptor now 32, matching the tuples, the codec
      (`internal/catalog/codec.go:730`) and the executor. **pg_language was
      genuinely short at 7 of 9**, so `lanvalidator` (2246/2247/2248 from
      `pg_language.dat`) and `lanacl` (NULL) were added to the HEAP and the
      descriptor in the same commit. `information_schema.tables` is the new
      absence probe (F29): `pg_stats_ext_exprs` cannot hold the role because
      its failure trips `Assert("OidIsValid(typentry->typrelid)")` and kills
      the backend, so the probe crosses into S9.4's territory and becomes that
      slice's own fail-when-fixed tripwire. Three expectation guards moved with
      the change and are part of the acceptance: pg_type's column count
      (`initdb_test.go`, 14 → 32), `base/{1,5}/2838` (6 → 10 pages) and the
      toasted-rule set (four → five, +12102 at 18 chunks / 34093 B — more than
      the other four together). 2 ledger rows (companions 3597/3598/3599/2996
      unbootstrapped, inert while the heaps are empty; and the descriptor audit
      being VIEW-DRIVEN not exhaustive — `pg_namespace` describes 5 where PG18
      has 4, and any catalog no corpus view joins may still be wrong). Gates:
      `internal/initdb` PASS (214 s), `^TestE2E_` family PASS (102 s),
      `TestE2E_PGColdStartOnGoopgDataDir` PASS + 2 break directions, UNITS
      PASS, `go build ./...` + `go vet` clean, pgbench smoke via the hook.
      Design `0131-0009` §S9.3f + F27/F28/F29.
      **Remaining after S9.3f: ONE view, ONE ceiling — #6.**
      `pg_stats_ext_exprs` (12063) wants a real pg_type row for 10029
      (pg_statistic's composite rowtype). Then S9.4 (`information_schema`,
      65 views, expected to defer); its first step is the complement query
      S9.3d used (F19).
      **Superseded — remaining after S9.3e: TWO views, one ceiling.** `pg_seclabels` (12099)
      wants pg_seclabel (3596) + pg_largeobject_metadata (2995) — mechanically
      THIS slice twice over, and its 35379 B value already toasts cleanly; and
      `pg_stats_ext_exprs` (12063), ceiling #6, wants a real pg_type row for
      10029 (pg_statistic's composite rowtype).
      **Next in S9:** two ceilings left, both initdb-bootstrap gaps. The
      `pg_rewrite` TOAST (2838/2839) slice is the critical path — it gates
      EIGHT of the nine remaining views (`pg_indexes`, `pg_stats`,
      `pg_stats_ext`, `pg_stats_ext_exprs`, `pg_seclabels`,
      `pg_statio_all_tables` + its two dependents) AND (per F15) every future
      base capture; `pg_seclabels` at 35379 B makes it multi-chunk work. Off
      that path: `pg_policy` as an on-disk relation (`pg_policies`, the ninth).
      The `pg_amop` ceiling is GONE — discharged by M0131-S12, re-measured and
      adopted by S9.3d (F18). Then S9.4 (`information_schema`, expected to
      defer); its first step is the complement query S9.3d used (F19).

Theme F — Findings measured by M0131-S4 (filed 2026-08-11; each is locked into `TestE2E_PGColdStartOnGoopgDataDir` in the FAIL-WHEN-FIXED direction, so landing one turns that assertion red until it is inverted):
- [x] **M0131-S12 — Bulk-load `pg_opclass_am_name_nsp_index` (2686) at initdb** (DONE 2026-08-11; design `docs/design/0131-0018-opclass-amop-index-bulk-load.md`, 2 ledger rows). **A hosted PG can now sort** — but NOT for the reason this item predicted. `lookup_type_cache(TYPECACHE_LT_OPR)` is a THREE-hop index-only walk (2686 `GetDefaultOpClass` → 2687 `get_opclass_family` → **2653** `get_opfamily_member`/AMOPSTRATEGY, then **2654**/AMOPOPID for the planner's `get_ordering_op_properties`), and **fixing 2686 alone left the error message bit-identical** — the pre-existing "`pg_amop` 2653 blocker" ledgered under S9.1 turned out to be the SAME bug, not an adjacent one, so this slice closed all three: `bootstrapPgOpclassAmNameNspIndex`, `bootstrapPgAmopFamStratIndex`, `bootstrapPgAmopOprFamIndex` (+ new `pgBuildIndexTupleOidCharOidKey`; `bootstrapPgAmopTuples` now returns its heap TIDs). S12.5 done: `assertEmptyOpclassIndexStillBlocksSorts` fired as designed and is now `assertHostedPGCanSort`, with `0130-0002` Guard #1 restored to its `ORDER BY c.relname` form. S12.3/S12.4 resolved as NON-actions: the placeholder lists are the sibling-correct pattern (2687/2754/2755 all stay listed and are overwritten by their bootstrapper), and 2687 was already bulk-loaded. Incidental fix: 2754 passed `nkeyatts = 4` for a 3-key index and is multi-leaf, so the wrong `indnkeyatts` was baked into its pivot tuples. Gates: the S4 E2E PASS (24 s), `internal/initdb` PASS (63 s), `^TestE2E_` family PASS (100 s), UNITS + pgbench smoke via the hook. **Follow-ups ledgered:** initdb-time only (existing bench clusters need re-initdb), cross-type `pg_amop` heap rows still out of scope, and a NEW finding — an assert-enabled hosted PG traps in `procarray.c:2857` `TransactionIdIsNormal(latestCompletedXid)` from the bgwriter ~15 s in, which no current E2E runs long enough to see. **NOTE — id collision:** a SECOND, unrelated item below also carries the id `M0131-S12` (goopg stamps `DB_IN_PRODUCTION`, design `0131-0014`); it is untouched by this loop and needs renumbering. (orig est ~1 loop; MEASURED by M0131-S4 F1). A real PG hosted on a goopg directory cannot execute **any** sort, for **any** type: `SELECT x FROM (VALUES (2), (1)) v(x) ORDER BY x` fails *"could not identify an ordering operator for type integer"*. `lookup_type_cache(TYPECACHE_LT_OPR)` → `GetDefaultOpClass` scans `pg_opclass` through `OpclassAmNameNspIndexId` (**2686**) with `indexOK = true` and NO seq-scan fallback (`postgres/src/backend/commands/indexcmds.c:2374-2384`) — the same shape as blockers #7/#8 and M0131-S5. The heap is correct (probed on the hosted PG: 177 rows; `int4_ops` = oid 1978, `opcmethod` 403, `opcdefault` 't'; 38 default btree opclasses) and `pg_index` carries valid 2686/2687 rows; the index is EMPTY. `internal/initdb/initdb.go` writes 2686 as a bare `makeBtreeRootPage()` placeholder (three lists, ~:1902/:2047/:2144) while its sibling `pg_opfamily_am_name_nsp_index` (2754) has a real bulk-load bootstrapper, and `pgBuildIndexTupleOidNameOidKey` (`internal/initdb/btree_index_bootstrap.go:1909`) **already names 2686 in its doc comment** as one of the two indexes it serves — no caller ever builds its tuples. Subtasks: S12.1 add a `bootstrapPgOpclassAmNameNspIndex` on the 2754 bootstrapper's exact pattern (`btree_index_bootstrap.go:1972-2021`), keyed `(opcmethod oid_ops, opcname name_ops, opcnamespace oid_ops)` UNIQUE per `pgIndexInitialEntries` at `internal/initdb/initdb.go:4934`; S12.2 write it into `base/1` AND `base/5` like every sibling; S12.3 remove 2686 from the three empty-root-page lists; S12.4 check 2687 (`pg_opclass_oid_index`) for the same gap — it sits in the same lists and PG's `RelationCacheInitializePhase3` may nail it; S12.5 INVERT `assertEmptyOpclassIndexStillBlocksSorts` in `internal/testport/e2e_pg_coldstart_on_goopgdata_test.go`, restore the `ORDER BY` form of the Guard-#1 query and drop the Go-side sort. **Scope limit to ledger: initdb-time only** — every existing goopg `$PGDATA` (bench clusters 65433/65436/65437, any operator dir) keeps the empty index, re-initdb required.
- [x] **M0131-S13 — `pg_proc.proconfig` must be NULL, not a malformed array** (DONE 2026-08-12) (est ~1-2 loops; MEASURED by M0131-S4 F2). Calling ANY non-builtin function on a hosted PG aborts the backend: `SELECT 'a'::text || 1` gives `TRAP: failed Assert("ARR_NDIM(array) == 1"), File: "guc.c", Line: 6411` via `TransformGUCArray` ← `fmgr_security_definer` ← `client backend was terminated by signal 6`. goopg writes **all 3397** `pg_proc` rows with a non-NULL `proconfig` (probed: `SELECT count(*) FROM pg_proc WHERE proconfig IS NOT NULL` = 3397 = the full row count; `prosecdef` is correctly `'f'` everywhere, so it is `proconfig` alone) and the bytes are not a valid 1-D `text[]`. Upstream's trigger is `fmgr_info_cxt_security` (`postgres/src/backend/utils/fmgr/fmgr.c:203-211`): `prosecdef || !heap_attisnull(…, Anum_pg_proc_proconfig, NULL) || FmgrHookIsNeeded`. Blast radius is bounded by `fmgr_isbuiltin` — an OID in the compiled-in `fmgrtab` never reads `pg_proc`, so `int4eq`/`textout`/`count`/`sum` are fine; the LANGUAGE SQL builtins break, `textanycat` (2003) among them. This is an assert-enabled PG build so the failure is loud; a production build would walk a bogus `ArrayType`, which is worse. Subtasks: S13.1 find where the pg_proc heap tuple's null bitmap is built (`internal/initdb/pg_proc_seed_data.go` + its tuple encoder) and determine whether the bug is a short bitmap, a wrong `t_hoff`, or a written-empty-varlena; S13.2 **check the trailing attributes as a group** — `proconfig`, `proacl`, `prosqlbody`, `probin` are all nullable and adjacent, so a single bitmap error hits several, and the sibling-paths rule applies; S13.3 the sharper related fact from F3 — `attmissingval` is not a column in goopg's `pg_attribute` self-description, i.e. the trailing-nullable-attribute handling may be systematically short across catalogs, so probe `pg_class`/`pg_attribute`/`pg_type` the same way rather than fixing `pg_proc` alone; S13.4 INVERT `assertProconfigGapStillCrashesSQLFunctions` and fold `label || '/' || qty` back into the row-content reads. **RESOLVED 2026-08-12.** Root cause was a STALE SIBLING, not a bitmap/`t_hoff` defect as S13.1 hypothesised: goopg has two builders for the 30-column physical `pg_proc` row and only one was right. The runtime `buildPGProcRow` (`internal/executor/sys_pg_proc.go`) already wrote `executor.NullDatum` for every absent nullable varlena, with a comment saying exactly why; the initdb seed builder `pgProcRow` (`internal/initdb/initdb.go`) wrote `NewStringDatum("")`, which falls through `encodeValuePG` to `emptyArrayTypeBytes` — a NON-NULL zero-dimension `ArrayType`. `NullBitmapPG`/`writeMultiPageHeapRows` were already correct and unchanged; they simply had nothing to mark. Fixed for the whole trailing nullable group per S13.2 (`proallargtypes`, `proargmodes`, `proargnames`, `proargdefaults` when `pronargdefaults=0`, `protrftypes`, `probin`, `prosqlbody`, `proconfig`, `proacl`) — fixing `proconfig` alone would have left `prosqlbody` as an empty `pg_node_tree`, i.e. `stringToNode("")`, one probe away. **Measured on a real hosted PG:** `SELECT 'a'::text || 1` = `a1` and `SELECT count(*) FROM pg_proc WHERE proconfig IS NOT NULL` = **0** (was 3397). Guard `internal/initdb/pg_proc_nullable_varlena_test.go` sweeps all 3397 seed entries at the `Row` level AND re-decodes a physical tuple through `DecodeRowIntoMctxPGTuple` (the `Row` half alone passes even if the tuple writer drops the bitmap); proven fail-when-broken (restore `NewStringDatum("")` on proconfig → fails at `oid=3 heap_tableam_handler`). S13.4 done: `assertProconfigGapStillCrashesSQLFunctions` INVERTED to `assertHostedPGCanCallSQLBuiltins` (success + the zero-count, since either alone passes vacuously), `label || '/' || qty` folded back into the row-content reads as the new tripwire, and the probe is no longer destructive so it runs inline before guard 4. **S13.3 half-discharged:** `pg_attribute` was already correct (Step 3u fixed the identical bug); `pg_class`'s `pgClassNailedRow` still writes `relacl='{}'`/`reloptions='{}'`/`relpartbound=''` where upstream leaves NULL — LATENT (the E2E connects as superuser, who bypasses `pg_class_aclcheck`), so ledgered 2026-08-12 with a non-superuser probe as the resume point rather than fixed blind. Design: `0131-0004` §Findings F2.
- [x] **M0131-S14 — Fast defaults: `atthasmissing`/`attmissingval` (DONE 2026-08-12; a real PG 18.3 cold-started on a goopg directory now materialises an `ADD COLUMN … DEFAULT` value on pre-existing short tuples through its own `getmissingattr()`. S14.3 remains DEFERRED and ledgered — see the end of this item. MEASURED by M0131-S4 F3).** `ALTER TABLE … ADD COLUMN tag text DEFAULT 'dflt'` reads back `'dflt'` on goopg and **NULL on the hosted PG** for all 15 pre-existing rows. The `pg_attrdef` half is CORRECT and is asserted positively by the test (the hosted PG reads `pg_get_expr(adbin, adrelid)` = `'dflt'::text` on `adnum` 4, `relnatts` = 4) — that is M0130's 2604/2656/2657 work validated outside the basebackup lane for the first time. Missing is PG's fast-default mechanism: since PG 11 an ADD COLUMN with a non-volatile DEFAULT does not rewrite the heap; it stores the value in `pg_attribute.attmissingval` with `atthasmissing = true` and short tuples materialise it on read. goopg does neither. **S14.1 DONE 2026-08-12** — the prerequisite is discharged. The 42703 was NOT a missing heap row (relid 1249 always had 25, `attmissingval` among them) but `pg_class.relnatts = 24` on the nailed row: PG unlinks goopg's `pg_internal.init` at startup (`xlog.c:5633`) and builds `pg_attribute` from its own compiled 25-column `Desc_pg_attribute`, then `RelationCacheInitializePhase3` copies `rd_rel` out of goopg's `pg_class` tuple, and the short `relnatts` truncated the last column — which in PG18 order is `attmissingval`. Fixing it exposed a permutation: goopg's tail was `attacl/attoptions/attfdwoptions/attmissingval/attstattarget`, PG18's is `attstattarget/attacl/attoptions/attfdwoptions/attmissingval` (`attstattarget` moved to #21 when it became nullable; the code's comment still claimed #4, PG16's position). Invisible while all five are NULL — same null bitmap, same `t_hoff` — but `SET STATISTICS` writes `attstattarget` and a hosted PG read it as `attacl`. Four sibling definitions had drifted; `initdb.pgAttributeAttrs` (a 24-column PG-11-era layout with an `attcacheoff` PG18 removed) now derives from `pgAttrColDefs`, and the other two are pinned by `TestPGAttributeSelfDescriptionIsPG18Canonical` / `TestPGAttributeColumnsPG18IsCanonical`. Verified on a hosted PG: `attmissingval` reads, `relnatts`=25, tail attnums PG18-ordered. Ledger row 2026-08-12 M0131-S14.1 carries the two remaining type-fidelity divergences (`attoptions`/`attfdwoptions` declared `text` not `text[]`, `attmissingval` `text` not `anyarray`; `attstattarget` derived NOT NULL) — both inert until S14.2 puts a real value in the group. **S14.2 + S14.4 DONE 2026-08-12** — `buildUserPGAttributeRow` now writes `atthasmissing` plus the one-element `ArrayType` that `StoreAttrMissingVal` (`postgres/src/backend/catalog/heap.c:2030`) builds via `construct_array`: `ndim=1`, `dataoffset=0` (a NULL default stores nothing — `ATExecAddColumn` skips the call when `missingIsNull`), `elemtype` = the COLUMN's own `atttypid`, `dims[0]=lbound[0]=1`, element normalised to the 4-byte varlena header. The value itself still comes from the existing fast-default backfill (`catalog.Column.MissingValue`); an encode failure degrades to the pre-S14.2 shape rather than failing the DDL. **Two sibling divergences had to be fixed WITH it, exactly as the S14.1 ledger row predicted:** `attmissingval` was declared `text` in all three column lists where PG has `anyarray` (2277) — PG dereferences the datum as `ArrayType *` — and `anyarray` carries `typalign => 'd'` (`postgres/src/include/catalog/pg_type.dat:573`), 8 bytes, not the `'i'` every other varlena array uses; both `initdb.pgTypeAlignChar` and `executor.physicalPGTypeAlign` had grouped it with the 4-byte varlenas, which also under-padded `pg_statistic.stavalues1..5`. Same shape as S14.1 and S13 for the fourth/fifth time this milestone: an always-NULL column hides sibling disagreement until something writes it. Measured on the hosted PG: `atthasmissing='t'` for `s4_items.tag` and `tag='dflt'` on all 15 physically short pre-ALTER rows, with no heap rewrite on either side. S14.4 inverted the lock-in — `assertFastDefaultGapReadsNullOnHostedPG` → `assertFastDefaultMaterialisesOnHostedPG` (its relid-1249 tripwire is now scoped to 1249's own rows, since a user table's fast default legitimately makes the unscoped count non-zero). New guards `TestPGSingletonArrayBytesMatchesConstructArray` and `TestBuildUserPGAttributeRowLeavesMissingValNullWithoutFastDefault`; `TestPhysicalPGTypeAlignArrayTypes`'s `anyarray` expectation INVERTED 4→8. Design `0131-0004` §F3 rewritten (F3 CLOSED) + README row. **S14.3 DEFERRED (ledger row 2026-08-12 M0131-S14.2):** goopg's OWN reader still materialises from the in-memory `catalog.Column.MissingValue` (`internal/executor/codec.go`, M0097-0077) rather than from the heap's `attmissingval` — the two halves are written together but read independently. `pgSingletonArrayElement` is the inverse reader that swap needs and it is written and guarded; making the heap the source of truth is a reload/restart-surface change of its own. The same row also carries: the 8-byte `anyarray` padding changes `pg_statistic.stavalues1..5` encoding with NO in-place upgrade for an existing `$PGDATA`, and `attoptions`/`attfdwoptions` (`text` vs `text[]`) plus the `attstattarget` NotNull derivation remain, inert until something writes them.
- [x] **M0131-S15 — Runtime `CREATE DATABASE` must lay down the critical system indexes** (DONE 2026-08-12, design `0131-0032`). **The filed hypothesis below is REFUTED and that is the discovery:** the runtime path does NOT clone "only what goopg needs" — `CreatePerDatabaseScaffolding`/`copyBootstrapCatalogImage` already copies template0's ENTIRE on-disk image; **template0 itself was the stale one**. `bootstrapPostgresDatabase` materialises `base/4` EARLY in `Init` (copying a `base/1` the btree bulk-loaders have not written yet) and then stamps 1-page metapage-only placeholders over its per-database index OIDs, while the ~40 bootstrappers that follow target `base/1` and `base/5` explicitly and never revisit it. Measured on a fresh `goopg init`: `base/4` = 89 files with an **8192-byte** `2662`, against `base/5` = 149 files with 16384 — 59 files missing outright (the `pg_proc` heap 1255 among them) and 35 more stale. Fix: `initdb.refreshTemplate0Image` re-clones `base/4` from the finished `base/5` at the END of `Init` (after `bootstrapRelcacheInitFiles`, before `stampClusterChecksums` and the trailing fsync), which is upstream's own ordering — `initdb.c make_template0` creates template0 from a fully bootstrapped template1 via `STRATEGY = file_copy`, which is why a real cluster's `base/4` is byte-identical to `base/1` (301/300/375 files for 1/4/5 in `bench/tpch/runtime/pgdata`). **Whole-image, not a critical-index set** — that answers S15.2 (copy the template, don't re-run the bootstrappers per database) and makes S15.1/S15.3 moot by construction: the PANIC named one index, but the same ordering bug had left EVERY populated file behind, so enumerating `RelationCacheInitializePhase3`'s set would have fixed the symptom and kept the defect. Source is `base/5` not `base/1` because it is the superset (it has `pg_filenode.map` and the shared-index placeholder files `base/4` must keep). Guards: `TestTemplate0ImageMatchesPostgresDatabase` (`internal/initdb/template0_image_test.go`) asserts whole-directory equality plus a size floor on `2662` so a future change making both dirs equally EMPTY cannot pass, proven fail-when-broken (59 missing / 35 differing with the call removed); **S15.4 DONE** — the E2E lock-in is inverted to `assertHostedPGReadsGoopgCreatedDatabase`, which failed first in the fail-when-fixed direction and now reads `s4other`'s row through the hosted PG, and Guard 4's log scan gained coverage because the last probe is no longer destructive. 1 ledger row (no in-place upgrade for a pre-existing `$PGDATA`; `base/1` has no `pg_filenode.map`; template0 keeps a `pg_internal.init` upstream's lacks). ORIGINAL FILING: (est ~2-3 loops; MEASURED by M0131-S4 F4). Connecting a hosted PG to a database goopg minted at runtime fails `PANIC: could not open critical system index 2662` (`pg_class_oid_index`) — `RelationCacheInitializePhase3` nails and opens a small critical set during `InitPostgres` and a failure there is a PANIC, so the whole cluster goes down with the connection attempt. **This is about the runtime CREATE DATABASE path, not the cold start:** the `postgres` database in the SAME directory serves every other S4 assertion. `initdb` writes each bootstrapped btree into `base/1` **and** `base/5` (`internal/initdb/btree_index_bootstrap.go` throughout); the runtime path clones only what goopg itself needs, and goopg reads its catalogs without those indexes, so nothing in the goopg-only world notices. Subtasks: S15.1 enumerate PG's critical set (`relcache.c` `RelationCacheInitializePhase3` / `criticalRelcachesBuilt`, plus the shared set in Phase 2) and check each against what CREATE DATABASE copies; S15.2 decide the mechanism — copy `base/1`'s bootstrapped index files as the template (which is what upstream's `CREATE DATABASE … TEMPLATE` effectively does) versus re-running the bootstrappers per database; S15.3 the PANIC is on 2662 but the set is larger, so fix the set not the one index; S15.4 INVERT `assertGoopgCreatedDatabaseStillUnopenableByPG` to a direct equality.

It is not redundant with S8a's `TestNailedViewOIDsMatchUpstreamPins`, which reaches the manifest only *transitively* through `systemViewOIDPins()` — the one artefact in the set that is hand-written rather than machine-derived from a real PG. Proven fail-when-broken by repinning `pg_locks` 12073→12099 (reverted). S8b.2: `scripts/capture-ev-action.sh --verify`, knowingly red between S7 and S8b, is **PASS — 30 views + the manifest byte-identical** to a fresh throwaway PG 18.3 `initdb --no-sync` capture, so the whole committed `ev_action` corpus is now verified against the oracle with the identity OID mapping Option A assumes and no rewriting pass in between; S9 may widen the corpus by running the tool. Design findings appended to `docs/design/0131-0008-system-view-oid-policy.md` + README index. Gates: `go test ./internal/initdb/` PASS (79.9s), `--verify` PASS, UNITS, pgbench smoke via the hook.)
