- [x] **M0131-S11 — Move `HEAP_HOT_UPDATED`/`HEAP_ONLY_TUPLE` into `t_infomask2`** (DONE 2026-08-11; all five subtasks landed. Both bits now live in `t_infomask2` and every access goes through four new `storage.HeapTupleHeader` accessors — `IsHotUpdated`/`SetHotUpdated`/`IsHeapOnly`/`SetHeapOnly`, one per upstream `HeapTupleHeader*` macro — so the encode/decode siblings cannot drift apart again (Hard-won Rule #2). S11.1 writers: `PageStampHotOldTuple` + `PageStampHotOldTupleMulti` (raw-page, now read-modify-write `[off+18:off+20]`) and the HOT new-version encoder in `operators_storage.go`. S11.2/S11.3 readers: `followHOTChain`/`followHOTChainNoCopy`/the dead-to-all walk, `PageVacuumPrune`, the UPSERT arbiter follow, `isConcurrentlyUpdated`, and `internal/amcheck` (gained `readInfomask2`). **S11.4 needed no separate change** — both WAL replay paths, goopg-native and PG-format `xl_heap_update`, stamp the page through `storage.PageStampHotOldTuple` (`internal/wal/recovery.go:2896`, `:4277`), so replay moved with the writers; verified rather than assumed. Also verified rather than assumed: no `t_infomask2` write in the tree clobbers the field (every one is a read-modify-write preserving natts/`HEAP_KEYS_UPDATED`). S11.5: the S3 lock-in block is INVERTED — both `WHERE id = 9` and `WHERE label = 'label-9'` now assert the PG-written row contents through the PG-authored HOT chain, and the `id = 7` control assertion stays so a future regression is still distinguishable from a wholesale btree-read failure. Hand-stamping unit tests were moved to the accessors (not to the new field name) so they follow the field if it ever moves again; the `internal/amcheck` `setInfomask` helper splits the two HOT bits out internally, leaving its ~20 call sites untouched. 1 ledger row for the scope limit. Gates: `internal/storage`+`internal/executor`+`internal/amcheck`+`internal/wal` PASS; `TestE2E_GoopgColdStartOnPGDataDir` PASS (the measurement that filed this task, now green in the positive direction); whole `^TestE2E_` family PASS (75 s); UNITS PASS; `scripts/tpch-spotcheck.sh` PASS (Q12=2, Q13=35); pgbench smoke via the hook. **Scope limit, restated: no in-place upgrade** — the bench clusters 65433/65436/65437 and any operator directory need a reload. **Unblocks M0131-S4.**) (est ~2-3 loops, REINDEX/RELOAD-required; FILED 2026-08-11 by M0131-S3, which measured it). PG keeps both flags in **`t_infomask2`** — `HeapTupleHeaderSetHotUpdated` writes `tup->t_infomask2 |= HEAP_HOT_UPDATED` (`postgres/src/include/access/htup_details.h:550`), `HeapTupleHeaderSetHeapOnly` the same for 0x8000 (`:568`), read back at `:542`/`:562`. goopg defines the identical bit VALUES (`internal/storage/heap.go:118-122`) but keeps them in **`t_infomask`**. **Reverse direction, MEASURED by `TestE2E_GoopgColdStartOnPGDataDir`:** on a PG-authored page `followHOTChain` (`internal/executor/operators_index.go:54`) reads `Infomask & HeapHotUpdated == 0` on the chain root, declares a chain end, and BOTH index scans return **zero rows** for a HOT-updated row — while a seq scan over the same page, which never consults the flag, is correct. The un-UPDATEd row resolves through the same index, so this is HOT-specific and not a failure to read PG's btree. **Forward direction, by inspection and WORSE:** in PG's `t_infomask` 0x4000/0x8000 are `HEAP_MOVED_OFF`/`HEAP_MOVED_IN`, and `postgres/src/backend/access/heap/heapam_visibility.c:183`/`:202` branch on them to derive visibility from `t_xvac` — a hosted PG reading a goopg HOT chain does not merely lose rows, it takes the pre-9.0 binary-upgrade path on an uninitialised field. **This is a blocker for M0131-S4**, whose workload includes `UPDATE`. Subtasks: S11.1 writers — `internal/storage/heap.go:1477`, `:1589`, `internal/executor/operators_storage.go:3594`; S11.2 readers — `operators_index.go:54` (both `followHOTChain` and `followHOTChainNoCopy`) and `internal/storage/prune.go:160-163`; S11.3 sweep every remaining `HeapHotUpdated`/`HeapOnlyTuple` reference (`operators_upsert.go:705`, `operators_storage.go:3215`, `:3639`, `:3694`, the `operators_merge.go` chain walks) — **sibling-paths rule: encode and decode must flip together or every HOT chain goopg itself wrote becomes unreadable**; S11.4 WAL replay + the pruner, which read the same bits off replayed pages; S11.5 **INVERT the lock-in block** in `internal/testport/e2e_goopg_coldstart_on_pgdata_test.go` — restore the direct `WHERE id = 9` / `WHERE label = 'label-9'` assertions against `wantRow9`; the block is written to fail loudly the moment the gap closes. **Scope limit to state in the commit message and ledger:** the bits are already wrong on every page every existing goopg `$PGDATA` has written, so there is **no in-place upgrade** — the bench clusters (65433/65436/65437) and any operator directory need a reload, exactly like the S11.2/S11.3 nbtree breaks under M0130-0011. Gates: `internal/storage` + `internal/executor` units, the `^TestE2E_` family, `scripts/tpch-spotcheck.sh` (heap/visibility change → Hard-won Rule #1) and the TPC-DS SF0.5 gate. Design: `docs/design/0131-0011-hot-flags-infomask2.md` (accepted).

Theme F — Crash-state cluster-directory interchange (added 2026-08-11, user directive; removes Themes A/B's clean-shutdown precondition):

**Why:** S3 and S4 both assert `DB_SHUTDOWNED` before handing the directory over, per `0130-0002` §"WAL replay constraint". Two engines are not interchangeable on a directory if the interchange only works when the previous engine exited politely. **Theme F's filing investigation found that each direction already LOSES COMMITTED DATA today** — S11 and S12 are live bug fixes, not features, and land before everything else in the theme.
**Bounds established at filing (do NOT re-plan these):** WAL segment zeroing is a non-issue — goopg zero-fills recycled segments (`internal/wal/writer.go:2369-2379`) where upstream's `InstallXLogFileSegment` does not, so goopg is strictly safer; `CheckRequiredParameterValues` is a no-op in crash recovery (every branch gated on `ArchiveRecoveryRequested`, `xlog.c:5429`/`:5442`); and empty `pg_twophase`/`pg_commit_ts`/`pg_multixact` are all fine forward (`PrescanPreparedTransactions` over an empty dir returns `nextXid`, which is what `StartupSUBTRANS` wants; `StartupCommitTs` is gated on `track_commit_timestamp=false`; `TrimMultiXact` succeeds because initdb's zeroed `pg_multixact/offsets/0000` placeholder is load-bearing). Unlogged relations having no `_init` fork is a "too durable" divergence, not corruption — ledger it, do not absorb it here.
**Theme design:** `docs/design/0131-0012-crash-state-cluster-dir-interchange.md`. Theme F is independent of Themes C/D and may proceed in parallel.

- [x] **M0131-S16 — Fail closed: an unrecognised WAL record is not end-of-WAL** (est ~1 loop, **LIVE DATA-LOSS FIX, LAND FIRST**). `internal/wal/xlog_record.go:218-220` bounds known rmids at `MaxKnownRmgr = RmgrSeq = 15`, but PG 18's real max is 21 — so rmids **16 SPGist / 17 BRIN / 18 CommitTs / 19 ReplicationOrigin / 20 Generic / 21 LogicalMessage** fail *header decode* inside the reader, and `readAllPageAware` (`internal/wal/reader.go:148-165`) treats that as **end of WAL**. Every later record is dropped, `ReplayRecords` reports SUCCESS, and `detectWritePos` → `scanLastSegmentEnd` (`writer.go:1287`, `:1494`) then appends over the survivors, making the loss permanent on first write. One `pg_logical_emit_message` in a PG crash tail is enough. (Rmids 6/12/13/14 decode fine and hard-error at `recovery.go:2525` — that half is safe.) Subtasks: S16.1 raise the structural bound to PG's real `RM_MAX_BUILTIN_ID` 21, keep rejecting `(21,128)`; S16.2 split the two meanings collapsed into `endOfWAL` (`reader.go:212-241`) — a torn/zero/CRC-failed tail is end-of-WAL, a decodable-but-unhandled record is an error the caller must see; delete the unconditional `len(stream)-off <= segSize` silent breaks (`:157`, `:169`, `:186`, `:198`) that suppress even the `slog.Warn`; S16.3 the btree `default:` arm (`recovery.go:2516-2523`) applies FPIs only if EVERY mutated block carries `ImageApply`, else refuse — today it discards PG records lacking an FPI while returning `applied=true`, and PG emits an FPI only on a page's first post-checkpoint touch; S16.4 audit the `RmgrXLog` `default: return false, nil` (`recovery.go:2245-2249`) — keep the no-op for NOOP/SWITCH/CHECKPOINT_*/RESTORE_POINT/FPW_CHANGE/BACKUP_END/END_OF_RECOVERY/CHECKPOINT_REDO and error on genuinely unknown opcodes — but **`XLOG_NEXTOID` 0x30 is NOT benign**: `xlog_redo` sets `nextOid` exactly (`xlog.c:8292-8308`), so dropping it lets goopg re-issue OIDs PG already allocated post-checkpoint; it moves to S21a as real work; S16.5 **substantially already done** — `pg_xlog_decode.go:345-350` (the block-header decoder, not `decodeXLogBlockImage`) already errors "compressed PostgreSQL backup block images are not supported yet"; the residual defect is a SYMPTOM of S16.2 (that error is swallowed by the `<= segSize` guard and reported as a clean end-of-WAL), so keep S16.5 only to wrap it in `ErrCorruptRecord` and test that it escapes. Gate: a unit test feeding valid → rmid-18 → valid asserting a non-tail stop (today: 1 record, no error). Design: `docs/design/0131-0013-wal-reader-fail-closed.md` (**note: the design filename is `0131-0013`, not `0131-0012` — 0012 is the crash-state theme doc**). **PARTIALLY DONE 2026-08-11 — S16.1, S16.2 and S16.5 LANDED; the live data-loss half is closed.** `MaxKnownRmgr` is now upstream's `RM_MAX_BUILTIN_ID` (21) with rmids 16-21 named, new sentinel `ErrUnsupportedRecord` separates "intact but unapplicable" from `ErrCorruptRecord`'s "never durable", the four `<= segSize` silent breaks are deleted, the body-decode arm propagates `ErrUnsupportedRecord` to the caller (sound because the CRC is verified *before* body parsing), and the header-decode arm re-checks the record CRC (`durableUnknownRecord`) so a CRC-valid unknown rmid errors while a torn tail still stops silently. 6 guards in `internal/wal/reader_fail_closed_test.go`, all proven fail-when-broken. **The slice also exposed a live goopg-side bug and fixed it:** goopg's own block-ref encoder writes the real `TblOid` into the locator, but `decodeXLogBlockRefHeader` rejected every non-1663/1664 tablespace OID — so every record touching a `pg_tblspc`-resident relation was read as end-of-WAL **on goopg's own restart**; the OID now maps to `RelFileNode.TblOid` exactly as the sibling `decodeXLogSmgrCreate` already did. **STILL OPEN: S16.3** (btree `default:` arm must refuse unless every mutated block carries `ImageApply`) **and S16.4** (enumerate the `RmgrXLog` no-op opcodes, error on the rest; `XLOG_NEXTOID` moves to S21a) — both are replay-side and can turn a today-silent no-op into a refused start on goopg's own WAL, so they want a crash-restart gate, not unit tests alone. **DONE 2026-08-11 — S16.3 + S16.4 landed, S16 CLOSED.** S16.3: new `requireFullPageImages` gates the btree `default:` arm — every block reference must carry `HasImage && ImageApply` (and a record with no block refs at all is refused), else `ErrUnsupportedRecord`; the arm previously `continue`d past an imageless block and still reported `applied=true`, which is silent index corruption on a real-PG tail (PG emits an FPI only on a page's FIRST post-checkpoint touch). S16.4: `RmgrXLog`'s opcode space is enumerated — 10 named benign no-ops (CHECKPOINT_SHUTDOWN/ONLINE, NOOP, SWITCH, BACKUP_END, RESTORE_POINT, FPW_CHANGE, END_OF_RECOVERY, OVERWRITE_CONTRECORD, CHECKPOINT_REDO), a real arm for **XLOG_FPI_FOR_HINT** (was dropped into the blanket no-op, discarding a PG hint-bit page; upstream replays it on the same arm as XLOG_FPI, `xlog.c:8748`), and refusal for everything else **including XLOG_NEXTOID** (real redo is S21a; a refused start beats a rewound OID counter). **The flagged self-inflicted hazard was real and was caught:** `xlogInfoDefault` (0xF0) is NOT a PG opcode but goopg's own `classifyXLogRecord` marker for an empty-payload record (`format.go:151-153`) on `RmgrXLog` — the first cut of the refusing default arm made goopg refuse its OWN WAL, caught by the pre-existing `TestApplyRecordPrefersDecodedXLogForUnknownPayloadKind`, not by the new guards; 0xF0 is now benign-with-reasoning, which costs no real-PG coverage since PG defines nothing at 0xF0. 3 new guards in `internal/wal/reader_fail_closed_test.go` (18 subtests), all proven fail-when-broken by scripted reverts (3 FAIL, and the benign no-op subtests correctly kept PASSing). **Crash-restart gate as the plan required, not unit tests alone:** btree-heavy workload (PK + secondary index, 20k inserts / modulo DELETE / 10k inserts / VACUUM) → `kill -9` → restart → a second uncheckpointed round → `kill -9` → restart; row counts identical across both crashes (23334, then 36429), index lookups resolve, ZERO refusal lines in either restart log — so neither subslice turned a today-silent no-op into a refused start on goopg's own WAL. (Run it on a genuinely free port: the first attempt bound nothing because an orphaned server held 5533 and the workload landed in *its* cluster.) 3 ledger rows: NEXTOID redo unimplemented (S21a), native btree redo for the unflipped opcodes still missing so those PG tails are now unreplayable rather than lossy (S21b), and upstream's FPI-vs-FOR_HINT image-tolerance asymmetry not reproduced. Design `0131-0013` gained an S16.3/S16.4 Implementation-notes section; README row updated. Gates: `internal/wal` PASS + `-race` PASS, `internal/initdb` PASS (65 s), `internal/control` + `internal/storage` PASS, crash-restart gate PASS, UNITS PASS, pgbench smoke via the commit hook.
- [x] **M0131-S17 — goopg stamps `DB_IN_PRODUCTION` at startup** (DONE 2026-08-11; **the live data-loss window is closed**. New `stampInProduction` (`internal/initdb/open.go`) writes `State = DB_IN_PRODUCTION` + a current `Time` as the last act of `Open`, after replay/pool/VM/FSM/background workers and strictly before `cmd/goopg/main.go` hands the runtime to the accept loop — `main.go:453` in `runStart` is the ONLY non-test caller of `initdb.Open`, so `init`/`stop`/`checkpoint` do not stamp. S17.1 + S17.2 landed; **S17.3's probe came back negative and is ledgered**: goopg has no reader of `State` at all, so it does not act on its own stamp (that is S20.1, and the fix_plan's "S15 changes that" pointer was stale — the reader is S20). Guard `TestOpenStampsDBInProduction` (`internal/initdb/pgcontrol_runtime_state_test.go`) is proven **fail-when-broken**, not merely green: with the stamp short-circuited it reports `state after Open: got 1, want 6`. It pins the precondition (fresh `initdb` = `DB_SHUTDOWNED`), asserts `LastCheckpointLSN() == 0` at the moment of the check so a pass cannot be a checkpoint in disguise, and asserts the inverse — a clean `Close` still lands on `DB_SHUTDOWNED`, which is what `TestE2E_PGColdStartOnGoopgDataDir` depends on. Design `0131-0014` §S17 draft → accepted with a Findings section; README row rewritten. Note the plan's design filename below was wrong — the doc is `0131-0014`, not `0131-0013` (that number is the WAL-reader doc). Gates: new guard PASS + proven failing without the fix, `internal/initdb` PASS (65 s), `internal/control` + `internal/wal` PASS, both cold-start E2Es PASS (27 s), whole `^TestE2E_` family PASS, UNITS PASS, pgbench smoke via the commit hook.) The cheapest fix in the milestone and it gates every other forward item — nothing is reachable until PG actually enters recovery. `pg_control.state` has two runtime writers, the checkpointer (`internal/wal/checkpointer.go:736-748`), whose first tick is one full `checkpoint_timeout` (PG default 300 s) after start (`checkpointer.go:440`, no leading immediate checkpoint). **No startup path writes `State`.** So a SIGKILL inside that window leaves `DB_SHUTDOWNED`; PG then falls through ALL THREE arms of `postgres/src/backend/access/transam/xlogrecovery.c:924-936`, leaves `InRecovery` false, **skips `PerformWalRecovery()` entirely**, and resumes inserting WAL at the end of the shutdown checkpoint — overwriting goopg's tail with no PANIC and nothing alarming logged. Subtasks: S17.1 stamp `State = DB_IN_PRODUCTION` + a current `Time` durably in the runtime open path (`internal/initdb/open.go`, near where `internal/server/server.go:677` writes the PID file) BEFORE the first client is accepted, mirroring `xlog.c:6204-6205`; S17.2 fix the misleading comment at `internal/initdb/open.go:3195-3198` which asserts a postcondition the code does not establish; S17.3 confirm goopg's own restart path agrees (it does not read `State` today — S15 changes that). Gate: unit test with a long `checkpoint_timeout` asserting `DBStateInProduction` before any checkpoint could have run, then again after SIGKILL. No PG needed. Design: `docs/design/0131-0013-pgcontrol-runtime-state-and-durability.md`.
- [x] **M0131-S18 — pg_control writer durability + full checkpoint-struct coverage + live TLI** (**DONE 2026-08-11 — all four subtasks landed**). **S18.1 landed:** `UpdateControlFile` now ends in `writeControlFileDurably` (`internal/control/pgcontrol.go`) — `os.OpenFile(O_RDWR)` (no `O_CREATE`, no `O_TRUNC`) + `WriteAt(buf[:8192], 0)` + `Sync()` + `Close()`, mirroring `update_controlfile` with `do_sync = true`; the zero-length-pg_control window is gone and a longer file keeps its tail rather than being truncated. **S18.2 landed:** all nine remaining `checkPointCopy` members are in `ControlFileData`, `decodeControlFileData` and `encodeControlFileData` at the offsets the design tabled (76/80/84/88/92/96/112/116/120) — the table needed no correction, the real `pg_controldata` agrees on every row. **Finding that shaped the guards:** a missing *encode* line is INVISIBLE to both the oracle comparison and a byte-for-byte round-trip, because read-modify-write preserves the on-disk value — the field is merely unsettable; only a missing *decode* line is destructive. So three assertions are needed and all five break directions were proven fail-when-broken by scripted revert: `TestPgControlCheckPointCopyMatchesPgControldata` (`internal/initdb/`, byte-for-byte identity + oracle agreement + settability; an 88→89 offset slip reports `changed byte 89`), `TestUpdateControlFileRoundTripsCheckPointCopy` / `TestUpdateControlFileWritesCheckPointCopy` / `TestWriteControlFileDurablyDoesNotTruncate` / `...RequiresExistingFile` / `...RejectsWrongSize` (`internal/control/`). No `fsync = off` GUC gate was wired in — upstream passes `do_sync = true` from every backend caller and no `UpdateControlFile` caller is per-transaction. **2 ledger rows:** the nine fields are now settable but nothing POPULATES them from live state (a goopg checkpoint still leaves `nextMulti = 1` / `oldestXid = 3` forever), and a new discovery — `encodeCheckPointStruct` never writes payload offsets 12 (`PrevTimeLineID`) or 16 (`fullPageWrites`) at all, so every goopg checkpoint RECORD carries `PrevTimeLineID = 0`; fold that into S18.4. Design `0131-0014` §S18.1/§S18.2 draft → accepted with a Findings section; README row rewritten. Gates: the 6 new guards PASS + each proven failing without the fix, `internal/control` PASS, `internal/initdb` PASS (67 s), `internal/wal` + `internal/storage` PASS, both cold-start E2Es PASS (27 s), UNITS PASS, pgbench smoke via the commit hook. **S18.3 + S18.4 as originally filed (all now done):** S18.3 thread the LIVE TLI into `Checkpointer.Config` and `UpdateControlCheckpoint` — both hardcode `1` (`checkpointer.go:757-758`, `initdb/pgcontrol.go:154-155`), so the next checkpoint after M0130-S8.5's `finalizePromotion` stomps the TLI back to 1 while segments are named for TLI 2 and PG then PANICs `could not locate a valid checkpoint record`; thread `full_page_writes` the same way (it reaches the buffer pool at `cmd/goopg/main.go:719-721` but not pg_control); S18.4 stop hardcoding `nextMulti=1`/`oldestMulti=1`/commitTs in `encodeCheckPointStruct` (`internal/wal/recovery.go:783-794`) — and add the two payload offsets it never writes at all, 12 (`PrevTimeLineID`) and 16 (`fullPageWrites`), from the same live sources S18.3 threads. Gate: golden-byte round-trip against the real `pg_controldata` in `postgres/local_install/bin` (the S18.2 test `TestPgControlCheckPointCopyMatchesPgControldata` is the harness to extend). **S18.3 + S18.4 landed 2026-08-11.** `runCheckpoint` now takes ONE sample per checkpoint into a new `wal.CheckPointFields` and feeds BOTH the record encoder and the pg_control writer from it — they were literals forty lines apart (`EncodeCheckpointPG(..., 1, ...)` and `cd.CheckPointCopyThisTLI = 1`) and PG cross-checks the two at startup, so implementing them separately would have left two driftable copies. Three live sources are wired: `TimelineIDFn` → `Writer.TimelineID` (the promotion stomp is fixed — a TLI-3 writer now produces TLI-3 in both places), `FullPageWritesFn` → `Pool.FullPageWrites` (the buffer pool, mirroring upstream's `Insert->fullPageWrites` shadow copy at xlog.c:7041, NOT the GUC text), and `SetNextMultiXactFn` → `multixact.Store.Next()` (a setter, not a config field: the store is created in `cmd/goopg/main.go` after `initdb.Open` has built the checkpointer). `encodeCheckPointStruct` now writes payload offsets 12 (`PrevTimeLineID`, mirrors ThisTLI per xlog.c:7030-7034) and 16 (`fullPageWrites`) **for the first time** — they were never written at all — plus nextMultiOffset/oldestXidDB/oldestMultiDB. **Two corrections, not preservation:** `oldestCommitTsXid`/`newestCommitTsXid` drop from a hardcoded `3` to PG's `InvalidTransactionId` (0) with `track_commit_timestamp` off, and `oldestXidDB`/`oldestMultiDB` from `0` to `Template1DbOid` (1) — the reference cluster's `pg_controldata` confirms both. `UpdateControlCheckpoint` gained a `tli` parameter (BASE_BACKUP passes the same `LoadOrCreateTimelineID` value its `START_TIMELINE` reply carries; the call moved after the timeline load) and `MinRecoveryPointTLI` moved off its own hardcoded 1 — a mismatch there is the exact xlogrecovery.c:878-886 FATAL S29 is filed for. **4 new guards, 8 break directions each proven fail-when-broken by scripted revert:** `TestEncodeCheckPointStructSettable` / `TestCheckPointFieldsDefaultsMatchPG` / `TestCheckpointerStampsLiveTimelineAndFPW` (`internal/wal/`) and `TestCheckpointerWritesLiveTimelineToPgControl` (`internal/initdb/` — a real checkpointer over a real `initdb` dir, cross-checked with the real `pg_controldata` including the text-valued `full_page_writes: off` line). The S18.1/.2 settability discipline earned its keep immediately: dropping the new `PrevTimeLineID` line is invisible to any round-trip. **2 ledger rows:** `oldestXid`/`oldestMulti` still publish the bootstrap floor (the datfrozenxid horizon exists only inside the CLOG-truncation closure, computed AFTER the marker is durable), and the live multixact counter is in-memory-only and never seeded back on restart (S20.4 owns the reader half, S24 the SLRU). Gates: the 4 new guards PASS + each proven failing without the fix, `internal/wal` PASS (7 s), `internal/control` PASS, `internal/initdb` PASS (68 s), cold-start + standby E2Es PASS (34 s), UNITS PASS, pgbench smoke via the commit hook. Design: `docs/design/0131-0014-pgcontrol-runtime-state-and-durability.md` (NOT `-0013`, which is the WAL-reader doc).
- [x] **M0131-S19 — Validate `xlp_pageaddr`/`xlp_tli`; stop trusting recycled segments** (DONE 2026-08-11; **goopg no longer reads a PostgreSQL-recycled WAL segment as live WAL.** New `xlogPageValidator` (`internal/wal/xlog_page.go`) ports `XLogReaderValidatePageHeader` plus the two cross-page fields upstream keeps in `XLogReaderState` — one type, two call sites, so the reader (`readAllPageAware`) and writer (`scanLastSegmentEnd`) halves cannot drift (Hard-won Rule #2). Five rules: header form vs position in segment, `xlp_seg_size`, `xlp_xlog_blcksz`, `xlp_pageaddr == stored address`, and TLI not going backwards. **The design's hedge was right about the writer half and wrong about why the reader half is conditional:** the writer fixture reproduced immediately (`detectWritePos` returned **32904** against a true end of **136** — a whole segment inside stale bytes), but the reader fixture did NOT, because of a sixth hole the design never named — **`extractRecordBytes` swallows straddled page headers without looking at them**, so validating only where the walk LANDS on a boundary catches or misses a stale page depending on record tiling (with 400 uniform records the boundary always fell inside a record and the stale page was never examined). Upstream has no such hole — `XLogReadRecord` validates continuation pages too. Hence `checkSpan`, called by both walkers after `extractRecordBytes`; with it the reader half is unconditional as well. No change was needed to `detectWritePos`'s phantom-drop loop or its "non-final segments are fully used" rule: a recycled segment now scans as EMPTY and is dropped before either can misfire. The all-zero-header preallocated-tail sentinel is still checked first everywhere, so goopg's own EOS meaning is untouched. 1 ledger row (`xlp_sysid` still uncompared — the validator has no pg_control handle and threading one in reaches the crash-recovery entry path). 3 new guards in `internal/wal/recycled_segment_test.go`, each proven fail-when-broken by scripted revert over a /tmp backup across 4 break directions, each caught by a different assertion. Note the older `TestDetectWritePos_*` fixtures use segSize=1024 — SMALLER than `XLOGBlockSize`, so they never cross a page boundary at all; the S19 fixtures use `4*XLOGBlockSize`. Gates: `internal/wal` PASS (5.7 s) + RACE PASS (8.2 s), `internal/initdb` PASS (65 s), `TestPort_Recovery`+`TestE2E_` PASS (112 s), **crash-restart smoke** (18 353 pgbench txns → SIGKILL via postmaster.pid → restart: `count|sum` identical at `500000|-301853`, further pgbench clean at 924 tps), SPOT `scripts/tpch-spotcheck.sh` RESULT=PASS (Q12 rows=2, Q13 rows=35), UNITS PASS, pgbench smoke via the commit hook. Design `0131-0013` updated with a full S19 implementation-notes section; README status flipped to CLOSED. **Trap re-encountered:** the first crash-restart attempt silently talked to an ORPHANED server from an earlier session already bound to 5533 — `bind: address already in use` was buried in the server log while `pg_isready` answered happily; grep the log for that string before believing any lifecycle result.) (est ~2 loops, RISKY). **This also fixes the CLEAN reverse path (S3), so it is not gated on the crash work.** goopg zero-fills recycled segments; **PG does not** — `InstallXLogFileSegment` (`postgres/src/backend/access/transam/xlog.c:3559`) is a bare `durable_rename`, so a real PG's `pg_wal` holds full-size future segments packed with stale CRC-valid records from a previous cycle, and PG's only defence is the `xlp_pageaddr` check at `postgres/src/backend/access/transam/xlogreader.c:1324-1340`. goopg decodes `PageAddr` (`internal/wal/xlog_page.go:91`, `:144`) and writes it (`xlog_emit.go:125`) but **never compares it** — grep returns no other consumer. Subtasks: S19.1 `reader.go` — **no per-page header decode exists to hook** (the walk's only `DecodeXLogPageHeader` is `:119`, the first-page contrecord skip), so S19.1 must ADD one, then require `hdr.PageAddr == baseOffset+off` and a consistent TLI, else stop as end-of-WAL; consider validating `xlp_sysid` against `pg_control.system_identifier` (S2 made that readable); S19.2 the **sibling path** per Hard-won Rule #2 — `scanLastSegmentEnd` (`writer.go:1538-1549`), plus `detectWritePos`'s "non-final segments are fully used" assumption (`:1435-1440`) and its phantom-drop loop (`:1409-1417`): a PG recycled segment scans as non-empty so the loop breaks immediately and `writePos` lands tens of MB past the true end, inside garbage. **The reader half is conditional on the last page ending exactly on a boundary; the writer half is not conditional and should reproduce directly.** Risk: most likely slice to break goopg's own restart — land with SMOKE + a crash-restart test, not unit tests alone. Design: `docs/design/0131-0012-wal-reader-fail-closed.md`.
- [x] **M0131-S20 — pg_control-driven recovery in goopg: read `DBState`, redo start, startup hygiene** (**DONE 2026-08-11 — all five subtasks landed**). **S20.3 + S20.4 + S20.5 landed 2026-08-11.** **S20.3:** `catalog.RelcacheInitFileRemoveAll` + `clearRelcacheInitFiles` (`internal/initdb/recovery_state.go`) sweep EVERY `pg_internal.init` — `global/`, every all-digit `base/<n>/`, and every all-digit `pg_tblspc/<oid>/<TablespaceVersionDirectory>/<n>/` — from `Open` BEFORE replay and UNCONDITIONALLY, mirroring `RelationCacheInitFileRemove` (xlog.c:5633; upstream sweeps even after a clean shutdown, "it seems safest to just remove them always"). Failure warns and continues (upstream's elevel = LOG at this call site). **The loop's finding — the obvious guard is green while proving nothing:** planting a foreign `global/pg_internal.init` and asserting it is gone after `Open` PASSES with the sweep deleted outright AND with it made conditional on `crashRecovery` (both scripted and verified), because `Open` regenerates `global/`, `base/1/` and `base/5/` after replay (`relcache_init.go:31-47` from `open.go:1690/1844`). The guard had to plant in `base/16384`, a database goopg does not regenerate for — which is exactly the gap S20.3 closes, since the pre-existing reactive `RelcacheInitFileUnlink` takes ONE `dboid` and so never reached a database the session had not itself invalidated. **S20.4:** `beginRecovery` also returns `checkPointCopy.nextMulti`, `Open` publishes it as `Runtime.NextMultiXact`, and `cmd/goopg/main.go` builds the process-shared store with `multixact.NewStoreAt(...)` instead of `NewStore()` — the seam had existed since M0118-0003 with **no caller**, so every restart rewound the allocator to `FirstMultiXactId` and re-issued MultiXactIds that live tuple `xmax` fields (on disk, outliving the process) already carry. `NewStoreAt` clamps, so the no-control-file case (0) degrades exactly to the old behaviour. **S20.5:** the policy is now executable, not a comment — upstream's rule is `minRecoveryPoint = InvalidXLogRecPtr` / `minRecoveryPointTLI = 0` on every checkpoint a non-recovering cluster writes (xlog.c:7295-7297, "crash recovery should always recover to the end of WAL"), with `InitWalRecovery` adopting the control file's value only when `InArchiveRecovery` (xlog.c:5778-5794). goopg already held the policy; nothing would have noticed it breaking. The guard POISONS both fields (`0xDEAD`/tli 9) before a crash-shaped `Open`, so a pass requires the end-of-recovery checkpoint to actively clear them. Confirmed `internal/server/basebackup.go:232` is the only caller of `UpdateControlCheckpoint`, so the `MinRecoveryPoint = 1` divergence is confined to the backup lane — but it mutates the LIVE primary's control file where upstream streams a modified copy, ledgered against S29. **5 new guards, 7 break directions each proven fail-when-broken by scripted revert** (`TestClearRelcacheInitFilesSweepsWholeCluster`, `TestClearRelcacheInitFilesToleratesMissingDirs`, `TestOpenRemovesPreexistingRelcacheInitFile`, `TestOpenSeedsMultiXactFromPgControl`, `TestOpenWithoutControlFileLeavesMultiXactUnseeded`, `TestCrashRecoveryLeavesMinRecoveryPointInvalid` — `internal/initdb/recovery_startup_hygiene_test.go`). **3 ledger rows:** no `tablespace_map` reader so a tablespace present only in a restored backup is not swept; multixact MEMBER SETS are still in-memory (S24 owns the SLRU), so a seeded id resolves to an unknown set; and the BASE_BACKUP `minRecoveryPoint` violator (S29). Design `0131-0014` §S20.3/.4/.5 Findings + corrected Guards 7-9; README row rewritten. **S20.1 landed:** `beginRecovery` (`internal/initdb/recovery_state.go`) is goopg's FIRST reader of `pg_control.State` — `Open` now asks it before replaying anything, stamps `DB_IN_CRASH_RECOVERY` durably when the previous run did not shut down cleanly, and forces an end-of-recovery checkpoint (`CheckpointNow`) after replay and before S17's `DB_IN_PRODUCTION` stamp. **One plan correction:** the filing said "neither Shutdowned nor ShutdownedInRecovery"; upstream's test is a single `state != DB_SHUTDOWNED` (`xlogrecovery.c:931`) — a standby shut down tidily still has to replay back to consistency, so the two-value set would have skipped recovery on exactly the cluster that needs it. Upstream's SECOND arm is implemented too (`redo < CheckPoint` ⇒ the last checkpoint was ONLINE ⇒ the cluster never ran its shutdown checkpoint); verified it does not misfire on a clean goopg restart, whose shutdown checkpoint satisfies `redo == CheckPoint` exactly. **S20.2 landed:** `wal.ReplayRecordsFrom`/`replayStartAt`/`ReplayFromDirWithMgrAt` take the redo anchor from `checkPointCopy.redo` the way `InitWalRecovery` does, with the stream scan surviving only for `redo == 0`; **the "teach `isCheckpointRecord` about `XLOG_CHECKPOINT_REDO` (0xE0)" subtask is DROPPED as a mis-specification** — that record carries 4 bytes of `wal_level`, not an 88-byte `CheckPoint` struct, and `checkpointStructOf`/`DiscoverLastCheckpointLSN` both want the struct; the marker's address is exactly what `checkPointCopy.redo` now supplies directly. Also new: `control.DBStateName` (pg_controldata's `dbState[]` strings) so the startup log greps against a real cluster's. **6 break directions proven fail-when-broken by scripted revert** across `TestBeginRecoveryReadsPgControlState` (5 subtests), `TestOpenAfterCrashRunsEndOfRecoveryCheckpoint` (`internal/initdb/recovery_state_test.go`) and `TestReplayStartAtUsesControlFileRedo`/`TestReplayStartAtOverridesTheScan` (`internal/wal/replay_redo_start_test.go`). **3 ledger rows**, one of them a wrong-answer-class discovery filed as its own task below (crash recovery loses AND duplicates rows — pre-existing, reproduced at the parent commit). Gates: the 6 new guards PASS + each proven failing without the fix, `internal/wal` + `internal/control` + `internal/initdb` PASS (74 s), cold-start/standby/failover E2Es PASS (68 s), in-situ SIGKILL crash-restart smoke, UNITS, `scripts/tpch-spotcheck.sh`, pgbench smoke via the commit hook. Design: `docs/design/0131-0014-pgcontrol-runtime-state-and-durability.md` §S20 + Findings (NOT `-0013`, which is the WAL-reader doc). **Remaining: S20.3 / S20.4 / S20.5 only.** goopg **never reads `pg_control.State`** — the constants exist (`internal/control/pgcontrol.go:29-36`) and the field is decoded (`:126`) but there is no reader anywhere in `internal/` or `cmd/`; `Open()` reads pg_control only for `DataChecksumVersion` (`internal/initdb/open.go:292`) and later `nextOid`/`nextXid` (`:1188-1206`). It also finds its redo start by SCANNING the WAL for the last checkpoint (`internal/wal/recovery.go:4408-4432`) rather than reading `checkPointCopy.redo`. Subtasks: S20.1 read `State` before replay; if not `DBStateShutdowned`/`DBStateShutdownedInRecovery`, log "crash recovery required", set `DB_IN_CRASH_RECOVERY`, replay, then `DB_IN_PRODUCTION` + a forced end-of-recovery checkpoint; S20.2 let `replayStart` take an explicit redo LSN from `CheckPointCopyRedo` when available, keeping the scan as the goopg-authored fallback, and teach `isCheckpointRecord` about `XLOG_CHECKPOINT_REDO` (0xE0, PG17+); S20.3 add a `RelationCacheInitFileRemove` equivalent — unconditionally unlink `global/pg_internal.init` and every `base/*/pg_internal.init` BEFORE replay (today goopg only unlinks reactively, `internal/catalog/relcache_inval.go:14-21`, so a PG-authored init file survives into a goopg session; note the symmetry with S10 — goopg must do to PG's init file exactly what PG does to goopg's); S20.4 seed `multixact.NewStoreAt` from `pg_control.nextMulti` — the seam exists at `internal/multixact/store.go:88` and **has no caller**, `cmd/goopg/main.go:560` always calls `NewStore()`; S20.5 document the `minRecoveryPoint` policy (PG leaves it invalid for crash recovery; goopg must not invent one). Design: rides `docs/design/0131-0013-pgcontrol-runtime-state-and-durability.md`.
- [x] **M0131-S21 — Opcode coverage inside the already-handled rmgrs** (est ~6-8 loops, **LARGE — split 21a/21b**; re-sized after review). The bulk of the reverse work and the part `0130-0002` does not anticipate: goopg's per-rmgr arms cover only the opcodes goopg EMITS, so every other PG opcode hard-errors (or, for btree, hits S16.3's new refusal). All of the following are ordinary heap+btree traffic. **S21a non-btree (~2 loops):** **FIRST fix the mask bug — the highest-value item in the slice and absent from the original filing:** `internal/wal/recovery.go:2439` masks RM_HEAP2 with `XLRRmgrInfoMask` (0xF0) where RM_HEAP correctly uses `xlogHeapOpMask` (0x70), but `XLOG_HEAP_OPMASK` applies to HEAP2 too and upstream OR's `XLOG_HEAP_INIT_PAGE` (0x80) into MULTI_INSERT (`postgres/src/backend/access/heap/heapam.c:2607-2611`), so a COPY onto a fresh page arrives as `info == 0xD0` and would MISS its case even after the case is added. Then: RM_HEAP `LOCK` 0x60 / `CONFIRM` 0x50 (**`TRUNCATE` 0x30 needs NO redo** — upstream: "TRUNCATE is a no-op because the actions are already logged as SMGR WAL records", `heapam_xlog.c:1201-1208`; recognise it, do not implement it) (model on the native heap-lock replay at `recovery.go:4079`), and zero-extend rather than error at `recovery.go:2665` when `block.Block > nblocks` to match PG's `XLogReadBufferExtended` — a PG crash tail routinely references a block past the flushed length; RM_HEAP2 `MULTI_INSERT` 0x50 (every COPY) and `VISIBLE` 0x40 (every VACUUM) first — a native multi-insert analog exists at `recovery.go:3842`, but **`HeapVisible` gives 0% reuse — goopg's `RecordKindHeapVisible` replay is an explicit no-op (`recovery.go:2109-2118`)**, so `XLOG_HEAP2_VISIBLE` is from scratch — then `LOCK_UPDATED` 0x60, `NEW_CID` 0x70 (logical-only, no-op fine), `REWRITE` 0x00; RM_XACT `ASSIGNMENT` 0x50 and `INVALIDATIONS` 0x60 → recognised no-ops matching `postgres/src/backend/access/transam/xact.c:6428-6443`, and `PREPARE`/`COMMIT_PREPARED`/`ABORT_PREPARED` → refuse loudly (2PC recovery out of scope, `max_prepared_transactions` BootVal is `"0"`); RM_STANDBY `LOCK` 0x00 and `INVALIDATIONS` 0x20 → recognised no-ops (upstream's `standby_redo` returns immediately when `standbyState == STANDBY_DISABLED`, always true outside hot standby); RM_CLOG `ZEROPAGE` 0x00 (maps onto `EnablePGSLRUMirror`, `internal/mvcc/clog.go:567-601`); RM_SMGR `TRUNCATE` 0x20 (native `replaySmgrTruncate` exists); RM_XLOG apply `FPI_FOR_HINT` 0xA0 through the existing FPI path — currently a silent no-op **losing torn-page protection** — and handle `OVERWRITE_CONTRECORD` 0xD0. **S21b btree (~2 loops):** real redo for **six** opcodes — `INSERT_UPPER` 0x10, `INSERT_META` 0x20, `INSERT_POST` 0x50, `DEDUP` 0x60, `DELETE` 0x70, `META_CLEANUP` 0xE0 (**`REUSE_PAGE` 0xD0 needs NO redo** — its body is only the hot-standby conflict resolve, `nbtxlog.c:1006-1015`); M0130-S11's PG-identical nbtree page/tuple work is the foundation and `postgres/src/backend/access/nbtree/nbtxlog.c` the reference. **S21a-1 LANDED 2026-08-12 — the recognition layer.** The mask bug is fixed FIRST as the plan demanded (`recovery.go`'s RM_HEAP2 arm now masks with `xlogHeapOpMask` 0x70, matching `heap2_redo`'s own `info & XLOG_HEAP_OPMASK` at `heapam_xlog.c:1229`), so the MULTI_INSERT arm S21a-2 adds cannot miss a COPY-onto-a-fresh-page record (`info == 0xD0`). Six opcodes whose correct redo is genuinely NOTHING became recognised no-ops, each carrying its upstream citation: `HEAP_TRUNCATE` 0x30, `HEAP2_NEW_CID` 0x70, `XACT_ASSIGNMENT` 0x50, `XACT_INVALIDATIONS` 0x60, `STANDBY_LOCK` 0x00, `STANDBY_INVALIDATIONS` 0x20 — `STANDBY_LOCK` alone is why a PG tail containing ANY DDL refused the start. The three 2PC opcodes (`PREPARE`/`COMMIT_PREPARED`/`ABORT_PREPARED`) refuse loudly with `ErrUnsupportedRecord` and a message naming the feature, because a silent no-op on `COMMIT_PREPARED` would stamp an XID committed whose heap changes were never applied. **`XLOG_NEXTOID` moved from S16.4's refusal to a REAL apply** through a two-pass split: `replayDecodedXLogRecord` recognises it as a page-level no-op, and the new `replayNextOIDFromWAL` (`internal/initdb/xact_recovery.go`, called from `Open` right after the pg_control seed) advances `catalog.InMemory`'s counter — the OID counter is not reachable from the physical pass, which holds only a `*storage.Manager`, exactly as with `CLOG_TRUNCATE`. Without it, goopg starting on a PG cluster that crashed after allocating an OID block re-issues OIDs PG already gave to catalog rows. Two deviations recorded in a ledger row: goopg takes the MAX of pg_control and the WAL records where upstream *sets* exactly (its allocator is monotone with no wraparound path), and the scan starts at record zero rather than `ExportedReplayStart`. 4 new guards (`TestReplayRecognisesPGOnlyNoOpOpcodes`, `TestReplayRefusesTwoPhaseCommitOpcodes`, `TestReplayHeap2UsesHeapOpMask`, `TestReplayNextOIDFromWAL{,NoRecords}`), ALL proven fail-when-broken by scripted reverts; one pre-existing test corrected — `TestApplyRecordRejectsUnknownDecodedXLogStandbyRecord` used `XLOG_INVALIDATIONS` 0x20 as its stand-in for an *unknown* standby opcode, a value S21a-1 makes known, so it now names 0x30 which PG really leaves undefined. S28 correctly stays a self-arming skip (its refusal moves to the first page-mutating opcode). **S21a-2 PART 1 LANDED 2026-08-12 — `XLOG_HEAP2_MULTI_INSERT` + the replay gap.** `replayDecodedXLogHeapMultiInsert` mirrors `heap_xlog_multi_insert` (heapam_xlog.c:600-731): **both** main-data layouts — the `offsets[]` array is present ONLY when `XLOG_HEAP_INIT_PAGE` is clear, and with the bit set tuples land at `FirstOffsetNumber+i` (reading the wrong layout is silent corruption, not a decode error, so each has its own guard) — the SHORTALIGNed `xl_multi_insert_tuple` walk with upstream's exact-consumption check ("total tuple length mismatch": a short walk means a dropped tuple while replay reports success), the `PD_ALL_VISIBLE` page-header mirroring of `XLH_INSERT_ALL_VISIBLE_CLEARED`/`ALL_FROZEN_SET`, and FPI restore. The per-tuple rebuild is byte-identical to the single-tuple insert path, so the two now share `buildTupleFromXLogHeapHeader` (`xl_multi_insert_tuple[2:7]` IS `xl_heap_header`). **The replay gap closed on BOTH heap paths at once** via the new shared `redoHeapPageForBlock`: a block past the fork's flushed length is not an error — the primary's extension is not WAL-logged, so a crash tail routinely references one — and upstream zero-extends up to it (`XLogReadBufferExtended`, xlogutils.c:479-539) where goopg refused the whole start with "xlog heap-insert replay gap". One deviation, ledger row: goopg refuses (never mis-applies) a multi-insert onto an ALREADY-ALLOCATED line pointer, because `PageInsertItemRawAt` would shift the array where upstream's `PageAddItem(overwrite=true)` refills in place; the missing primitive is in `internal/storage`, not the WAL layer. 6 new guards / 9 subtests in `internal/wal/heap_multi_insert_pg_test.go`, ALL proven fail-when-broken by 5 scripted reverts. **S21a-2 PART 2 LANDED 2026-08-12 — `XLOG_HEAP_LOCK` + `XLOG_HEAP_CONFIRM`.** The two remaining RM_HEAP page mutations, one slice because both stamp a single tuple header on an already-existing page. `replayDecodedXLogHeapLock` mirrors `heap_xlog_lock`: `xl_heap_lock` decode, `xlogHeapLockInfomaskBits` (goopg's port of `fix_infomask_from_infobits`), and the new `storage.PageApplyHeapLockRedo` — the **redo sibling** of the runtime `PageSetHeapTupleLockOnly`, separate on purpose because redo must reproduce whatever PG decided (a multixact xmax, an updater's key-share lock) rather than the single lock-only xid goopg itself takes. It clears `HEAP_XMAX_BITS|HEAP_MOVED` + `HEAP_KEYS_UPDATED`, ORs the record's bits, and **when the result is locked-only clears `HEAP_HOT_UPDATED` and re-points `t_ctid` at the tuple itself** (a locker must not leave a forward chain link behind — taking that branch when the xmax is an updater's, or skipping it when it is not, is silent corruption, so each shape has its own guard), then stamps xmax and `cmax = FirstCommandId`. `isHeapXmaxLockedOnlyPG` is upstream's `HEAP_XMAX_IS_LOCKED_ONLY` in FULL, incl. the pre-9.3 bare-EXCL_LOCK clause the exported `IsHeapTupleLockOnly` omits (that one is the goopg-tuple shortcut; redo classifies tuples PG wrote). `HeapMovedOff/In` and `HeapXmaxBits` are named in `internal/storage` for the first time — goopg never sets HEAP_MOVED, but redo must still clear it or a pg_upgrade'd tuple's `t_field3` survives as a cmax. `replayDecodedXLogHeapConfirm` overwrites the speculative token a spec-insert leaves in `t_ctid` with the self-pointer. **New `redoExistingHeapPageForBlock` is the RBM_NORMAL sibling of part 1's `redoHeapPageForBlock`**: a block past the fork length or an all-zero page is SKIPPED (BLK_NOTFOUND, `xlogutils.c:500-540`), never zero-extended — the asymmetry is upstream's, since a pure tuple stamp can only find its page missing if the relation is dropped later in the same stream. 8 guards / 9 subtests in `internal/wal/heap_lock_confirm_pg_test.go`, ALL proven fail-when-broken by 6 scripted reverts. Also fixed: part 1's `TestReplayPGHeapMultiInsertRefusesLinePointerReuse` seeded from a **map literal**, so Go's randomised iteration order failed it on ~half of all runs ("slot 2 out of range") — ordered slice now. 2 ledger rows (no invalid-page table; `XLH_LOCK_ALL_FROZEN_CLEARED` not acted on — both resume in the VISIBLE slice). **S21a-2 PART 3 LANDED 2026-08-12 — `XLOG_HEAP2_VISIBLE` + the VM fork.** Every VACUUM emits one per page it marks all-visible (and an INSERT that freezes a page it filled emits one too), with 0% reuse from goopg's native `RecordKindHeapVisible` no-op, and it is the FIRST goopg redo to write a fork other than `main`. `replayDecodedXLogHeap2Visible` mirrors `heap_xlog_visible` (heapam_xlog.c): block 1's heap page gets `PD_ALL_VISIBLE`, block 0's visibility-map page gets the record's bits, and **the halves are independent** — upstream's "if the heap file has dropped or truncated later in recovery, we don't need to update the page, but we'd better still update the visibility map", so the heap-side `redoExistingHeapPageForBlock` skip must not skip the map. New `redoVMPageForBlock` is the `RBM_ZERO_ON_ERROR` member of the `redo*PageForBlock` family (a vm fork shorter than the referenced block is initialised on the spot, not a replay gap — vm extensions are no more WAL-logged than heap ones). **The "thread a VM handle into replay" premise from part 2 turned out to be wrong and better:** `ApplyRecord` needs no `*storage.VisibilityMap`, because crash recovery replays at `initdb/open.go:380` while `VMLoadForks` populates `Runtime.VM` at `:2472` — a redo that mutated the in-memory map would be *overwritten* by that load, whereas the on-disk `_vm` fork is picked up by it; the fork layout was already PG's, so only the addressing arithmetic was new (`internal/storage/vm_redo.go` ports `HEAPBLK_TO_MAPBLOCK`/`MAPBYTE`/`OFFSET` + the `visibilitymap_set` OR with upstream's `flags != status` short-circuit and the `visibilitymap_clear` AND-NOT that refuses to clear ALL_VISIBLE alone). `VISIBILITYMAP_XLOG_CATALOG_REL` is wire-only and masked off; a flags byte outside `VISIBILITYMAP_XLOG_VALID_BITS` refuses where upstream merely asserts. **Part 2's `XLH_LOCK_ALL_FROZEN_CLEARED` deferral is discharged**, in upstream's position: `heap_xlog_lock` clears the block's ALL_FROZEN bit BEFORE and independently of the heap page redo ("the visibility map may need to be fixed even if the heap page is already up-to-date"), so a pd_lsn-skipped lock record still fixes the map — its own guard, since the alternative leaves an index-only scan trusting a frozen bit for a page that now carries a live locker. 6 guards / 8 subtests in `internal/wal/heap_visible_pg_test.go` + 4 in `internal/storage/vm_redo_test.go`, ALL proven fail-when-broken by 6 scripted reverts. 4 deferrals in one ledger row: hot-standby snapshot-conflict resolution, upstream's FSM free-space update, `VMSaveForks` dropping ALL_FROZEN (the in-memory VM is `[]bool`), and the deliberate no-extend on a clear. **S21a-2 PART 4 LANDED 2026-08-12 — `XLOG_HEAP2_LOCK_UPDATED`.** `heap_xlog_lock_updated`'s near-sibling relationship to part 2's `heap_xlog_lock` is exact at the wire level — `xl_heap_lock_updated` is byte-identical to `xl_heap_lock`, so `replayDecodedXLogHeap2LockUpdated` reuses `decodeXLogHeapLockMainData`, `xlogHeapLockInfomaskBits`, `redoExistingHeapPageForBlock`, and the `XLH_LOCK_ALL_FROZEN_CLEARED` → `redoClearVMBitsForHeapBlock` call verbatim — but the tuple mutation itself is deliberately smaller: new `storage.PageApplyHeapLockUpdatedRedo` clears `HEAP_XMAX_BITS|HEAP_MOVED`, ORs in the record's bits, and stamps xmax, but (unlike `PageApplyHeapLockRedo`) never touches `t_ctid`/`HEAP_HOT_UPDATED` and never stamps `cmax` — upstream's redo for this opcode has no locked-only branch and no cmax write at all, because re-locking an *older* version of an update chain (which is what this opcode means: the original lock target was concurrently updated, so the locker re-locks the newest visible version instead) can never legitimately claim to be the chain head or the locker's own command target. 7 guards in `internal/wal/heap_lock_updated_pg_test.go`, ALL proven fail-when-broken by 3 scripted reverts (ctid/HOT-bit stamp, cmax stamp, dispatch wiring). **S21a-2 PART 5 LANDED 2026-08-12 — `CLOG_ZEROPAGE`.** RM_CLOG's `WriteZeroPageXlogRec` opcode (0x00), fired once per 32768 XIDs right before the first commit/abort into a fresh CLOG page. Unlike `CLOG_TRUNCATE` it needs no `*mvcc.CLog`/catalog handle — it is pure page-zeroing at a fixed segment offset — so `replayDecodedXLogClogZeroPage` runs directly in the physical pass (`initdb/open.go:380`, well before `mvcc.OpenCLog` at `:1006`) rather than deferring to `replayCLogFromWAL`'s initdb second pass. It writes a zero-filled BLCKSZ page into the `pg_xact/` segment named `"%04X"` of `pageno/32` at offset `(pageno%32)*BLCKSZ`, creating the segment if absent — a deliberate small duplication of `clogBufferPool.segPathForPage`'s arithmetic (`wal` cannot import `internal/mvcc`; the reverse import already exists for the async-commit WAL-flush hook). **Why an arm is needed at all, given goopg's own CLOG fault-in already zero-fills a missing/short segment (`clogBufferPool.readPageFromDisk`) and so silently dropping this record was accidentally harmless for goopg's own reads:** upstream `SimpleLruReadPage` hard-errors on a missing segment rather than zero-filling, so a real PG standby cold-starting on a goopg-written cluster, or any tool reading `pg_xact/` directly (`pg_resetwal`, `amcheck`), needs the segment to physically exist for every XID range ever assigned — a crashed real PG's WAL tail that allocated a fresh CLOG page left that segment absent. 2 guards in `internal/wal/clog_zeropage_pg_test.go` (fresh low-segment page; a high-segment pageno names its segment file correctly and does not disturb segment 0), proven fail-when-broken by a scripted revert of the dispatch case. **S21a-2 PART 6 LANDED 2026-08-12 — `XLOG_SMGR_TRUNCATE`.** RM_SMGR's 0x20 opcode — the physical half of every TABLE/INDEX TRUNCATE and every VACUUM tail truncation (`XLOG_HEAP_TRUNCATE` above is the logical-decoding-only no-op; the real shrink arrives here, `heapam_xlog.c:1201-1208`). goopg's own native `RecordKindSmgrTruncate`/`replaySmgrTruncate` always drops a relfile to zero blocks and its decoder carries no `blkno`/fork bitmask — a real PG's VACUUM tail truncation is a genuinely partial shrink of the main fork plus an independent zeroing of the vm/fsm forks, which those primitives cannot express. Two new pieces: `storage.relFile.truncateTo`/`Manager.TruncateRelationTo(rel, n)` (idempotent — no-op if the file is already `<= n` blocks, mirroring `TruncateRelation`'s zero-block idempotency) alongside the decoder `decodeXLogSmgrTruncate` (`BlockNumber blkno` + `RelFileLocator` + `int flags`, 20 bytes, `storage_xlog.h:46-51`, reusing `decodeXLogSmgrCreate`'s default/global-tablespace-OID remap to goopg's TblOid=0 convention). `applySmgrTruncate` mirrors `smgr_redo`'s exact order: forcibly `applySmgrCreate`s the main fork first (a later-dropped relation still gets its truncate replayed, matching upstream's "prefer to recreate the rel … until the drop is seen"), then truncates main to `blkno` under `SMGR_TRUNCATE_HEAP`, vm to 0 under `_VM`, fsm to 0 under `_FSM`, each flag independent. Deliberate deviation needing no ledger row: upstream's pre-truncate `XLogFlush(lsn)` is a live-server torn-truncate durability guard with no counterpart in goopg's single-threaded startup replay pass. 4 guards in `internal/wal/smgr_truncate_pg_test.go` (partial main-fork truncate to a non-zero blkno, idempotent on replay; VM-only flag leaves main/fsm untouched; a truncate naming a relation with no on-disk main fork recreates it then truncates per the flags; tablespace-OID remap round-trip), ALL proven fail-when-broken by a scripted revert of the dispatch case. **S21a-2 PART 7 LANDED 2026-08-12 — `XLOG_HEAP2_REWRITE`'s loud refusal, CLOSING S21a-2.** RM_HEAP2's 0x00, the only S21a-2 opcode whose landing is a refusal rather than a redo. PG emits it while a `VACUUM FULL`/`CLUSTER` rewrites a table whose pre-rewrite row versions a logical replication slot may still have to decode (`rewriteheap.c:894`, reachable only via `logical_rewrite_log_mapping` — `wal_level=logical` plus a slot that can reach the relation), and its redo touches no relation page at all: it truncates a `pg_logical/mappings/` file to the record's `offset`, rewrites the old-ctid→new-ctid mapping tail and fsyncs (`heap_xlog_logical_rewrite`, `rewriteheap.c:1073-1160`). goopg has no `pg_logical/mappings` consumer, so neither alternative is honest — implementing the redo maintains a file nothing reads (a half implementation whose only effect is hiding the gap), and recognising it as a no-op the way `HEAP2_NEW_CID` is would leave a slot on the resulting cluster decoding the rewritten table against mappings that stop mid-rewrite with nothing reporting an error. The arm returns `ErrUnsupportedRecord` + a message naming the feature (same shape as the 2PC refusal); the class matters because it is what stops the reader treating a durable record as a torn tail (`format.go`, S16.2) — before this arm the record fell to RM_HEAP2's `default:`, whose bare error carries no class. 3 guards in `internal/wal/heap2_rewrite_pg_test.go` (dispatch-level refusal + wording; a real-shaped 40-byte `xl_heap_rewrite_mapping` record driven through encode→decode→`ApplyRecord` hits the refusal rather than an earlier decode error; `HEAP2_NEW_CID` still returns `(false, nil)` so the new arm has not swallowed its neighbour), proven fail-when-broken by a scripted revert of the dispatch case. One ledger row for the unimplemented mapping-file redo. **S21b PART 1 LANDED 2026-08-12 — `INSERT_UPPER` 0x10 + `INSERT_META` 0x20 + `META_CLEANUP` 0xE0.** The three btree opcodes whose whole redo is a downlink insert and/or a metapage rewrite — no new page primitive, which is what makes them one coherent slice. `replayDecodedXLogBtreeInsert` grew upstream's own arguments (the function is literally `btree_xlog_insert(isleaf, ismeta, posting)`, nbtxlog.c:160-247, instantiated four ways; goopg already had `(true,false,false)`) plus two limbs in upstream's order: block 1 when `!isleaf` — an insert into an INTERNAL page IS the completion of the child's split, so redo clears the child's `BTP_INCOMPLETE_SPLIT`; goopg REFUSES a record missing block 1 rather than skipping the limb, because `_bt_insertonpg` registers `cbuf` unconditionally on that path (nbtinsert.c:1342-1343) and upstream PANICs on an unregistered id, so a skip would leave a permanently half-split child while replay reported success; and block 2 when `ismeta` — the WILL_INIT metapage rebuilt from the carried `xl_btree_metadata`. **The block-0 image branch must NOT return early** (the shape the pre-S21b one-limb insert replay had): upstream's `XLogReadBufferForRedo` reports BLK_RESTORED for block 0 only and still reaches `if (ismeta) _bt_restore_meta(record, 2)`, so returning after the image restore leaves the metapage naming a stale root with replay reporting success — its own guard. `_bt_restore_meta` became the shared `replayDecodedXLogBtreeRestoreMeta(…, blockID, what)` because three call sites need it on TWO different block ids (2 for INSERT_META and NEWROOT, **0** for META_CLEANUP) — hard-coding the id, the obvious refactor, would silently rebuild the wrong page; NEWROOT's metapage limb folded onto it unchanged (its error strings were already identical). 5 guards in `internal/wal/btree_insert_upper_pg_test.go`, ALL proven fail-when-broken by 4 scripted reverts (dropped child limb → 2 FAIL; early return on a block-0 image → 1 FAIL; META_CLEANUP reading block 2 → 1 FAIL; all three dispatch arms removed → 5 FAIL). **Correction to the S21a-2 closing note:** `REUSE_PAGE` 0xD0 was NOT recognised in S21a-1 (its six no-ops are all RM_HEAP/HEAP2/XACT/STANDBY); it still hits the `default:` refusal and is part 3's work. **S21b part 2a landed 2026-08-12:** `XLOG_BTREE_INSERT_POST` (0x50) — the leaf insert whose heap TID fell INSIDE an existing posting list, so the primary SPLIT that posting (`_bt_insertonpg`'s `postingoff > 0` path). goopg never emits it (its duplicate-key inserts APPEND via `appendTIDToPosting`) but deduplication is ON BY DEFAULT in every real PG index whose opclass allows it, so it is ordinary crash-tail traffic. The non-obvious part: **the record does not carry the item that ends up on the page** — block 0's data run is `{uint16 postingoff, orignewitem}`, and redo must re-run `_bt_swap_posting`, which grows NOTHING: the posting keeps its exact byte length and TID count, its rightmost/max TID falls off the end, `orignewitem`'s TID fills the gap at `postingoff`, and the EVICTED max TID becomes the final new item's `t_tid`. Inserting `orignewitem` verbatim leaves both items holding the wrong TID — an index that scans wrong rather than one that fails to start. goopg's first posting-list WRITERS landed in `internal/access/btree/posting.go`: `SwapPosting` (`_bt_swap_posting`) and the exported `PGBTPostingRaw` (`_bt_form_posting`), the latter now the ONE tuple-format posting encoder (`marshalPosting` delegates; byte-identical, no on-disk bytes move). `btree.ApplyInsertPostingRecordAt` is the page-level redo; `replayDecodedXLogBtreeInsert` grew the third upstream argument `posting` and is now instantiated all four ways. Refusals BEFORE the page is touched for `postingoff` outside `(0, nhtids)`, a non-posting left neighbour, and `offnum < 2` (whose unchecked `offnum-1` underflows to 65535). 5 guards in `internal/wal/btree_insert_post_pg_test.go` (incl. the FPI shape: restore the image, do NOT re-run the split on top), proven fail-when-broken by 2 scripted reverts. **S21b part 2b landed 2026-08-12** — `DEDUP` 0x60, the leaf deduplication pass and the most common PG-only btree opcode in a crash tail (dedup is the default for every opclass that allows it; it is the record S16.3 named when it made the FPI-only `default:` a refusal). The record carries NO tuples — `xl_btree_dedup{nintervals}` plus a block-0 array of `BTDedupInterval{baseoff, nitems}` — so redo REBUILDS the page from its own pre-image: `btree.ReplayDedupPage` collapses each interval's run into one posting via `PGBTPostingRaw` (`_bt_form_posting`) and copies the rest verbatim, where upstream re-runs its dedup state machine under the same bounds. Two traps, both yielding the right item COUNT when got wrong: `nitems` counts ITEMS not heap TIDs (a run's base may already be a posting, contributing several TIDs — upstream's `basetupsize = BTreeTupleGetPostingOffset(base)`), and the high key must NOT be re-added because `resetPageItems` re-installs it for every rewrite path in the package. `BTP_HAS_GARBAGE` cleared as upstream does; FPI branch restores and stops. Refusals before the page is touched for a short interval array vs the record's own `nintervals`, an unmatched `baseoff`, a one-item run, an overrunning run, a pivot inside a run. 6 guards in `internal/wal/btree_dedup_pg_test.go`, proven fail-when-broken by 2 scripted reverts. **S21b part 3 landed 2026-08-12 and CLOSES S21b** — `DELETE` 0x70, the LP_DEAD simple-deletion pass (`_bt_delitems_delete`, nbtpage.c:1369: an index scan marks the entry it finds dead, the next insert short of room deletes the marked ones), plus `REUSE_PAGE` 0xD0 as a NAMED no-op — upstream's `btree_xlog_reuse_page` (nbtxlog.c:1006-1015) is one `if (InHotStandby)` conflict resolve and registers no blocks at all, which is exactly why it could not stay on the S16.3 `default:` arm that demands an applicable image on EVERY block. The substance is `btree_xlog_updates` (:556-597): a posting list's TIDs die ONE AT A TIME, so the tuple is rewritten without them rather than deleted, and `updatePostingRaw` reproduces `_bt_update_posting`'s two outcomes — re-form a posting sized exactly as `_bt_form_posting` would, or, for a SINGLE survivor, collapse back to a plain non-pivot tuple (alt-TID bit off, survivor's heap TID in `t_tid`, size back to `keysize`); a one-entry posting is a tuple no PG writes and every reader keys off BT_IS_POSTING. Updates apply BEFORE the deletions because both offset arrays are in the PRE-deletion coordinate space — deleting first shifts every later offset down and rewrites the wrong tuples. **Sibling path fixed in the same slice:** `xl_btree_vacuum` carries the same payload and does the same page work, and goopg REFUSED its `nupdated > 0` form outright, so a real PG's VACUUM records were failing the start on the same missing primitive; both opcodes now share `decodeXLogBtreeDeletePayload` + `btree.ReplayDeletePage`. RM_BTREE's opcode space is now COMPLETE (every nbtxlog.h value has a named arm), so the S16.3 fallback guard's probe moved from 0xD0 to the undefined 0xF0. 7 guards in `internal/wal/btree_delete_pg_test.go`, proven fail-when-broken by 3 scripted reverts (deletions before updates → 2 FAIL; no single-survivor collapse → 1 FAIL; `xl_btree_update` decode dropped → 4 FAIL). Ledger row for what goopg still does not EMIT (no LP_DEAD deletion pass, no dedup pass, no hot-standby conflict resolution). Gates: `internal/wal` PASS + `-race` PASS, `internal/storage` PASS, UNITS, pgbench smoke via the commit hook. Design: `docs/design/0131-0015-pg-wal-opcode-coverage.md` (**note: the file is `0131-0015`, not `0131-0014` — 0014 is the pg_control doc**) §"S21a-1 / S21a-2 — implementation notes". **S21 CLOSED 2026-08-12 — the whole opcode space asserted at once, and one defect found by asserting it.** Fourteen S21a/S21b slices each landed an opcode with its own behavioural guard; none could answer the milestone's own question, which is whether any opcode PG 18 defines inside a handled rmgr STILL falls to that rmgr's `default:`. `TestReplayOpcodeSpaceCoverageForHandledRmgrs` (`internal/wal/opcode_space_coverage_pg_test.go`) enumerates upstream's space for all **16 rmgrs with a dispatch arm** — XLOG/XACT/SMGR/CLOG/DBASE/TBLSPC/RELMAP/STANDBY/HEAP2/HEAP/BTREE/SEQ/COMMIT_TS/REPLORIGIN/GENERIC/LOGICALMSG, **74 opcode probes** cross-checked against every `#define XLOG_*` in `postgres/src/include` — and the answer is **zero holes**: S21a and S21b are complete as specified, now machine-checked rather than read off this file. Probes are header-only because this is a DISPATCH test, so any error but the default arm's own is a pass; that forces an EXACT message match rather than `errors.Is(err, ErrUnsupportedRecord)`, since the deliberate refusals (2PC, `HEAP2_REWRITE`, commit_ts-tracked) carry the same sentinel on purpose. **The defect is in the controls:** each rmgr also probes a value PG leaves undefined, which must land on `default:` — and 13 of the 14 failed, 12 for one cause: **only RM_XLOG's `default:` carried `ErrUnsupportedRecord`**, the other thirteen returned a bare error. That contradicts `format.go:45-56` (an unsupported record is durable, categorically unlike a torn tail, and a caller must be able to tell them apart) and is the exact hazard S21a-2 part 7 named. It survived fourteen slices because nothing observable changed — both `ApplyRecord` callers refuse the start on ANY error — which is the argument for pinning it now rather than after the first caller that discriminates. All 16 default arms now route through the new `unsupportedDecodedXLogOpcode` (`internal/wal/recovery.go`). RM_BTREE's control expects different wording on purpose: its `default:` is S16.3's FPI fallback, not a bare refusal. Out of scope and stated, not assumed: RM_HASH/GIN/GIST/SPGIST/BRIN are refused WHOLESALE by rmgr (S25), so enumerating their opcodes would assert the opposite of what S25 decided; RM_MULTIXACT has no arm at all and is S24's open work. No new ledger row — nothing PG-behavioural was deferred by this slice. Both halves proven fail-when-broken by scripted reverts (drop the `XLOG_HEAP_INPLACE` arm → 1 FAIL naming it; drop the class → 13 control FAILs). Gates: `internal/wal` PASS + `-race` PASS, `internal/initdb` PASS, UNITS, pgbench smoke via the commit hook. Design `0131-0015` §"S21 closure".
- [x] **M0131-S22 — CLOG replay opcode dispatch + commit-record `subxacts[]` parsing** (**DONE 2026-08-12**) (est ~1 loop). A separate second WAL pass with its own bug: `internal/initdb/xact_recovery.go:87-92` treats ANY `RmgrXact` record with a non-zero XID as commit-or-abort (`isCommit := (Info & XlogXactOpMask) == XlogXactCommit`), so a PG `XLOG_XACT_ASSIGNMENT` (0x50), `PREPARE` (0x10) or `COMMIT_PREPARED` (0x30) is **stamped ABORTED**. Separately goopg never parses the commit record's `subxacts[]` array, so **subtransactions of a committed top-level xact are never stamped committed** and their rows stay invisible after a reverse crash start. Dispatch on the opcode and parse the array. Gate: a unit test over a captured PG commit record carrying subxacts, plus an E2E whose PG workload uses a SAVEPOINT before the kill. Design: rides `docs/design/0131-0014-pg-wal-opcode-coverage.md`. **LANDED:** new `internal/wal/pg_xact_parse.go` (`XactParsed`, `ParseXactRecord` — the `ParseCommitRecord` chunk walk: xact_time → xinfo → dbinfo → subxacts, truncation = error); `internal/initdb/xact_recovery.go` dispatches on the opcode (non-COMMIT/ABORT skipped, not stamped; two-phase stays refused by the physical pass) and stamps the whole tree; mirror encoders `EncodeXactCommitPGWithSubxacts` / `EncodeXactAbortPGWithSubxacts` / `EncodeXactAssignmentPG`. 10 guards (6 wal + 4 initdb), fail-when-broken by 2 scripted reverts (dispatch dropped → 1 FAIL, subxact walk dropped → 3 FAIL). Notes in `docs/design/0131-0015-pg-wal-opcode-coverage.md` §"S22 — implementation notes". Ledger row: goopg still never EMITS `subxacts[]` in its own commit records.
- [x] **M0131-S21c — `XLOG_HEAP2_MULTI_INSERT` redo must reuse an already-allocated line pointer** (LANDED 2026-08-12) (est ~1 loop; **found 2026-08-12 while gating S22, and it is a RED GATE**). `internal/wal/recovery.go:3275` refuses a multi-insert entry whose target offset already has an allocated line pointer: `"xlog heap-multi-insert entry %d targets already-allocated line pointer %d (page has %d) — goopg redo has no in-place line-pointer reuse"`. Upstream's `heap_xlog_multi_insert` does not distinguish the two cases — it calls `PageAddItemExtended(..., PAI_OVERWRITE | PAI_IS_HEAP)` with the record's offset (`heapam_xlog.c`), and OVERWRITE reuses an UNUSED (or redirect-reclaimed) line pointer in place. A real PG tail reaches that shape whenever a page was pruned before the multi-insert (`COPY` after a `VACUUM`, which is exactly the S28 workload). **Impact:** `TestE2E_GoopgCrashStartOnPGDataDir` (S28's main variant) now HARD-FAILS rather than self-arm-skipping: its skip recognises only the pre-S21a `XLOG_NEXTOID` refusal, and once S21a made that record replay the refusal moved to this one. Verified the same failure at HEAD with the S22 changes stashed, so it is not an S22 regression. Repro: `go test ./internal/testport/ -run 'TestE2E_GoopgCrashStartOnPGDataDir$'`. Resume: `internal/wal/recovery.go:3230-3290`, allow the already-allocated case when the existing line pointer is UNUSED (and only then), mirroring `PageAddItemExtended`'s overwrite branch; then re-run the S28 E2E, which should advance to the next refusal or go green. Design: `docs/design/0131-0015-pg-wal-opcode-coverage.md` §"S21a-2 … part 1: MULTI_INSERT".

Upstream makes no such distinction — `heap_xlog_update` uses `XLogReadBufferForRedo`/`...Extended` like every other heap redo routine. **Impact:** `TestE2E_GoopgCrashStartOnPGDataDir` now fails at replay record 43900 with "block 41 is uninitialised" (it failed at 24720 before S21c, so this is forward progress, not a regression). Resume: route both the new-tuple page and the cross-page old-tuple page through `redoHeapPageForBlock`, keeping the per-page pd_lsn idempotency the function already has. - [x] **M0131-S21e — a real PG's tables are not resolvable after a goopg cold start on its `$PGDATA`** (RESOLVED 2026-08-12 by M0131-S21g, and NOT by the work described below — the diagnosis in this item was wrong. goopg's boot reload was never the problem: it scanned the replayed `base/5/1259` and kept 424 of 429 pg_class tuples. It could not decode exactly three, `pg_class physical row too short: len=4`, and those three were exactly `s28_items`/`s28_sub`/`s28_scratch` — their index and toast rows reloaded fine. The husks came from replay: an `xl_heap_update` omits the bytes the new version shares with the old one (`XLH_UPDATE_PREFIX_FROM_OLD` / `XLH_UPDATE_SUFFIX_FROM_OLD`), and goopg wrote the record's middle bytes as the whole tuple. A `pg_class.relhasindex` flip compresses to ~4 bytes, which is why tables and not indexes were hit. See S21g.) ORIGINAL TEXT: (est ~1-2 loops; found 2026-08-12 landing S21d, and it is the CURRENT stop of the S28 gate). With S21d in, the PG crash tail now replays END TO END — recovery completes and goopg writes its own checkpoint over the PG data directory — but `TestE2E_GoopgCrashStartOnPGDataDir` then fails one layer up: `SELECT count(*) FROM public.s28_items` returns `pq: relation "s28_items" does not exist (42P01)`. This is cold-start CATALOG VISIBILITY, not WAL: the relation was created by the real PG, so it exists only in the on-disk `pg_class`/`pg_attribute` heaps goopg just replayed, and goopg's startup does not build its in-memory catalog from them. Repro: `go test ./internal/testport/ -run 'TestE2E_GoopgCrashStartOnPGDataDir$'`. Resume: start from the boot-time catalog load (see `goopg_pg_class_virtual_pg_attribute_heap` — pg_class is VIRTUAL, pg_attribute is a heap) and decide where a foreign on-disk `pg_class` is read. Design: `docs/design/0131-0015-pg-wal-opcode-coverage.md` §"S21d".
- [x] **M0131-S21g — an `xl_heap_update`'s new tuple is not all in the record** (DONE 2026-08-12; **the S28 reverse crash E2E is GREEN — a real PG 18.3 cluster SIGKILLed mid-life is now served by goopg end to end**, all twelve row-equality checks matching PG's own pre-crash answers, and the `SELECT … FOR UPDATE`'d row readable and updatable afterwards). Upstream's `log_heap_update` (`postgres/src/backend/access/heap/heapam.c:8730-8800`) strips the leading/trailing run the new version shares with the old one from the record, logging `uint16 prefixlen`/`uint16 suffixlen` in front of the `xl_heap_header` and setting `XLH_UPDATE_PREFIX_FROM_OLD` (0x20) / `XLH_UPDATE_SUFFIX_FROM_OLD` (0x40); `heap_xlog_update` (`heapam_xlog.c:933-1005`) splices them back from the old tuple, which it can do because both flags imply `newblk == oldblk`. goopg DISCARDED the flags byte and treated block 0 as an `xl_heap_insert` payload, so it wrote the record's MIDDLE bytes as the whole tuple — a silent corruption, not a refusal: the page stayed structurally valid and only the catalog reload downstream noticed. New `decodeXLogHeapUpdateNewTuple` (`internal/wal/recovery.go`) does the splice, reading the old version with `storage.PageGetItemRaw` and refusing a cross-page record that claims compression; the tuple is now built INSIDE the `BLK_NEEDS_REDO` branch, as upstream does. 3 sub-tests (prefix+suffix, prefix-only, suffix-only) in `internal/wal/heap_update_pg_test.go`, proven fail-when-broken. Design: `docs/design/0131-0015-pg-wal-opcode-coverage.md` §"S21g" + Guard 10.

Upstream reads all of these with `XLogReadBufferForRedo` RBM_NORMAL, where an absent or all-zero page is `BLK_NOTFOUND` and the redo routine does nothing (`xlogutils.c:500-540`) — the page can only be missing if the relation is dropped or truncated later in the same stream. Deliberately NOT folded into S21d: these fail loudly rather than corrupting, none is the gate's current stop, and the change flips several opcodes from "refuse the start" to "silently skip" at once. Resume: route each through `redoExistingHeapPageForBlock` (`recovery.go:3639`), which already carries the invalid-page-table deviation note.
