- [x] **M0131-S21h — the two heap arms that dispatched the opcode but dropped `infobits_set`** (DONE 2026-08-12). S21 asked "is this opcode dispatched?"; this is the first slice to ask whether an arm applies the WHOLE record, and two arms green since B0.2 did not. `replayDecodedXLogHeapDelete` and `replayDecodedXLogHeapUpdate` discarded `infobits_set` / `old_infobits_set`. **F36: that byte is the ONLY place either record says its `xmax` field is a MultiXactId** (`XLHL_XMAX_IS_MULTI`; upstream `fix_infomask_from_infobits`, `heapam_xlog.c`), which PG sets whenever the deleted/updated row was also held by a locker — commonest producer an FK RI check's `FOR KEY SHARE`, i.e. two concurrent inserts of children of one parent. Replayed as a bare xid, the page carried a multi wearing an xid's clothes and every reader judged the delete against an arbitrary transaction id instead of `mvcc.TupleVisible`'s conservative IS_MULTI branch (`visibility.go:126-146`). Strictly a **PG→goopg** defect — goopg's own emit hardcodes `infobits_set = 0` (`pg_assembled_emit.go:174/242/274`), so no goopg-authored record could expose it, which is why every goopg↔goopg replay test stayed green; reachable through a landed path since S21a-2 restores IS_MULTI from `xl_heap_lock`. **F37 (found by enumerating `heap_xlog_delete` rather than chasing only the named bit): four more mutations were missing** — `ClearHotUpdated`, `cmax = FirstCommandId`, `PageSetPrunable`, the self-pointing `t_ctid`, and BOTH flag branches: `XLH_DELETE_IS_SUPER` kills a speculative tuple by clearing **xmin**, not by stamping xmax, so goopg's unconditional stamp left an aborted speculative tuple alive; `XLH_DELETE_IS_PARTITION_MOVE` needs the `t_ctid` sentinel. The update arm also never CLEARED `HOT_UPDATED` on its non-HOT branch. **F38: `PageSetPrunable`'s input is the RECORD's xid, never the stamped xmax** (they differ exactly when xmax is a multi — feeding it in would pin a MultiXactId into `pd_prune_xid`); separately upstream keeps the OLDEST candidate while goopg's producer helpers keep the NEWEST (ledgered, left alone). Landed: `storage.PageApplyHeapDeleteRedo` + `PageApplyHeapUpdateOldRedo` (siblings of S21a-2's `PageApplyHeapLockRedo`, deliberately not the producer `PageStamp*` helpers) + the two arms translating the byte through the shared `xlogHeapLockInfomaskBits`. Delete and update moved together per the sibling rule. Guards `internal/wal/heap_delete_update_infobits_pg_test.go` (multi/keyshare/keys-updated restore, the `infobits_set = 0` control proving goopg↔goopg replay is unchanged, IS_SUPER, IS_PARTITION_MOVE, prunable-from-record-xid, and the update pair over both opcodes); 2 break directions proven fail-when-broken by scripted revert (old `PageSetHeapTupleXmax` call → 4 FAILs; old `PageStamp*` closure → both update subtests FAIL). Gates: `internal/wal` PASS, `internal/storage` PASS, `internal/mvcc` + `internal/executor` PASS, `^TestE2E_` family PASS (109 s), UNITS PASS, `go build ./...` + `go vet` clean, pgbench smoke via the hook. Design `0131-0015` §S21h + F36/F37/F38. 2 ledger rows.
- [ ] **M0131-S24 — MultiXact: durable `pg_multixact` SLRU + `multixact_redo`** (est ~4 loops, **LARGE/RISKY, deferrable — decide explicitly**). RM_MULTIXACT_ID (6) is the **only genuinely unavoidable missing rmgr**: independent of `wal_level` and produced by ordinary concurrency — two sessions taking `FOR SHARE` on one row; `FOR UPDATE` + `FOR KEY SHARE` from different xacts; UPDATE of a row already locked by a live xact; **two concurrent sessions inserting children referencing the same parent row** (FK RI checks take `FOR KEY SHARE` — the commonest real-world source); and VACUUM via both `heap_prepare_freeze_tuple` and `TruncateMultiXact`. goopg has a real in-memory engine (`internal/multixact/multixact.go`, `store.go`) but it is process-local and transient; `pg_multixact/{offsets,members}` are created empty at initdb and never written. Needs a durable offsets+members SLRU modelled on `internal/mvcc/clog_bufferpool.go` — but its 2-bits-per-key locate math does **not** generalise to variable-length member runs, and there is no shared SLRU abstraction (`internal/mvcc/subxact_slru.go` already duplicates the constants; extract one rather than writing a third hand-roll). Then declare `RmgrMultiXact = 6` and port `multixact_redo` (`postgres/src/backend/access/transam/multixact.c:3481`, 4 opcodes), **carrying the `pre_initialized_offsets_page` flag across records** (`multixact.c:383`, `:969`, `:3500`, `:3539`) — skipping it double-zeroes a live offsets page. **Two findings worth ledger rows regardless of whether this lands:** (a) goopg's emit side stamps multi xmax with NO WAL record (`internal/executor/operators_lockrows.go:2040`, `:2126`; `operators_storage.go:3468`, `:3485`) — defensible for lock-only multis, NOT for the updater-bearing producers, so goopg's OWN crash recovery has this defect today; (b) `internal/mvcc/visibility.go:126-146` makes a tuple with an unresolvable multi xmax **invisible**, so a PG-authored multi xmax silently hides rows rather than erroring. **Scoping DECISION (recorded, not omitted): S28's workload is single-session, so S24 is DEFERRED out of M0131** — a single-session PG workload emits no multixact record, so S16-S23 + S25 suffice for S28. The re-arm trigger is executable: S28 ships a third `..._concurrent` variant (two sessions `FOR SHARE` on one row + concurrent FK inserts) carrying `t.Skip("re-arm trigger for M0131-S24")`; un-skipping it re-opens S24. **DEFERRAL CLOSED 2026-08-12 (loop #43): three ledger rows filed under `M0131-S24`** — (1) the S24 deferral itself with its executable re-arm trigger; (2) the producer-side gap, **corrected on verification at HEAD**: finding (a) above is wrong that all four sites emit no record — only the two row-lock sites are `MarkDirtyUnlogged`; `stampUpdaterXmaxNonHOT` / `carryForwardLockersToNewTuple` (now `operators_storage.go:3626`/`:3643`) DO ride a logged delete/update whose `xmax` is `effectiveWriterXID` and whose `infobits_set` is hardcoded 0, so redo clears `HEAP_XMAX_IS_MULTI` and silently drops the preserved lockers — pinned by `internal/wal/multixact_producer_redo_gap_test.go` (2 tests, fail-when-broken); (3) the consumer-side hazard, with the stale "unreachable today" comment at `internal/mvcc/visibility.go:126` corrected in place (it is live after every restart since S20.4). The item stays **unchecked** — ledger row + unchecked item is the only permitted form of deferral. **Original scoping note:** the trigger is concurrency — if the S23 workload is single-session this can be deferred with a clear re-arm trigger; decide explicitly, not by omission. Design: `docs/design/0131-0016-multixact-durable-slru.md` (status: accepted — S24 deferred).
- [x] **M0131-S25 — Index-AM boundary: detect, refuse specifically, ledger** (**DONE 2026-08-12**; design `0131-0015` §S25, guards `internal/wal/index_am_refusal_pg_test.go`, 2 ledger rows). Landed: `RmgrHash`/`RmgrGin`/`RmgrGist` ids (12/13/14 had no names at all), a five-rmid arm returning a refusal that names the access method + OPMASK-ed opcode + LSN + relation, and `preflightIndexAMRecords` scanning `records[startIdx:]` in `ReplayRecordsFrom` so one failed start lists every AM present AND the data directory is left byte-identical (previously the prefix up to the first offending record was applied first — proven by a test that runs the same prefix alone and sees a file appear). Measured sizes correct the estimates below: hash 1158/13, GIN 813/9, GiST **695**/6 (est. said 400), SP-GiST 1009/8, BRIN 367/6; `REGBUF_WILL_INIT` sites are **23**, not 20. Original plan text: Give rmids 12 Hash / 13 Gin / 14 Gist / 16 SPGist / 17 BRIN named arms returning a SPECIFIC error naming the access method and the LSN — the message an operator can act on, unlike `unsupported xlog record rmid=13`. Record that `REGBUF_WILL_INIT` blocks structurally never carry an FPI (20 such sites across the five AMs), which is why no FPI-only shortcut can work for any of them. Design: rides the implementation plan.
- [x] **M0131-S26 — `pd_lsn` completeness audit on logged change paths** (est ~2 loops). PG's crash recovery replays over live data files whose pages may already be AHEAD of the record and skips them via `if (lsn <= PageGetLSN(page)) return BLK_DONE;`. If any goopg **logged** mutation dirties a page without stamping `pd_lsn` to the emitting record's end LSN, PG double-applies — a duplicated row or a btree unique violation, not a crash. Streaming from a base backup barely exercises this; crash recovery does so constantly. Audit every `Pool.MarkDirty*` variant against its `SetLSN` (`internal/storage/bufpool.go:2091-2340`, 9 `SetLSN` sites) and every `logXxx` hook (`internal/initdb/open.go:430-745`); add a debug assertion. Document that `MarkDirtyHint` deliberately does NOT bump `pd_lsn` (`internal/storage/bufpool.go:2053-2057`) and that this exception is PG-faithful because hint bits are recomputable — the question is whether any *logged* path shares it. Design: rides the implementation plan. **↳ AUDIT DONE + the defect it found is FIXED (2026-08-12); item stays UNCHECKED for the debug assertion.** All 23 plain-`MarkDirty` call sites classified (design `docs/design/0131-0033-pdlsn-completeness-audit.md`): 15 are hook-nil fallbacks that emit no record at all, 4 are deliberately unlogged (multixact lock state, `pg_database` in-place writes), and ONE was a real defect — `updateHeapRowCanonicalPG`'s cross-page branch (`operators_storage.go:9306`) stamped the old page's xmax under the same `xl_heap_update` that covers the new page, yet only the new page carried the LSN. Plain `MarkDirty` advances `pd_lsn` only as a side effect of the first-touch FPI, so on an already-imaged (hot catalog) page the stamp was left behind its own record — breaking WAL-before-data (`flushSlots` flushes only to `max(pd_lsn)`) and making a replaying PG re-apply (`lsn <= PageGetLSN(page)`). Fixed with a new `Pool.MarkDirtyCoveredByRecordLocked`, whose contract is the non-obvious part: image first, RAISE `pd_lsn` (never lower — `maybeEmitFPI` may have just stamped a larger one), and do NOT advance `nativeImageLSN`, because no image of that page exists at the record's LSN and moving the watermark would suppress its next first-touch FPI. Guards `internal/storage/pdlsn_secondary_page_test.go` + `internal/executor/pdlsn_cross_page_update_test.go` (fail-when-broken proven: reverting the call site reports `old page pd_lsn = 1001, still behind the covering xl_heap_update at 1002`). **DONE 2026-08-12 (loop #21):** the remaining two pieces landed. (a) The env-gated guard `GOOPG_PDLSN_ASSERT=1` (`internal/storage/pdlsn_assert.go`) reports every plain `MarkDirty` that reaches a WAL-wired pool (`LogPageImage` non-nil) — the observable form of the invariant, since the pool cannot see whether its caller emitted a record — one line per caller PC, report-only, off by default. The class-B sites moved to a new `Pool.MarkDirtyUnlogged(s, reason)` (2× multixact lock stamp, `pg_database` datconnlimit + datfrozenxid) so the report is only the sites that still need classifying. Guards `internal/storage/pdlsn_assert_test.go` (fires for plain MarkDirty under a wired pool; silent for the stamping/unlogged/hint variants; silent when WAL is unwired; off by default). Empirically: pgbench -i -s2 + 20s/4 clients (12 783 txns) + VACUUM + CREATE INDEX + a 20 000-row UPDATE on a capped server → **zero reports**, and arming was PROVEN rather than assumed (a temporary probe in `MarkDirtyLogicalChange` produced `PDLSN-UNSTAMPED … site=…markHeapInsertDirty (operators_storage.go:9387)`; both probes reverted). (b) The `logXxx`-hook audit: five hooks register >1 block (btree split/newroot/unlink/markhalfdead, heap update) and on the pd_lsn axis they are CLEAN — every named block is stamped with the record's LSN by its caller. It did surface an adjacent-axis gap, filed as S26b below. Ledger 2026-08-12 ×3.
- [x] **M0131-S26b — image-less secondary blocks must not advance the FPI watermark** (est ~1-2 loops; found by S26's hook audit 2026-08-12). `MarkDirtyWithLSNLocked` advances `nativeImageLSN`, and `needsImage` is `nativeImageLSN <= redoRecPtr`, so stamping a page that way asserts "an image of this page exists in WAL at that LSN" and suppresses its first-touch FPI for the rest of the checkpoint epoch. True for a block the record carries as an image or rebuilds wholesale (WILL_INIT); FALSE for the blocks upstream registers plainly and describes only via main data — `EncodeBtreeSplitPG`'s sibling (block 2) and child (block 3) carry neither image nor data yet `_bt_split` stamps them (`internal/access/btree/btree.go:3055,3058`), `btree_vacuum.go`'s unlink siblings are the same shape (its own comment says it "skips the per-epoch FPI path"), and split's LEFT page joins them in the incremental form of M0130-S11.5b-2, which reconstructs from the ON-DISK pre-page. Upstream PG registers all of these as ordinary buffers, i.e. takes an FPI when it is the first modification since the checkpoint. Exposure is torn-write-only — which is exactly what full-page writes exist for. Fix: use `MarkDirtyCoveredByRecordLocked` (image first, raise pd_lsn, leave the watermark) at those sites; it changes WAL volume on the btree-split hot path, so it needs the pgbench + tpch-spotcheck gates and a WAL-volume before/after number. Design: `docs/design/0131-0033-pdlsn-completeness-audit.md` §Hook audit. **↳ DONE 2026-08-12** (design `docs/design/0131-0034-fpi-watermark-bare-blocks.md`). Six bare-block sites now use `MarkDirtyCoveredByRecordLocked`: split's sibling (blk 2) + incomplete-split child (blk 3) + LEFT page in the incremental form (`btree.go`), half-dead's PARENT (blk 1 — one the S26 audit's list had missed, same shape), and unlink's two siblings (`btree_vacuum.go`). The left page asks the encoder's own predicate rather than re-deriving it: `btree.SplitLeftIsIncremental` is now the single decision `EncodeBtreeSplitPG` and `splitPage` share. `WILL_INIT`/image blocks keep `MarkDirtyWithLSNLocked`. Guard `TestSplitBareBlocksKeepFirstTouchFPI` (reverting the sibling site fails it). WAL-volume A/B (`analysis/s26b-walvolume.sh` + `pg_waldump --stats`, 2 pairs): 9,337,546 / 9,280,089 then 9,344,350 / 9,342,632 total bytes — the between-arm difference is under the same-binary run-to-run spread, because a page reached as a split sibling is nearly always one the backend also mutated directly this epoch. Gates: btree/wal/storage packages, units pre-commit, tpch-spotcheck Q12=2/Q13=35 PASS, pgbench smoke via the hook.
- [x] **M0131-S27 — Forward crash E2E (+ stale pidfile, torn contrecord)** (est ~3 loops). New `internal/testport/e2e_pg_crashstart_on_goopgdata_test.go`, sibling of S4's test: `goopg init` → start → workload with a known committed/uncommitted split → force one online checkpoint (`goopg checkpoint`) so `state` is `DB_IN_PRODUCTION` and there is a real tail past redo → more committed work → **`kill -9` via `cluster.Kill()`** (`internal/testutil/cluster/cluster.go:303-333` — it SIGKILLs the process GROUP by pgid from the cmd handle, no PID-file read, and satisfies the never-`pkill -f` rule) → assert `pg_control.State == DB_IN_PRODUCTION` → `postgres -D <same dir>` → assert the PG log has `redo starts at` and `redo done at`, **no** PANIC/FATAL, and that any `invalid record length`/`invalid magic number` line is the LAST WAL complaint (benign end-of-WAL per the taxonomy in `0131-0012`) → assert every committed row incl. post-checkpoint is visible, uncommitted ones are not, and aggregates match values captured before the kill. Sub-items: **stale `postmaster.pid`** — a SIGKILL leaves it (`RemovePIDFile` runs only on the clean path, `internal/server/server.go:808-815`) and PG's `CreateLockFile` (`postgres/src/backend/utils/init/miscinit.c:1300-1425`) reads line 0 as a pid and **FATALs `lock file "…" already exists`** if `kill(pid,0)` succeeds, so this fails NON-DETERMINISTICALLY whenever the dead PID has been recycled; otherwise it unlinks and proceeds (goopg's 5-line file has no line 6 so the shmem check is skipped). Either make the file PG-shaped or remove it on start; until then the test deletes it as a documented handover step. **Torn contrecord** — kill during a large `INSERT … SELECT` so the tail ends inside a multi-page record; assert PG logs `there is no contrecord flag at %X/%X` (or `invalid contrecord length`) then `successfully skipped missing contrecord at %X/%X` after writing its `XLOG_OVERWRITE_CONTRECORD`; **this is the one crash-only WAL-format path the standby lane structurally cannot reach** (`xlogrecovery.c:3188-3193` suppresses it under `ArchiveRecoveryRequested`). **Honesty note for the doc: a `kill -9` does NOT exercise torn PAGES** — writes reach the page cache atomically from a reader's perspective; torn-page coverage needs a machine-level crash or a half-page-write fault injector, so this test must not be cited as FPI coverage. Design: `docs/design/0131-0016-crash-interchange-e2e.md`. **S27 MAIN VARIANT DONE 2026-08-12** — `internal/testport/e2e_pg_crashstart_on_goopgdata_test.go` runs green: the committed/uncommitted split (the open transaction is held by a background `psql` parked on `pg_sleep`, since `psql -c` exits and rolls back), the online checkpoint plus a post-checkpoint tail, `cluster.Kill()`, the `DB_IN_PRODUCTION` assertion, the stale-`postmaster.pid` handover (asserted PRESENT then removed, so a future goopg-side cleanup fails this test rather than silently passing), the `redo starts at`/`redo done at`/no-PANIC/benign-end-of-WAL log contract, row equality against goopg's own pre-crash answers, and the uncommitted rows' absence. **It found two production defects, both fixed in the same commit:** (1) `updateOp` dereferenced a nil `IndexScan.Key` for a ranged `UPDATE` on an indexed column (`WHERE id BETWEEN a AND b` plans as an IndexScan with `Key==nil` + LowKey/HighKey) and panicked the backend goroutine — the fast path now requires an equality `Key`, guard `internal/executor/update_range_index_scan_test.go`; (2) goopg's `xl_heap_prune` omitted **`XLHP_CLEANUP_LOCK`**, so upstream computed `lp_truncate_only=true` and an assert-enabled PG 18.3 **aborted the startup process** at `pruneheap.c:1677` (`ItemIdIsDead(lp) && !ItemIdHasStorage(lp)`), leaving the cluster unstartable — a standby-lane blind spot, because the flag only matters when PG replays a prune record it did not write. **TORN-CONTRECORD VARIANT DONE 2026-08-12** — `internal/testport/e2e_pg_torn_contrecord_test.go` (`TestE2E_PGCrashStartOnGoopgTornContrecord`) runs green. The earlier "needs the kill timed against WAL page boundaries" reading was wrong and would only have made the test flaky: goopg's ring drain is BYTE-granular (`state.drainBufferBytes`, `internal/wal/writer.go` — `n` bytes off the ring head, no record alignment), so a SIGKILL legitimately ends the segment at an arbitrary offset. The test does a REAL `cluster.Kill()` and then applies the lost ring remainder deterministically: it finds the last valid page carrying `XLP_FIRST_IS_CONTRECORD` (never page 0 — that would take the long header's cross-check with it) and zeroes from there to the end of the segment; observed loss is one record. Upstream's two contrecord LOG lines are NOT assertable in this lane — `there is no contrecord flag` is pre-empted by page-header validation on the zeroed continuation page (`invalid magic number`, benign end-of-WAL, still reaching xlogreader.c's `assembled` exit), and `successfully skipped missing contrecord` needs a pass that REPLAYS the overwrite record, which crash recovery's end-of-recovery checkpoint puts the redo point past. So the test asserts the on-disk consequence instead, which is stronger: the page at the missing continuation's address carries `XLP_FIRST_IS_OVERWRITE_CONTRECORD` (and not `XLP_FIRST_IS_CONTRECORD`) with `xlp_pageaddr` unchanged, and `pg_waldump` reports the `OVERWRITE_CONTRECORD` record there — a bit upstream sets only from `CreateOverwriteContrecordRecord`. Verified to bite (disable the cut → `xlp_info=0x0001` → FAIL). No defect found by this variant. Deferred: the PITR/archive-replay lane that would exercise the two LOG lines — ledger row 2026-08-12.
- [x] **M0131-S32 — a single-session UPDATE loop on one row silently stops applying after 64 updates** — **FIXED 2026-08-12 (loop #144)** (filed 2026-08-12 loop #143; **wrong-answer class, HIGHEST-SEVERITY OPEN BUG in M0131 — it needs NO concurrency, NO crash and no special config**). Design `docs/design/0131-0025-single-row-update-stall.md`; repro `bash analysis/hotstall.sh` (~40 s, self-contained cluster, gate = `OVERALL: PASS`). `CREATE TABLE t(id int primary key, v bigint); INSERT INTO t VALUES (1,0);` then 300 autocommit `UPDATE t SET v=v+1 WHERE id=1` in ONE session: `v` advances 1..64 and then **freezes at 64 forever**, while every one of the remaining 236 statements still reports `UPDATE 1` and commits. No error, `count(*)` stays 1, server log silent — the only observable is a wrong value. The stall point 64 is stable across runs, so this reads as a capacity boundary, not a race. Scaling: clients 1/2/4/8 -> lost 6589/1/3599/10520 of the committed updates; the single-client row is the important one. **Rides along:** after the stall the index read `WHERE id=1` returns NOTHING while the seq read `WHERE id+0=1` returns the stale row — the S31 signature (`0131-0019`) reappearing, so `hotstall.sh` reports both reads and the two must never be conflated. **Ruled out by inspection, do not re-test:** `tryApplyHOTUpdate` (`internal/executor/operators_storage.go:3555`) DOES re-read the old tuple under the exclusive content lock and call `isConcurrentlyUpdated` before stamping; `isConcurrentlyUpdated` (`:3180`) returns true for a *committed* foreign xmax as well as an in-progress one (it excludes only aborted); and the `!used` fallback runs a full EPQ retry loop with RC chain-following (`:4328`). None can fire with one client. **Next step (cheapest decisive probe):** put an unconditional counter on every `used == false` return site in `tryApplyHOTUpdate` and on entry to the `!used` fallback, then run `hotstall.sh` — prediction is updates 1-64 take the HOT path and 65+ take the fallback; if the fallback is never entered, the stall is inside `tryApplyHOTUpdate`'s own `oldItem.Flags != storage.ItemIDNormal` skip return (`:3625`, whose comment 'Caller treats (false, nil) as skip this row' is stale — the caller actually falls through to the non-HOT path). Independently of the cause, the statement reports `UPDATE 1` while writing nothing, so the row count is coming from the planned row set rather than from rows actually written; upstream `heap_update` (`postgres/src/backend/access/heap/heapam.c`) has no 'success, nothing written' outcome at all. Then look at why 64 versions fill an 8 KiB page permanently, i.e. whether `PagePruneOpt` reclaims the dead HOT versions. **Blast radius:** invalidates `pgbench_tellers`/`pgbench_branches` as measurement signals in every S30 probe (they disagree with `sum(delta)` on a CLEAN no-crash cluster: -90351 / -182750 vs -54526) and plausibly contributes to S30's crash-probe divergences. **ROOT CAUSE + FIX (loop #144):** every HOT/CTID chain walker bounded its loop with a hand-written `const maxChain = 64` — `followHOTChain`, `followHOTChainNoCopy`, `heapChainDeadToAll` (`internal/executor/operators_index.go`), `epqChainCheckMovedPartition`, `epqFollowChainFull` (`internal/executor/operators_storage.go`), `pruneChainTip` (`internal/storage/prune.go`). One HOT update = one chain link, so version 65 sat at depth 65 and the walk gave up before reaching it: the index scan driving the UPDATE found no live tuple, wrote NOTHING, and still reported `UPDATE 1` (the count comes from the planned row set); `WHERE id=1` returned nothing while the seq read returned version 64 with an unstamped xmax — so the "S31 index-unreachable signature" here was a CONSEQUENCE of the same truncation, not a second defect; and `pruneChainTip` gave up at the same depth, never reclaiming the dead versions, which is what made the boundary look like page capacity. PG has no such cap (`heap_hot_search_buffer` loops to `at_chain_end`; `heap_prune_chain` sizes `chainitems[]` by `MaxHeapTuplesPerPage`) — the real bound is that a HOT chain visits DISTINCT SLOTS OF ONE PAGE, so exceeding `MaxHeapTuplesPerPage` (291 at 8 KiB) proves a cycle. Fix: new PG-faithful `storage.MaxHeapTuplesPerPage` used by all four single-page walkers; the two cross-page t_ctid walkers use an explicit `maxCTIDChainWalk = 1<<20` corruption backstop (PG's `heap_get_latest_tid` is uncapped). Gates: `analysis/hotstall.sh` PASS at N=300 AND N=2000; new `TestHOTUpdateChainBeyond64Versions` reads back through the index after each of 120 updates and fails at exactly "after update 65: v=64" when the bound is restored to 64; `TestMaxHeapTuplesPerPagePGParity` pins 291; units precommit PASS; tpch-spotcheck PASS (Q12=2, Q13=35).
- [x] **M0131-S32.1 — under concurrency the SAME hot row is still mis-updated, in BOTH directions** — **INDEX ARM FIXED 2026-08-12 (loop #145)**; SeqScan arm continues as **M0131-S32.2**. Design `docs/design/0131-0026-concurrent-hot-row-lost-updates.md`; minimal repro/gate `analysis/concurrent-hotrow.sh` (C sessions x N blind `v=v+1` increments on ROWS rows; invariant `sum(v) == C*N`, exact and arithmetic; `NOIDX=1` switches IndexScan->SeqScan). **ROOT CAUSE A (lost updates, the tellers direction):** the EvalPlanQual retry loop treated a failed chain-follow (`if !chainFound { epqSkip = true; break }`) as "the row is gone" and SKIPPED it silently while the statement still reported `UPDATE 1`. Under contention the chain tip is routinely owned by a transaction that has NOT committed yet — session D waits for A, refreshes its snapshot, walks to A's new version, and B has already replaced it with an uncommitted one — so the tail is invisible and D's write is dropped. Env-gated counters (`internal/executor/s321_probe.go`, `GOOPG_S321_PROBE=1`) pinned it exactly: `epq_chain_notfound=1112` of 1946 attempts, `hot_applied=1`. PG never skips: `ExecUpdate` loops on `TM_Updated` and `heap_lock_tuple` blocks on the in-progress updater (`XactLockTableWait`) before re-fetching. Fix = `epqChainPendingWriter` walks the t_ctid chain to its tail, classifies the tail's xmin via `epqXmaxSettled` (authoritative, not snapshot membership), and both EPQ loops now `epqWait` on a still-in-flight writer + refresh the RC snapshot + retry instead of skipping (bounded by `maxEPQRetriesRC`). **ROOT CAUSE B (over-application, the branches direction), exposed by making the loop retry:** after a chain-follow two `pending` entries of the SAME statement converge on one physical tuple; `isConcurrentlyUpdated` ignores our own xmax, so the second entry re-stamped a tuple this transaction had already killed and inserted a SECOND live version — 1720 live rows in a one-row table. Fix = PG's `TM_SelfModified` skip at the top of both EPQ retry loops (the index arm's AI-20260810-011258-006 dedupe only covers duplicates within one scan, before any chain-follow). **Gates:** `analysis/concurrent-hotrow.sh` exact 1600/1600 at ROWS=1 and ROWS=5, with and without BEGIN/COMMIT (was 470/1600); `RUNS=1 LOADSEC=30 analysis/atomicity-nocrash-control.sh` — `sum(tbalance)` now EXACT (-68881, was -14938 under-applied) and `sum(bbalance)` -54588 (was -309543, ~10x over) so only the SeqScan table still diverges; units precommit PASS; tpch-spotcheck RESULT=PASS (Q12=2, Q13=35).
- [x] **M0131-S32.2 — the SeqScan-driven UPDATE arm still loses concurrent updates in the SCAN phase** — **FIXED 2026-08-12 (loop #146)**; residual pgbench-only divergence continues as M0131-S32.3. Design `docs/design/0131-0026-concurrent-hot-row-lost-updates.md` §7. Both defects live in the scan-phase EPQ loop inside `updateOp.Next`'s `scanMatching` callback — a loop the S32.1 write-phase fixes never touched. **(C)** it dropped the row with the same not-found-means-deleted skip S32.1 fixed one loop later (`return nil // row deleted by concurrent tx`), so the row never entered `pending` and the statement reported `UPDATE 0` while no write-phase counter fired — which is why the loss read as a scan/visibility problem from outside. Fix = the same `epqChainPendingWriter` + `epqWait` + retry (`scan_epq_notfound` 20708 -> 4852). **(D)** `epqWait` refreshes `ctx.Snap`, and `scanMatching` decides tuple visibility from that SAME snapshot: refreshing it mid-scan let the rest of the scan see versions committed after statement start, so the same logical row was handed to the callback twice (once as the statement-start version, once as another session's committed successor). Both entries were written, and because they are two DIFFERENT physical tuples the S32.1 `TM_SelfModified` guard (keyed on our own xmax) could not fire — a one-row table ended with 130 live rows, `sum(v)=96435` instead of 1600. This is the true `pgbench_branches` over-application mechanism. Upstream holds the scan snapshot fixed for the whole statement and confines the EPQ re-fetch to the updated tuple (`postgres/src/backend/executor/execMain.c` `EvalPlanQual*`); fix = save/restore `ctx.Snap` around the loop. Both defects are required: C alone worsens the fork (166 live rows), D alone leaves the losses. The A/B that attributed them is the new `GOOPG_S322_NOWAIT=1` kill switch (scan-phase wait only). **Gates:** `analysis/concurrent-hotrow.sh` exact 1600/1600 with `count(*)` correct and ZERO client errors in all four configs (NOIDX ROWS=1/5, index arm ROWS=1/5, with and without BEGIN/COMMIT); `RUNS=1 LOADSEC=30 analysis/atomicity-nocrash-control.sh` — `sum(abalance)`/`sum(tbalance)` exact (-969135), `sum(bbalance)` -966698 (-0.25%, was -21%); units precommit PASS; tpch-spotcheck RESULT=PASS (Q12=2, Q13=35).
- [x] **M0131-S32.3 — the residual `pgbench_branches` divergence was a stale-snapshot commit window, not a percentage** — **FIXED 2026-08-12 (loop #147); the M0131-S32 series is CLOSED.** Design `docs/design/0131-0026-concurrent-hot-row-lost-updates.md` §8. **The framing was wrong first.** S32.2 handed this over as "0.25% of `pgbench_branches`" (`sum(bbalance)` -966698 vs `sum(delta)` -969135). With pgbench's `:delta` uniform on +/-5000, `sum(delta)` is itself a random walk of sd ~592000 over 42000 transactions, so ONE mis-applied update moves that difference by up to 5000: the 2437 gap was a single update, not 0.25% of them. Re-running a branches-only pgbench script with the delta pinned to 1 made it countable — 42487 transactions, `bbal=42481` vs `hist=42487` = **6 lost increments, and the write-phase probe read `epq_chain_notfound=6` exactly**. That also retired this task's own starting hypothesis: the 4-table BEGIN/COMMIT is NOT required (one hot UPDATE + one INSERT reproduces it), and the harness extension written to test it — `MULTI=1` in `analysis/concurrent-hotrow.sh`, TPC-B statement order with a wide uncontended table, a read-back SELECT and a history INSERT — passes exactly (3200/3200), which is what ruled the transaction shape out. **Root cause E:** both chain-follow helpers locate "the latest version" by SNAPSHOT visibility (`epqFollowHOT` -> `followHOTChain(ctx.Snap)`, `epqFollowChainFull` -> `mvcc.TupleVisible`). The EPQ loop refreshes `ctx.Snap` and then walks the chain; a writer that commits in that window leaves a version live on the page but OUTSIDE our snapshot, so chain-follow reports not-found while `epqChainPendingWriter` correctly reports nobody in flight (the writer has committed — neither in-flight nor aborted) and the caller takes the silent skip: the write is dropped while the statement still reports `UPDATE 1`. Same silent-skip family as defects A and C, one commit-window later. Upstream never uses a snapshot to find the latest version: `heap_lock_tuple` follows `t_ctid` to the version whose xmax is invalid/aborted whatever its xmin commit time (`postgres/src/backend/access/heap/heapam.c`, `EvalPlanQualFetch` in `executor/execMain.c`); only the qual is re-evaluated, against the original snapshot. **Fix:** new `epqChainTailLiveButUnseen` partitions the remaining not-found cases against `epqChainPendingWriter` — true only when the tail's xmin COMMITTED, its xmax is not a committed delete, and `mvcc.TupleVisible` still says no — and all three EPQ sites (index-arm write phase, seq-arm write phase, seq-arm scan phase, per Hard-won Rule #2) refresh via `epqRefreshSnapForRetry` and take another lap instead of skipping. The predicate is deliberately narrow so the retry cannot livelock: false once the version IS visible (the next lap terminates), false for a genuinely deleted row, false for an in-flight writer. **Gates:** `RUNS=1 LOADSEC=30 analysis/atomicity-nocrash-control.sh` **OVERALL: PASS** — accounts, tellers AND branches all exactly `sum(history.delta)`=133157 at LIVE and again after RESTART (first green since the tellers/branches arm was added); branches-only delta-pinned probe exact (42600=42600, `epq_chain_notfound=0`); `analysis/concurrent-hotrow.sh` exact 1600/1600 in six configurations (NOIDX ROWS=1/5, index arm ROWS=1/5, TXN=1, MULTI=1); new `TestEPQChainTailLiveButUnseen` PASS; units precommit PASS; `scripts/tpch-spotcheck.sh` RESULT=PASS (Q12=2, Q13=35). One ledger row filed for retiring `s321_probe.go` + the two kill switches.
- [x] **M0131-S31 — indexed equality lookup silently returns 0 rows for updated rows** (est ~2-3 loops; **wrong-answer class, HIGHEST-SEVERITY OPEN BUG — diagnosed 2026-08-11, supersedes S30's root-cause claim**). No crash, no concurrency, no special config: `analysis/idxprobe.sh` (20 s, single client, 20000-row `t(id int primary key, v int)`, `UPDATE t SET v=v+1 WHERE id=:id`) leaves the heap perfect — 20000 rows, 20000 distinct ids — while **9286 ids are unreachable by index** (4 clients: 5576). `WHERE id=11` returns nothing; `WHERE id+0=11` returns the row. An ordered range scan **skips the keys outright** (`WHERE id>=10 ORDER BY id LIMIT 5` → `14,15,17,18,20`), proving the entries are ABSENT from the tree rather than passed over by an early-stopping equality scan. Separately `REINDEX INDEX t_pkey` **fails** with `could not create unique index` — the index builder is handed superseded row versions that MVCC correctly hides, a second distinct defect (and the real grain of truth behind S30's "duplicated rows"). **Four mechanisms ruled out by experiment, do not re-test them:** (1) the non-atomic non-HOT WAL path — the loss needs no crash; (2) the swallowed error at `internal/executor/operators_storage.go:7267` (`_ = tree.Insert(key, ptr)`) — instrumented, printed ZERO lines over a full failing run (still fix the discarded error as robustness, but it is not this bug); (3) unique-key rejection in the btree — `BTree.Insert` (`internal/access/btree/btree.go:2543`) has no uniqueness check at all; (4) the cached-rightmost-leaf fast path (`tryInsertOnCachedRightmost`) — forced off with `false &&`, reproduced unchanged. **Next step (cheapest decisive probe):** the non-HOT path calls `maintainUniqueIndexesForInsert` only under `if destPart != nil {...} else if pu.scanTbl != nil {...}` (`operators_storage.go:5304-5311`) — if both are nil the new version gets NO index entry, which explains every observation INCLUDING the silent instrumentation (Insert never called, rather than succeeding). Put an unconditional print at the CALL SITE and compare reach-count against update-count. Second candidate: `insertIntoBlock`'s split path (weaker — losses are interleaved, 10-13 gone but 14,15 present). Design: `docs/design/0131-0019-index-entry-loss-on-update.md`. **RESOLVED 2026-08-11.** Root cause: `pagePruneCore` (`internal/storage/prune.go`) skipped every non-`ItemIDNormal` line pointer, so an `ItemIDRedirect` root produced by an EARLIER prune was never re-pointed — while the same pass reclaimed the (dead, HEAP_ONLY) slot that redirect addressed. The chain is severed: the btree entry resolves to an LP_UNUSED slot, the row vanishes from every index scan and a seq scan still returns it. Two HOT updates of one row on a page that prunes are sufficient; upstream `heap_prune_chain` starts from redirected roots for exactly this reason. Fixing it exposed a second defect in `pruneChainTip`: it followed `t_ctid.Offset` past a dead member whose update was NON-HOT, whose successor lives on another block — the offset read as a local slot could make the redirect resolve to a FOREIGN row (`WHERE id=104` returned two rows). Both arms fixed, so `PagePruneOpt` and `PageVacuumPrune` share the repair. Verified: `analysis/idxprobe.sh` 5576→0 unreachable, `analysis/idxprobe3.sh` 4335→0, `REINDEX INDEX t_pkey` now OK (it was the same defect, not a separate one). Regression tests `TestPagePruneOptRepointsExistingRedirect` / `TestPagePruneOptStopsAtNonHOTChainEnd` (`internal/storage/prune_test.go`) fail on the pre-fix file.

The loss is crash-recovery-only.
    - [x] **S30.1 — replay mistakes a segment's unusable tail for end-of-WAL** (the row-loss defect; 4416 of 500000 rows lost in one run). Replay stopped at `lsn=134217721` = `0x7FFFFF9`, seven bytes short of the 128 MiB segment mark, with `reason="invalid record header" detail="padding bytes nonzero (0xff 0x07)"`, and **discarded the entire WAL tail**. When the space left in a page/segment cannot hold a record header, PG continues at the next page after its header instead of parsing the leftover bytes; a RECYCLED segment makes those bytes non-zero, which is what trips this. Same family as `M0131-S19`'s page-header work (commit 15e73de3). **RESOLVED 2026-08-11 (loop #139)** — `docs/design/0131-0022`. Confirmed exactly as filed: when the space left before a segment boundary is smaller than one 24-byte header, `reserveEmittedAndPublish` cannot fit its `XLOG_NOOP` pad and leaves the bytes unwritten (`gapLen < xlogMinimumRecordSize`); `readAllPageAware` decoded them as a record header, which `extractRecordBytes` completed from the NEXT segment's first record (the intervening page header is skipped), so validation failed and the walk called it end-of-WAL and discarded every committed record behind it. Fix: skip a sub-header segment tail — but only when the bytes are NOT a record, decided by new `recordStartsAt` (header decode + `xl_tot_len` bounds + `xl_crc` over the reassembled record). **The unconditional skip is wrong and was measured wrong:** `state.appendPGCompat`'s Path A (`Config.WALBuffers == 0`, oversized record, ring drain) emits at the tracker cursor through `emitWithPageHeaders` with NO boundary re-land, so it really does write records that start in a segment's last 8 bytes and straddle — the pre-existing reader replays those correctly, and an offset-only skip dropped them and everything behind them. Guards in `internal/wal/reader_segment_tail_gap_test.go` (fixtures size each payload from the writer's own `predictXLogRecordLen`/`predictEmittedSize`, and select the writer path via `Config.WALBuffers`): `TestReadAllSkipsShortSegmentTailGap` (gap 8/16), `...WithStaleBytes` (gap overwritten with 0xff — the recycled-segment shape of the measured failure) and `TestReadAllKeepsRecordStraddlingSegmentBoundary`; validated both ways (skip disabled -> the three gap tests fail with the production signature `64 records read`; skip unconditional -> the straddle test fails). Deferred, ledger 2026-08-11: the two append paths disagree about segment-boundary layout at all, which is why the reader must tolerate both.

Guard `TestReplayCLogFromWAL_AdvancesPastInFlightXID` (validated both directions). After the fix the reuse window is gone (60422 vs `nextXid` 60423) and the failure signature CHANGED from bidirectional to unidirectional — the remainder is S30.8 below.

Decoding `pg_xact` showed recovery was RIGHT (40267 COMMIT, exactly 13 ABORT, contiguous at the top of the range): the reader ignored it. Two defects, both required. (1) `Snapshot.SeesCommittedXID` returned committed for any `xid < Xmin` WITHOUT consulting the CLOG — and `initdb.Open` advances NextXID past every replayed XID, so the first post-restart snapshot has Xmin above the whole aborted range; PG's `HeapTupleSatisfiesMVCC` always resolves a non-hinted xmin through `TransactionIdDidCommit` and relies on the hint bits, not on the snapshot xmin, to stay off the hot path.     - [x] **S30.9 — "concurrent committed INSERTs are silently lost on a LIVE server": EVIDENCE RETRACTED 2026-08-12 (loop #142), NOT a defect** (filed 2026-08-11 loop #141; `docs/design/0131-0024` §7 carries the full retraction, ledger row 2026-08-12). The reported 7.9% loss (`rows=75922 want=80000`) was a **harness artefact**, not data loss. All three probe scripts backgrounded the server with `&` and then called a bare `wait`, which waits on ALL background jobs — including the server, which never exits. Whether that call was a barrier at all depended on whether `scripts/goopg-test-run.sh` stayed in its job (→ `wait` blocks forever; observed twice this loop as a fake multi-hour hang while the load had finished in under a minute) or detached into a systemd scope (→ `wait` returns while clients are STILL INSERTING, so the script counts a partially-loaded table). The original numbers were internally inconsistent in exactly that way: `rows=75922` with `heap_missing=6328` implies 2250 duplicate rows under a PRIMARY KEY, which no page mechanism explains but two queries sampling a growing table do. Second measurement defect: `heap_missing`/`index_unreachable` came from a `NOT EXISTS (… WHERE b.id+0 = g)` anti-join over `generate_series` that does not finish at WANT=80000 (35 min in one post-mortem) and returned exactly `6328` on two runs whose `count(*)` shortfalls differed. **Landed:** `wait "${CLIENT_PIDS[@]}"` in all three harnesses; both shortfalls now computed by dumping ids and diffing with `comm` in the shell (terminates, and every number is re-checkable from `$W`); new `heap_dupes` assertion so "lost" can never again be conflated with "lost AND duplicated"; `analysis/lostrows-ctiddump.sh` + `analysis/lostrows-postmortem.sh` added as evidence collectors. **Verified:** 5 runs at the exact failing scale (80000 rows, 8 clients) all clean — `rows=80000 heap_missing=0 index_unreachable=0 heap_dupes=0`, `OVERALL: PASS` ×3 plus two independently shell-diffed runs. Also settled the planned §5 step 1 for free: the probe cluster boots `shared_buffers_slots=16384` against a ~1000-page working set, so eviction never happened, and the failing run logged one checkpoint — at startup, before the load. **Residual (ledger row 2026-08-12):** this proves the probe was broken, NOT that goopg is free of concurrent-insert loss; the hardened script is still only a manual shell gate and should be promoted to a Go test before any S30 conclusion leans on it.
    - [x] **S30.6 — the two WAL append paths disagree about segment-boundary layout** (found 2026-08-11 by S30.1; ledger row same date). `appendPGCompat` Path B (stripe, production) re-lands a crossing record at the segment boundary and pads the gap; Path A (`Config.WALBuffers == 0`, oversized record, ring drain) encodes at the tracker cursor and emits through `emitWithPageHeaders` with no re-land, so it writes records that straddle the boundary. goopg's WAL byte layout is therefore writer-path-dependent, and every consumer must tolerate both (the S30.1 reader fix does, via a CRC check). Upstream has one rule — `ReserveXLogInsertLocation` reserves in usable-byte space (`postgres/src/backend/access/transam/xlog.c`). Resume: `internal/wal/writer.go` `state.appendPGCompat` Path A (~:1696-1708), route the direct write through the same crossing prediction, with a cross-path parity test that both paths produce byte-identical layout for the same append sequence. **LANDED 2026-08-12 (loop #151); design `docs/design/0131-0030-wal-append-path-segment-boundary-parity.md`.** Path A now runs the same crossing prediction as Path B: predict the emitted size at the cursor (`predictEmittedSize(paddedLen, …)`), and on a crossing compose the gap exactly as `emitSegmentPad` does (pad RECORD = gap minus `pageHeaderBytesIn` per 0131-0028, emitted through `emitWithPageHeaders`), stamp the re-landed record's `xl_prev` with the pad's start LSN (matching `reserveEmittedAndPublish`'s `t.prev = startCandidate`), and write pad+record as ONE contiguous `writeAt` so a crash cannot make the record durable without its pad. A record whose emission exceeds a whole segment is left alone (re-landing would only straddle the NEXT boundary — the bound Path B panics on). **Porting the rule exposed a second defect in its shared half:** a gap too small for a well-formed record was left UNWRITTEN ("the bytes are zeros in a preallocated segment"), which with `Config.Preallocate` OFF leaves the segment file SHORT — replay stops at the short read and discards everything behind it (measured while porting: `TestReopenAfterSegmentStraddlingRecord/payload72` read 628 of 2068 records). `emitSegmentPad` now ZERO-FILLS such a gap and returns `padded=false`; `insertPosTracker.onCrossSegment` gained that boolean return so `reserveEmittedAndPublish` advances `prev` only for a real pad record. Zero-filling is PG-faithful: `AdvanceXLInsertBuffer` zeroes a WAL page before insertion, so an unusable page tail is durable zeros. Guard `TestAppendPathsAgreeOnSegmentBoundaryLayout` (`internal/wal/writer_segment_cross_parity_test.go`): the same payload sequence through both paths must produce BYTE-IDENTICAL segment files, at leads 8/16 (zero-filled gaps), 64 (pad) and 12288 (pad spanning a page boundary); negative control fails all four with the first differing byte at the gap. Consumer-visible change: Path A streams now carry an `XLOG_NOOP` pad per padded crossing instead of a straddling record — record-counting tests updated (`withoutSegmentPads`), and `TestReadAllKeepsRecordStraddlingSegmentBoundary`'s fixture is now hand-built since no writer produces that shape any more (the reader keeps the tolerance for older on-disk streams). Gates: `go test ./internal/wal/` + `-race`, `./internal/initdb/ ./internal/storage/`, units precommit, `RUNS=2 crashprobe30` **OVERALL: PASS** (both runs exact on the atomicity invariant). Residual (ledger 2026-08-12): the page-boundary pad `xl_prev` quirk is reproduced deliberately in Path A to keep the two paths byte-identical.
    - [x] **S30.1b — the `invalid page header` sibling of S30.1's early end-of-WAL** (found 2026-08-12 loop #148 by the S30.7 verification run). `crashprobe30` run 2 stopped replay with `reason="invalid page header" detail="wal: invalid page header: magic=0x0020 want 0xd118" lsn=117432305 stream_offset=100655088` and lost 6762 rows (493238/500000). S30.1/`0131-0022` fixed the `padding bytes nonzero` variant of the same walk; this is a distinct variant — a page whose header magic is 0x0020, i.e. the walk landed mid-page or on an unwritten page. Repro: `RUNS=3 bash analysis/crashprobe30.sh` (~1 in 2 runs); the failing cluster is preserved at `/tmp/crashprobe30/run2`. Resume from the page-header validation in the replay walk (`internal/wal/recovery.go`) and the stripe writer's page-boundary accounting, exactly as 0131-0022 did. **ROOT-CAUSED AND FIXED 2026-08-12 (loop #149); design `docs/design/0131-0028-segment-pad-clobbers-page-header.md`.** Not a reader defect at all — the WRITER produced a stream no reader should accept. When a stripe reservation would straddle a segment boundary, `reserveEmittedAndPublish` re-lands it at the boundary and `emitSegmentPad` fills the gap `[curr, boundary)` with an `XLOG_NOOP`. The gap is a range of STREAM bytes, and in page-header mode every 8 KiB boundary inside it holds a page header — but the composer built a `boundary-gapStart`-byte RECORD and wrote it verbatim at `gapStart`, so a gap spanning a page boundary (a) overwrote the page-header slot with record bytes and (b) overran the boundary by the same amount, because the reader reassembles a record by skipping page headers. The measured `magic=0x0020` is the pad's own `xl_info`/`xl_rmid` pair (`0x20`=XLOG_NOOP, `0`=RM_XLOG_ID) — i.e. pad byte 16 — and `117432304` is exactly 16 bytes before the page boundary at `117432320`, one page short of the 112 MiB segment boundary. Gaps span a page only when the crossing record exceeds ~8 KiB, which goopg's FPI-per-record WAL makes routine. **Fix:** size the pad RECORD to the gap minus `pageHeaderBytesIn(gapStart, boundary, segSize)` and emit it through the same `emitWithPageHeaders` every other record uses (layout threaded in as `padLayout` via `newStripeWriterCore`). Guards `TestSegmentPadSpanningPageBoundaryReplays` (lead 8/16/64), `TestSegmentPadKeepsPageHeaderIntact`, `TestPageHeaderBytesIn` in `internal/wal/reader_segment_pad_page_gap_test.go`; the negative control reproduces the production signature byte for byte (`magic=0x0020 want 0xd118` at lsn=24561, 16 bytes before the fixture page boundary, 48 of 52 records read). `analysis/crashprobe30.sh` now snapshots `pg_wal`+`global` into `<run>/pg_wal_crash` BEFORE the restart, since recovery appends over the bytes that prove the early end-of-WAL. Residual (ledger 2026-08-12): `xl_prev` of a pad that itself starts on a page boundary is 24 bytes low.
    - [x] **S30.2 — an early end-of-WAL must not be a `WARN` that still starts the server.** goopg discarded committed work, logged it at WARN, and reported a successful start. A truncation that is not at the true stream end must be loud and must not silently open the cluster. **LANDED 2026-08-12 (loop #150); design `docs/design/0131-0029-early-end-of-wal-refuses-start.md`.** Both S30 row-loss defects (S30.1: 4416 rows, S30.1b: 6762 rows) had their truncation in the startup log all along and nothing consumed it. New rule: a walk stop is a real end of WAL only when nothing durable lies behind it. `durableWALAfter` (`internal/wal/reader_early_end.go`) scans forward from the first page boundary at or after the stop and accepts a page as evidence only when BOTH its header validates against the address it is stored at (`xlogPageValidator.check`, i.e. `xlp_pageaddr` — this is what keeps a recycled tail from reading as a hole) AND the first record position on it carries a CRC-valid record (`recordStartsAt`) — the same evidence `durableUnknownRecord` uses; false positive = a 2^-32 CRC collision on top of a self-consistent page header, false negative = the status quo silent loss. All six `endOfWAL` sites in `readAllPageAware` now route through a `stop` closure that records `ErrEarlyEndOfWAL`, plus a no-WARN `stopQuiet` on the all-zero page header (the ordinary preallocated tail — checked, because a zeroed page with durable pages BEHIND it is exactly the hole both writer defects left). `initdb.Open` wraps the error (`goopg: wal replay: %w`, open.go:382) so the server refuses to start; `GOOPG_WAL_ALLOW_EARLY_END=1` is the `pg_resetwal`-shaped escape hatch that downgrades it to a loud WARN. Upstream needs none of this because a record is durable only if every byte before it was flushed first (`ReserveXLogInsertLocation`/`XLogFlush`), so valid-behind-invalid is unreachable there. Guards `TestReadAllRefusesEarlyEndOfWAL` (incl. the env override), `TestReadAllAcceptsTrueEndOfWAL` (the ordinary crash tail must STILL be clean, else every crash bricks the cluster — it tears a page-internal record on purpose, since `xlp_rem_len` is not covered by the record CRC), `TestDurableWALAfterIgnoresZeroTail`. `TestReadAll_StopsAtStalePageAddr` updated: falsifying ONE page's `xlp_pageaddr` is a hole under the new rule, so the fixture now falsifies every page from the boundary on, which is what a real recycled segment looks like. Gates: `go test ./internal/wal/ ./internal/initdb/` PASS, units precommit, `RUNS=2 crashprobe30`. Residual (ledger 2026-08-12): no end-to-end `initdb.Open` refusal test.
    - [x] **S30.3 — replay and the runtime disagree about WHICH PHYSICAL PAGE a HOT update touched** (diagnosed 2026-08-11, `docs/design/0131-0021`; the earlier framing — "the non-HOT heap-update replay arm is wrong" — is superseded). The crashed cluster is PRESERVED and the failure is now deterministic and offline: copy `/tmp/s30_3_repro/data`, start goopg on the copy, and it refuses to start in ~35 s with `wal replay: replay record 826236 lsn[154662577,154662656]: xlog heap-update add new tuple: storage: not enough free space in page`. The record (goopg's decode and real `pg_waldump` agree byte-for-byte) is `HOT_UPDATE rel 1663/5/16407 (pgbench_tellers) blk 130, old_off 1, new_off 2`. On disk that page holds **185 line pointers, all LP_NORMAL, 28 bytes free**, `pd_lsn=123846600` = exactly the last record that touched it (idx 707555, a DELETE) — and **no WAL record of any kind references that page for the next ~120000 records**. `new_off: 2` is impossible on that page: `PageAddHeapTuple` (`internal/storage/heap.go:537`) always APPENDS at count+1 and never reuses a free slot, and `VacuumHeapPageBySlots` never shrinks the line-pointer array — so the runtime's page had exactly ONE line pointer, i.e. it was freshly `PageInit`-ed. This is a page-identity bug, not a free-space or missing-prune bug. **Ruled out, do not re-test:** missing prune records (they exist and decode — rmid 9 = Heap2 `PRUNE_ON_ACCESS`, 161 on this rel; note rmid 11 is **Btree**, not Heap2); "a prune would explain new_off=2" (it cannot, see the two primitives); unlogged `XLOG_SMGR_TRUNCATE` in the window (all 7 are at idx <= 504370); a goopg decode bug (pg_waldump agrees); replay corrupting the page (pre- and post-prefix-replay images match). **BOTH prime suspects are now REFUTED (2026-08-11, loop #134) — do not re-test them.** The probe was built: `internal/storage/pageident_probe.go` (`GOOPG_PAGEIDENT_PROBE=1`, driver `analysis/pageident_probe.sh`) reports `PAGEIDENT-REGRESS` when a heap page observed under a tag has FEWER line pointers than the high-water mark previously seen for that tag (= buffer tag/content aliasing) and `PAGEIDENT-REEXTEND` when extend hands out a block that already carried tuples (= double hand-out); it observes at every disk read, disk write, pre-eviction flush and inside `tryApplyHOTUpdate` under the content lock, filtered to heap pages via `pd_special == BlockSize` (without that filter btree splits produce 336 benign hits, `lp=205 high=407`). **ZERO hits** in a clean 45 s scale-5/16-client run AND in a full `crashprobe30` run that DID lose rows (497287/500000, 2713 missing). Suspect 2 is dead on inspection too: `relFile.readBlock` returns `ErrShortRead` for `blk >= nblocks` and `pinLoad` propagates it, so no path publishes a silently `PageInit`-ed page past EOF. **Next step:** (a) fire `PageIdentityObserve` inside `PageRemoveHeapTuple` and assert at `markHeapHotUpdateDirty` that the emitted `new_off` equals both `newSlot` and `PageLinePointerCount(page)` — an emit-time inconsistency must be caught before the crash hides it; (b) if that is clean, walk block 130's records from the START of the stream, since the divergence may begin far earlier than record 826236. **ROOT-CAUSED 2026-08-11 (loop #136) — two live buffers for one block.** Step (a) landed and FIRED: `PageIdentityAssertEmit` (emit-side `new_off` assert), `PageIdentityObserve` at every `MarkDirty*` with the SLOT's own tag + stack dump, and the decisive `Pool.probeAssertSlotIsMapped` (a slot about to mutate its page must BE the bufmap's slot for its own tag). `bash analysis/pageident_probe.sh` (60 s pgbench, NO crash, NO preserved cluster) reproduces the exact S30.3 record in 3 of 4 runs: `PAGEIDENT-DUPSLOT blk=149 slot=11706 mapped=11707` -> `preFlush lp=1 high=185` -> `postWrite` -> `EMIT-REGRESS new_off=2 lp=2 high=185`. Mechanism: `Pool.pinNewXID` (`internal/storage/bufpool.go`) releases `pinMu` across `mgr.Extend`; once Extend returns the block is inside `nblocks`, so another backend Pins it, misses the bufmap and loads it into a SECOND slot, while the extender publishes its own slot valid+dirty+pinned BEFORE calling `bmInsert` and, when `bmInsert` fails and `tryPinSlot` on the winner also fails, **falls through keeping its own publication** — an un-mapped dirty duplicate. Its stale near-empty page is a normal eviction candidate, so it overwrites the real 185-tuple block on disk and every later HOT update through it emits `new_off = 2, 3, 4 ...`. Upstream has no such window (`ReadBuffer_common` publishes under `BufMappingLock` + the extension lock, `postgres/src/backend/storage/buffer/bufmgr.c`). **Next step = the FIX slice:** insert the bufmap entry with `ioInflight` set BEFORE releasing `pinMu` for the extend write (mirror `pinLoad`'s publish-then-IO order) and delete the "fall through: keep our publication" branch — a loser must un-publish and retry. Gate: zero `PAGEIDENT-DUPSLOT`/`PAGEIDENT-EMIT-REGRESS` over several probe runs, then `RUNS=3 bash analysis/crashprobe30.sh`. See `docs/design/0131-0021` §Update 2026-08-11 (loop #136). **FIXED 2026-08-11 (loop #137).** The publish-before-Extend half of the filed plan is not available (the block number does not exist until `Extend` returns, so there is no tag to publish) and is not needed: step 1's publication is unreachable (`pinMu` held, slot not in the bufmap, `claimVictim` skips it as pinned). Only the fall-through made the duplicate durable, so it is gone — a losing extender now un-publishes (`s.tag = BufferTag{}` + `releaseVictimSlot`, matching `pinLoad`'s loser path) and re-acquires via `Pool.Pin`, which unlike `tryPinSlot` waits out the winner's `ioInflight` read instead of refusing it (that refusal WAS the trigger). Guard `TestPinNewLosingExtenderDoesNotKeepDuplicateSlot` (`internal/storage/bufpool_extendrace_test.go`) drives the interleaving deterministically via `OnExtendDone` + `OnPinWait`; it fails pre-fix (`slot 1 is a duplicate live copy … (mapped slot is 2)`) and passes post-fix, also under `-race`. Gate: `LOADSEC=60 CLIENTS=24 bash analysis/pageident_probe.sh` ×3 → ZERO `PAGEIDENT-*` events (fired in ~3 of 4 runs before). `RUNS=3 bash analysis/crashprobe30.sh` still FAILs — its remaining row-loss/atomicity failures are the S30.1/S30.2 WAL-tail-discard signature (`docs/design/0131-0020`), untouched by this slice. The `GOOPG_PAGEIDENT_PROBE` instrumentation stays until S30 as a whole closes (ledger 2026-08-11).
    - [x] **S30.5 — the HOT orphan-cleanup arm mutates a heap page with NO WAL record** (found 2026-08-11 by the S30.3 probe; own defect, not the S30.3 root cause). When the old-slot stamp fails, `tryApplyHOTUpdate` calls `storage.PageRemoveHeapTuple(s.Page(), newSlot)` (`internal/executor/operators_storage.go:3746`) and emits nothing — while the `PagePruneOpt` that usually CAUSED the failure was logged via `markHeapPruneOptDirty`. `PageRemoveHeapTuple` does not only blank the line pointer: when the removed slot is the last one it SHRINKS `pd_lower` (`internal/storage/heap.go:697-699`), reducing the page's line-pointer count. The slot is already dirty from the logged prune, so the mutation reaches disk and replay rebuilds a LARGER page than the runtime had — a runtime/WAL page divergence of exactly the S30.3 family (one line pointer per occurrence, so it does not by itself explain 185 -> 1). Upstream has no unlogged heap-page mutation of this kind: `PageRepairFragmentation` truncates trailing unused line pointers only inside a WAL-logged vacuum/prune under a cleanup lock (`postgres/src/backend/storage/page/bufpage.c`). Fix candidates: reuse `markHeapPruneOptDirty` with a one-slot `Unused` result (check that its replay arm reproduces the `pd_lower` shrink), or stop shrinking `pd_lower` and let the next logged prune reclaim the slot. Gate: a unit test asserting replay of the emitted stream reproduces the runtime page byte-for-byte. **RESOLVED 2026-08-11 (loop #135).** Neither filed candidate was taken: both miss that the ADD is unlogged too, so the add/remove pair needs no WAL record — it needs to be a no-op on the page, and it was not. `PageAddHeapTuple` moves `pd_lower` (+4) AND `pd_upper` (-MAXALIGN(len)); `PageRemoveHeapTuple` restored only `pd_lower`, leaking one tuple's free space per occurrence out of a page the WAL says still has it. `PageRemoveHeapTuple` (`internal/storage/heap.go`) now also raises `pd_upper` back over the item body when the removed slot is the last line pointer and its body sits exactly at `pd_upper` — the state an append leaves, making the call an exact inverse; interior removals are unchanged. The vacated bytes fall back inside `[pd_lower, pd_upper)`, PG's FPI hole, so they are outside the page image either way. Guards `TestPageRemoveHeapTupleUndoesAppend` (fails pre-fix: `pd_upper=8056 want=8096`) / `TestPageRemoveHeapTupleInteriorSlotKeepsUpper` in `internal/storage/heap_test.go`. One same-family divergence in this arm stays open (ledger 2026-08-11): a successful old-slot multixact stamp followed by a failing `PageSetHeapTupleCmax` leaves the old tuple's xmax mutated with no WAL. Design `docs/design/0131-0021` §Update 2026-08-11 (loop #135).
    - [x] **S30.4 — no checkpoint fired during ~180 MiB of WAL** (aggravating factor, decide explicitly): `checkpoint` appears ZERO times in the pre-crash log, so every run replays from `redo=16777256`. This is both why the volume reaches the segment boundary and why a truncated replay costs so many rows. **DECIDED 2026-08-12 (loop #152)** — `docs/design/0131-0031`. Verdict: **the probe is correct and the trigger works.** `analysis/crashprobe.sh` writes no `postgresql.conf` overrides, so it runs at the PG defaults (`checkpoint_timeout` 300 s, `max_wal_size` 1024 MB, `checkpoint_completion_target` 0.9) and is killed 30 s in — a real PG under the same workload also produces zero checkpoints, and ~180 MiB is below any volume threshold either engine uses. Zero checkpoints is an artifact of the probe's DURATION, exactly the aggravating factor this item suspected, not a broken checkpointer. Establishing that exposed three real `max_wal_size` deviations, all fixed here: (1) goopg used `max_wal_size` AS the trigger distance where upstream's `CalculateCheckpointSegments` (xlog.c:2170-2198) divides it by `1 + checkpoint_completion_target` and `XLogCheckpointNeeded` fires `CheckPointSegments - 1` **segments** past redo — at the shared defaults PG checkpoints every 512 MiB, goopg waited 1024 MiB; (2) the anchor was the checkpoint RECORD's end LSN rather than `RedoRecPtr`, shortening every window after the first by the flush phase plus the `XLOG_RUNNING_XACTS` record (`Run` now seeds a start-position anchor for the pre-first-checkpoint window, so a restart above `max_wal_size` no longer checkpoints on the first poll); (3) found by measurement — at `CheckPointSegments == 1` the literal `elapsed >= 0` test plus goopg's 1 s poll (upstream evaluates only at a segment switch) gave **42 checkpoints in 40 s** of pgbench, so the polled distance now floors at one segment (re-measured: 14 checkpoints over ~223 MiB, one per segment). Guards `TestCheckpointerCheckPointSegments` (pins the PG-default 33-segment/512 MiB row), rewritten `TestCheckpointerVolumeTriggerThreshold`, `TestCheckpointerVolumeTriggerDisabled`. DEFERRED (ledger row): the volume condition is still polled rather than signalled from the writer's segment switch, and neither `max_wal_size` nor `checkpoint_completion_target` recomputes the distance on a GUC reload.
- [x] **M0131-S28 — Reverse crash E2E** (DONE 2026-08-12) (est ~3 loops). The mirror: real `initdb` → PG → workload exercising **COPY, VACUUM, `SELECT … FOR UPDATE`, `TRUNCATE`, a SAVEPOINT, and an index-heavy insert** (precisely the S16/S17 opcode set) → SIGKILL the postmaster (**`pgcluster.Kill()` is NOT a SIGKILL** — `internal/testutil/pgcluster/cluster.go:325-336` runs `pg_ctl -m immediate stop`, i.e. SIGQUIT, and the postmaster still reaches `on_proc_exit(UnlinkLockFiles)` at `miscinit.c:1495` and removes `postmaster.pid`; **S28.0 must add a true-SIGKILL helper with `Setpgid` to `pgcluster` first** — **S28.0 DONE 2026-08-12**: `pgcluster.Start` sets `Setpgid`, new `pgcluster.KillHard()` group-SIGKILLs postmaster+backends, guard `internal/testutil/pgcluster/kill_hard_test.go` asserts `postmaster.pid` survives it and is removed by `Kill()`; ledger row records goopg's missing stale-lock-file start refusal) → start **goopg** on the directory → compare counts and aggregates against answers captured before the kill. Second variant: create a GIN index first and assert goopg refuses with S20's specific message and a non-zero exit — a specific refusal, never a silent skip. **S28 MAIN VARIANT DONE 2026-08-12** — `internal/testport/e2e_goopg_crashstart_on_pgdata_test.go`: the PG workload, the twelve captured answers, the `postmaster.pid`-survives / `pg_control.State != DB_SHUTDOWNED` crash assertions and guard 7 (every design-table opcode confirmed in a `pg_waldump` of the crash tail) all run green; guard 7 immediately earned its keep by correcting two rows of the opcode table (a `TRUNCATE` of a pre-existing table emits NO truncate record — `ExecuteTruncateGuts` only truncates in place when `rd_createSubid == mySubid`; and `XLOG_HEAP_TRUNCATE` is `wal_level=logical`-only and a redo no-op upstream). The goopg half is a **self-arming skip**: goopg refuses the tail at `rmid=0 info=0x30` (`XLOG_NEXTOID`) because **S21 is unchecked**, so `unsupportedXLogOpcode` recognises that one refusal and skips — every other startup failure stays fatal, and the assertions arm themselves the moment S21/S22/S23 land. The `_Concurrent` variant ships `t.Skip`ped as the S24 re-arm trigger (guard 9). **S28 GIN-REFUSAL VARIANT DONE 2026-08-12** — `internal/testport/e2e_goopg_crashstart_gin_refusal_test.go` (`TestE2E_GoopgCrashStartOnPGDataDirGINRefusal`, 1.7 s, green and proven to bite): a GIN index + 2000 INSERTs + `gin_clean_pending_list()` puts **2011 RM_GIN records** in the crash tail (`CREATE INDEX … USING gin` alone puts NONE — it logs the built index as rmid-0 `XLOG_FPI` via `log_newpage_range`), goopg exits **1** with S25's pre-flight message (`gin (rmid=13, 2011 record(s) …); nothing has been replayed`), and a real PG then **finishes recovery on the same directory** and re-reads both the heap and the GIN index — the operator-visible payoff of pre-flighting. No self-arming skip and it must never grow one: `preflightIndexAMRecords` runs before the first record is applied, so the GIN refusal pre-empts every other opcode gap. Exit status needed a foreground `go run ./cmd/goopg start` (`cluster.Start` reports only "process exited early"); the recovery leg needed `pgcluster.OpenExisting` (`New` runs initdb). **S28 COMPLETE 2026-08-12** — the last assertion ("uncommitted rows must not survive") landed via new `pgcluster.Session` (`internal/testutil/pgcluster/session.go`): a pinned `sql.Conn` (NOT `sql.DB` — a pool would run the INSERT and the COMMIT on different backends) holds `BEGIN; INSERT 100 rows` open across `KillHard`. Non-vacuity is asserted three ways, because "recovery discarded them" and "they were never written" look identical afterwards: the rows are visible inside their own transaction, a second session sees the backend `idle in transaction` (matched by `application_name`), and a committed marker row inserted AFTER them flushes the single WAL stream up to its own LSN (SIGKILL loses WAL buffers, not the log) — the marker is the positive half of the pair. New guard 13 `assertUncommittedXIDInTail` closes it in the bytes: `pg_waldump` must show >=1 record for that XID and NO COMMIT for it; all three failure branches proven to bite. **Discovery: the self-arming skip no longer fires — goopg now replays the PG-authored crash tail end to end** (the `XLOG_NEXTOID` refusal is gone), so all fourteen captured answers are compared for real against goopg (proven by corrupting one expected answer and watching goopg's own value come back). The skip stays as a tripwire; `_Concurrent` stays skipped as the S24 re-arm trigger. `dumpCrashTail` now runs once per test and feeds both guards. Design: `docs/design/0131-0017-crash-interchange-e2e.md`.
- [x] **M0131-S29 — BASE_BACKUP stops mutating the source `pg_control`** (DONE 2026-08-12). `internal/control/pgcontrol.go` gains `BuildUpdatedControlImage`, which is `UpdateControlFile`'s read-decode-mutate-encode-CRC half WITHOUT the write (`UpdateControlFile` is now a two-liner over it, so the shipped image and the on-disk write cannot drift). `initdb.UpdateControlCheckpoint` is RENAMED to `initdb.BackupControlImage` — renamed, not duplicated, so no caller can reach the mutating variant again — and `emitBaseBackupTar` takes a trailing `pgControlImage []byte` shipped at its pg_control-last step (nil = ship the file verbatim, which is what happens with no checkpoint REDO yet; the manifest records the shipped bytes because `record()` is fed the same buffer). Gate `TestBaseBackupDoesNotMutateLiveControlFile` (`internal/server/basebackup_control_test.go`) asserts BOTH halves — live file byte-identical across the backup AND the shipped image still patched/CRC-valid with `minRecoveryPoint != 0` — since either half alone passes by accident; proven fail-when-broken in both directions (re-add an `os.WriteFile` of the image → half 1 fails; no checkpoint REDO → half 2 fails). `TestE2E_PGStandbyFullCycle` PASS, so a real PG still restores the tar. **Discovery:** `LoadOrCreateTimelineID` resolves the timeline from **pg_control**, not from the `timeline_id` file the BASE_BACKUP fixture also writes, so a stub control file reports TLI `0xC0C0C0C0` in the shipped image and the START_TIMELINE reply alike. 1 ledger row (goopg still patches the image at all, where upstream ships the control file verbatim and lets `backup_label` drive the standby). Design: `0131-0014` §S29.

## M0132 — Explicit transactions across the extended query protocol (filed 2026-08-12, user directive)

**Priority: FILED, NOT PROMOTED.** The `## Current Priority` banner at the top of this file remains the sole ordering authority and still names M0131 first; M0132 is queued behind it. Working M0132 requires a banner edit — the filing directive was to raise the milestone, not to reorder the queue.

Milestone: `docs/milestones/0132-extended-protocol-explicit-transactions.md`. Authoritative decomposition: `docs/design/0132-extended-protocol-explicit-transactions.md`. Core design: `docs/design/0132-0001-extended-protocol-explicit-txn-state-machine.md`. Supersedes (on landing) the `design`-status sketch `analysis/perf-optimize3-dash/08-improvement-designs/09-extended-protocol-explicit-txn.md` (2026-07-14) and discharges `.ralph/deferral_ledger.md:347`, the 2026-07-14 row deferring it because "a partial land would silently skip INITIALLY DEFERRED constraints".

**The bug, verified at HEAD `28de7c44` (2026-08-12).** goopg's extended query protocol (Parse/Bind/Execute) begins and commits its own transaction on **every** `Execute`, and treats the client's `BEGIN`/`COMMIT`/`ROLLBACK` as accepted-but-ignored command tags — `dispatch_extended.go:110-112` returns a bare `CommandTag` for a `*planner.Transaction` node, `:134-139` unconditionally begins a fresh READ COMMITTED transaction on an offset proc slot, `:361-365` unconditionally commits it. `connTx *connTxState` is already threaded into `executeExtendedQueryViaExecutor` (`:30`) but is read only for `NonSuperuserRole` (`:36`, `:46`, `:238`, `:250`) and statement logging (`:68`, `:71`); `connTx.Begin`/`InExplicit`/`Tx`/`End` have **zero** call sites in `dispatch_extended.go` or `extended.go`. The simple path models the block (`dispatch.go:227-229` reuses `connTx.Tx()`; `:2696-2984` is the 289-line verb arm, promotion at `:2701`).

**Why it is a correctness milestone, not a perf one.** `ROLLBACK` over the extended protocol **does not roll back** — a `BEGIN` → `UPDATE` → `UPDATE` → `ROLLBACK` sequence has already durably committed both updates when the `ROLLBACK` arrives, which returns a success tag. Atomicity across statements is lost (a failure at statement *k* leaves `1..k-1` committed); `ReadyForQuery` reports `I` where PG reports `T` (the byte comes from `connTxState.wireStatus`, `conn_tx.go:415`, installed once per connection at `server.go:1526` as `w.TxStatusFn` — an *observable protocol* divergence drivers gate on); and isolation levels above READ COMMITTED are unreachable (`:139` hardcodes RC; the comment at `:119-123` says SERIALIZABLE "cannot apply").

**The shape real drivers emit is MIXED, and worse.** pgx v5 (`conn.go:515`, "Always use simple protocol when there are no arguments") and lib/pq (`conn.go:901`) send argument-less statements — including `BEGIN`/`COMMIT`/`ROLLBACK` — down the **simple** path while parameterised DML goes down the extended one. So the dominant real-world shape is a block opened on the simple path with its DML executed on the extended path, producing **two live transactions on one connection**: the block held on the connection's own slot (`dispatch.go:227-229`) while each `Execute` begins and commits its own on the offset slot. The client's writes land in the auto-committing one and the `ROLLBACK` discards the empty one. Consequences: S8 (mixed) is a **primary** slice, not an edge case; acceptance bar 2b covers the driver shape; and this is a better hypothesis for S7's `mvcc: unknown transaction` aborts than "the offset scheme collides" alone.

**CORRECTION to doc 09 that reshapes S4 — there is no `execCommit` route to adopt.** Doc 09 §2/§4.1 (and the first draft of this filing) said the extended COMMIT should route through `transactionOp.execCommit`. The simple path does not use it: `dispatch.go:2803-2807` states "the simple-query dispatch bypasses transactionOp.execCommit, so the checks queued on the session during INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE must be run here BEFORE TxnMgr.Commit". The deferred FK/UNIQUE/EXCLUDE passes are **inline** at `:2818-2828` (`RunDeferredFKChecks` under `FreshSnapshot`, then `RunDeferredUniqueChecks`, then `RunDeferredExclusionChecks`), the SSI pre-commit check inline after, and the arm returns at `:2980` without reaching the operator build at `:2985`. The bypass was never removed — 0119-0004 *added* the inline sequence. The extended COMMIT must inherit that sequence, which it gets **for free iff S2 is a genuine extraction**; S4 is therefore the proof obligation on S2, not independent wiring.

**LAND-TOGETHER RULE (non-negotiable): S2+S3+S4+S5 in ONE commit.** Two independent arguments, both "the half-landing opens a hole that does not exist today". (a) Without S4, a block whose COMMIT reaches `TxnMgr.Commit` by any route other than the extracted helper skips the deferred sequence and commits a block violating an `INITIALLY DEFERRED` constraint — today each statement commits individually so its constraints are checked individually. (b) Without S5, an error mid-block leaves the block open and un-failed — there is **no** `connTx.Fail()` call site on the extended path (its only two are `dispatch.go:950` and `:1019`) — so post-error `Execute`s keep running in the same live transaction and `COMMIT` commits them; PG aborts everything.

**Adjacent divergences recorded but NOT closed here (ledger rows required):** (i) **COPY ignores an open block on BOTH protocols** — `copy.go:157-167`: "The COPY always runs in its own auto-commit transaction regardless of whether the client has opened an explicit BEGIN block", using the same `(ProcNum + halfSize) % ConnSlotCount` offset that S7 exists to retire (`dispatch_extended.go:136` cites copy.go as its model), so `BEGIN; COPY …; ROLLBACK` does not roll back today, simple path included; (ii) **`SET LOCAL ROLE` semantics change silently under S2** — `SnapshotLocalRoleIfNeeded` (`conn_tx.go:390-396`) returns early when `!active`, so the extended path's calls (`dispatch_extended.go:252`, `extended.go:693`, `:712`) record no restore point and `End()` never reverts; setting `active` changes that with no slice claiming it.

Confirm and record the three doc-09 corrections: (a) its S1 "thread `connTxState`" is **already done** (`dispatch_extended.go:30`), so the first *code* slice is the state machine; (b) there is **no `execCommit` route** (see the correction paragraph above); (c) `Sync` (`server.go:1761-1765`) is **already correct** — it clears `syncRequired` and writes `ReadyForQuery` without touching transaction state, exactly PG's behaviour for an explicit block, so the tempting "end the transaction at Sync" edit would silently re-break every block. Add the tests that must FAIL against today's HEAD and become the acceptance bar: block-spans-Executes, rollback-actually-rolls-back, **the mixed driver shape (bar 2b)**, status-byte, aborted-block. A milestone whose first slice cannot produce a red test has not reproduced the bug. Gates: `go test ./internal/server/`.
- [ ] **M0132-S2 — `BEGIN`/`COMMIT`/`ROLLBACK` drive the real block state machine** (est ~2 loops; design `0132-0001` §3 D1). Replace the short-circuit at `dispatch_extended.go:110-112` by **EXTRACTING** the simple path's verb arm (`dispatch.go:2696-2984`, 289 lines) into one `applyTransactionVerb(ctx, connTx, txNode, autoCommitPtr) (tag, err)` helper both dispatchers call. Extraction — not mirroring — is what makes S4 free: the arm covers the COMMIT-in-failed-block→ROLLBACK rule (`:2782-2800`), the inline deferred sequence (`:2818-2828`), the SSI pre-commit check, enum-DDL undo, the `Begin/EndLocalTransaction` hooks and the pending enum/composite/range-type queues, and a hand-written extended COMMIT would have to re-derive a sequence its author may not know exists. Two asymmetries the helper absorbs: the simple path *promotes* an already-begun transaction (`:2701`) while the extended path reaches the verb before beginning anything, so the transaction is an argument; and the simple arm writes its own wire frames, so the helper returns the tag and each caller writes it in its own protocol's shape. The refactor must be proven behaviour-preserving for the simple path BEFORE the new extended behaviour is switched on. Free consequence: `ReadyForQuery` starts reporting `T`/`I` (shared `w.TxStatusFn`) — but NOT `E`, which needs S5. Gates: `go test ./internal/server/ ./internal/executor/`, D-002 isolation. **Lands with S3+S4+S5.**

Use the `connTx.Tx()` **accessor**, not a cached handle — it returns the session's current transaction once an XID has been materialised (`conn_tx.go:442-451`, M0100-0002) so a statement self-sees the block's earlier writes. This is also the fix for the mixed-protocol two-transactions-per-connection case. Gates: `scripts/tpch-spotcheck.sh` (canonical Q12=2/Q13=35) + `make plan-gate` — **note the rationale: the spotcheck's `cmd/tpch-runner` uses `lib/pq` with zero-argument `QueryContext` calls (`main.go:237`, `:392`), so it exercises the SIMPLE protocol.** It is the right gate because S2 refactors the simple path, NOT because it covers the extended one; extended coverage comes from S1's tests and S11's pgbench `-M prepared`. **Lands with S2+S4+S5.**

If S2's extraction is faithful this is a test, not a change; if it is not faithful, S2 is wrong and this slice is where that surfaces.
